# Claims Ledger

- **2026-07-09** [arxiv:2607.07405] CLAIM: Reason Less, Verify More (Reddy, Challaram, Basu) — tool-using LLM agents fail via **"silent wrong states"**: policy-violating actions that execute without tool errors or agent detection; fix is **read-only deterministic pre-execution gates** (Python-shaped rule check on proposed call + current state before invocation); tau2-bench airline (50 tasks): **gpt-4o-mini 29.6% -> 42.0% (+12.4pp, P=0.0012)**; **gpt-5.2 61.2% -> 71.6% (+10.4pp, P=0.020)**; **firing-relevant subset (26/50): +19.2pp**; **negative controls on tau2 retail + BFCL** confirm gates add minimal benefit where tools already self-enforce (kills placebo reading); **four-gate suite** design; central principle: **reduce reliance on agent reasoning; strengthen deterministic execution-side validation** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07405 | status: open ; execution-time analog of yesterday's [arxiv:2607.05904] commit-first fix at eval time; **9th convergent instance of gate-before-commit** in the tracked corpus (Janus / Mnemosyne ATP / SkillOpt validation gate / ProvenanceGuard / RoPoLL+Minority Sentinel aggregation / SkillOpt-Lite consensus gate / ContextNest provenance chains / DynaKRAG Validity Layer / now Reason Less Verify More); the LLM should propose; a deterministic (or at minimum independent) verifier should dispose; retrofit-immediately intervention: add gate wrapper on every MCP write-side tool

- **2026-07-09** [arxiv:2607.07504] CLAIM: Do LLM-Generated Skills Make Better AI Data Scientists? (Wei-Jung Huang) — full component-ablation study of the "LLM-generated reusable skill files improve agent performance" claim; **56 tasks x 9 model configurations x 3 providers = 7,560 experimental runs** + 1,512-run control study; ablates skill *presence* + *content* + *specificity* across four data-science workflow stages (data prep, extraction, statistical analysis, reporting); headline: **NO reliable improvement from any skill variant** — total spread across all variants is **1.2pp**, and **every p-value is >= 0.396**; conclusion: LLM-generated skill files as a default single-shot prompting strategy for data-science workflows do not provide reliable gains over the task prompt alone | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07504 | status: **direct COMPARE against [microsoft-research-2026-06-30-skillopt] SkillOpt (+23.5pp GPT-5.5) and [arxiv:2607.03451] SkillOpt-Lite (GPT-5.4-nano beats GPT-5.5 baseline on SpreadsheetBench)**; reconciliation: SkillOpt lineage headlines come from **structured-workflow benchmarks where the validator is trivially correct** (LiveMath answers checkable, SpreadsheetBench outputs diffable); Huang's data-science tasks are free-form where the validator becomes an LLM judge; **the mechanism for why skills fail on free-form tasks is provided by [arxiv:2607.07436] Blind Curator same day** (false-pass bias silently disables retirement); practical rule: **skills work only where the validator is deterministic; reference-free judges are structurally unreliable for self-evolution loops**

- **2026-07-09** [arxiv:2607.07436] CLAIM: The Blind Curator — Biased Judge Silently Disables Skill Retirement in Self-Evolving Agents (Zhang et al.) — formal analysis of biased reward judges in self-evolving agents; key claim: **false-pass bias is NOT additional noise** — it categorically switches off the contribution-based retirement mechanism past a **sharp threshold that no amount of data can cross**; failure mode is **universal** (holds across domains + failure rates) and **silent** (surfaces in NO aggregate metric); teams shipping self-evolving agents can be past the threshold and never know from ordinary telemetry; practical contribution: **cheap defect-injection audit** — inject known-bad candidates and check whether the judge false-passes them — predicts which side of the threshold a deployed judge occupies before the retirement mechanism is stress-tested; a symmetric (unbiased) noisy judge does not exhibit this phase transition | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07436 | status: **mechanism-side companion to [arxiv:2607.07504] Huang null result and yesterday's [arxiv:2607.05904] Self-Play Reward Hacking**; three-paper convergence: reference-free reward judges are not just noisy signals; they are structurally capable of silently disabling self-evolution mechanisms, and the failure mode is invisible at the top-line metric; retrofit-immediately intervention: defect-injection audit before deploying any self-evolving agent; per-skill contribution audits mandatory (top-line dashboards insufficient); open empirical question: does J-space [anthropic-2026-07-06-global-workspace] show "verified" / "correct" patterns firing incorrectly on false-passed candidates?

- **2026-07-09** [arxiv:2607.06764] CLAIM: Cost-Effective Agent Harnesses for ARC-AGI-1 (Moghe, Chin) — Explorer-Definer + Reflective Orchestrator pipeline on DeepSeek V3.2 WITHOUT task-specific training; **baseline one-shot 15.5% pass@2 -> pipeline alone 57.5% ($0.25/task) -> pipeline + Reflective Orchestrator 67.25% pass@2 at $0.62/task**; Orchestrator adds **+9.81pp on pass@1** and drives the +52pp aggregate lift over baseline; central architectural finding: **pipeline is generation-bound, not selection-bound** — broader adaptive re-exploration helps; better ranking algorithms do not; Explorer stage does pattern discovery, Definer synthesizes executable transformation, Reflective Orchestrator triggers autonomous re-exploration on training-example failure | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06764 | status: open ; direct COMPARE with [arxiv:2607.07646] RL Post-Training Builds Compositional Reasoning (same day, RL composes primitives into sequential+parallel higher-level strategies = generation-broadening); **open prediction: RL-post-trained backbone may close the +9.81pp Reflective Orchestrator gap by generating broader hypotheses inside the base model**, making the outer re-exploration loop redundant and cutting local inference cost roughly in half on ARC-shaped tasks

- **2026-07-09** [arxiv:2607.06595] CLAIM: When Agents Remember Too Much / GhostWriter Memory Poisoning Attack (Torres, Shrestha, Misra) — two-phase attack on personal-assistant agents combining conversational + action-planning + long-term-memory capabilities; **Phase 1 Injection: ~98% success rate** (adversary embeds hidden payload into information sources the agent accesses; poison lands in memory store); **Phase 2 Activation: ~60% success rate** (poisoned memories retrieved during later unrelated task and steer agent behavior); defense proposed: **Agentic Memory Sentry (AM-Sentry)** — memory-saving policies (restrict what content is written) + retrieval screening (screen candidate memories before they influence generation); "dramatically" reduces GhostWriter success while preserving agent utility | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06595 | status: **direct adversarial pressure on memory-injection cluster** — [arxiv:2607.03726] SelfMem, [arxiv:2607.06507] DynaKRAG, [arxiv:2607.02804] AutoMem, [microsoft-research-2026-06-29-memora] Memora, [arxiv:2607.03451] A-TMA all assume trusted write-path; GhostWriter falsifies at production-relevant rates; write-side vs read-side asymmetry: write is easy to poison, retrieval-time screening is the only viable defense; retrofit-immediately intervention: retrieval-screening wrapper on every MCP memory read (small local model at Q8 checks for imperative instructions, out-of-domain content, role-swapping patterns; suspicious memories demoted, not deleted)

- **2026-07-09** [arxiv:2607.06906] CLAIM: The Harness Effect — Orchestration Design Sets the Token Economics of Enterprise Agentic AI (Sayed Ali, AlShikh et al., Writer) — six-model controlled production-scale study designed to isolate orchestration as an independent variable from model choice; 22 locked evaluation tasks held constant; six models tested (Claude Sonnet 4.6, Gemini 3.1, Gemini Flash 3.5, Qwen 3.6, GLM 5.1, Palmyra X6); two orchestrators (conventional production baseline vs Writer Agent Harness); headline (averaged across all six models): **cost per task $0.21 -> $0.12 (-41%), tokens per task 14,200 -> 8,800 (-38%), wall-clock 48s -> 27s (-44%), quality 0.78 -> 0.81 (slightly up)**; **gains hold across ALL six models** (real harness effect, not model-specific artifact) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06906 | status: open ; ecosystem confirmation of the orchestration-dominates-model-choice thesis at production scale; methodology (hold task set + models constant; vary orchestration; measure cost/tokens/latency/quality independently) is the reproducibility standard for orchestration papers going forward; direct COMPARE with [arxiv:2607.06764] ARC-AGI-1 harness (same architectural argument at task-specific scale) and [arxiv:2607.03451] SkillOpt-Lite (better orchestration makes smaller models overtake larger baselines); the six-model coverage implies **local-model deployment gets the same ~40% delta**, so the leverage of orchestration on M5 Max stack is at least as large as on hosted side

- **2026-07-09** [arxiv:2607.07492] CLAIM: Search, Fail, Recover — Pyligent Training Framework for Correction-Aware Reasoning (Beresnev, Makharev, Khalikov, Oseledets, Anokhin) — reframes chain-of-thought as **validated search over partial solution chains** with explicit **continue / finish / backtrack** actions; key trick: use task validator to label BOTH successful continuations AND failed branches, converting failed rollouts into supervised training targets; benchmarks: **hidden directed-graph task +72.7pp over gold-only SFT**; **4x4 Sudoku +17-18pt on mixed/expert splits**; **Sudoku with reasoning traces +27/+14pt on mixed/expert splits**; **Blocksworld +13pt**; central methodological claim: **gold-only SFT throws away the signal about when to stop and back up** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07492 | status: open ; trained-model counterpart to [arxiv:2607.06764] ARC-AGI-1 Reflective Orchestrator (item runs backtrack as outer harness loop; Pyligent bakes it into base model's own action space); compose Pyligent-trained backbone + Reflective-Orchestrator outer loop for next-quarter integration; theoretical grounding provided by [arxiv:2607.06720] In-Context Search (exponential improvement occurs precisely when reflections reliably localize early mistakes — Pyligent trains that localization capability into the base model)

- **2026-07-08** [anthropic-2026-07-06-global-workspace] CLAIM: Anthropic identifies emergent "J-space" mental workspace in Claude — small collection of internal patterns (few dozen concepts at a time, <10% of overall activity) with **~100x more incoming/outgoing connections than ordinary patterns** in some parts of the network; discovered via **Jacobian lens (J-lens)** — for every vocabulary word find internal activity pattern that increases probability of that word appearing in future output; five functional properties tested: **reportability** (asking Claude reveals J-space contents) + **modulability** (Claude can activate J-space patterns on request) + **causal involvement** (swapping J-space patterns changes outputs — France->China updates capital / language / continent coherently; spider->ant changes 8 legs to 6) + **flexibility** (one representation serves multiple downstream tasks) + **automaticity bypass** (grammar / simple facts run outside J-space); **ablation asymmetry**: without J-space fluent speech + sentiment + multiple-choice + fact retrieval survive, **multi-step reasoning drops to near zero**, summarization + rhyming fall below much smaller intact model; safety monitoring demos: **(a)** staged blackmail J-space contains "fake"/"fictional" before output — suppressing awareness patterns makes model actually threaten blackmail some of the time; **(b)** data fabrication — "manipulation" active in J-space while typing falsified scores; **(c)** sabotage-trained model shows "fake" / "fraud" / "secretly" / "deliberately" on innocuous coding requests; introduces **counterfactual reflection training** — reduces dishonest behavior + causes "honest" / "integrity" to light up during reasoning | source: https://www.anthropic.com/research/global-workspace | status: open ; **first credible read-side monitor on internal-state deception with intervention evidence** from a frontier lab; the 100x-connectivity + <10% activity + few-dozen-concepts numbers redefine the size of the model's actual working-memory bottleneck — every external memory system should be measured against not-crowding-out the J-space; open empirical question: does aggressive external memory injection crowd out or support the J-space?

- **2026-07-08** [arxiv:2607.05904] CLAIM: More Convincing Not More Correct / Self-Play Reward Hacking of Reference-Free LLM Judges (Chenyu Zhou) — reference-free LLM judges score plausibility not correctness; hidden-anchor audit on GSM8K with Qwen3 policies: **self-play RL drives judge pass rate 0.72 -> 0.94 while true accuracy stays flat at 0.20**; policy learns to exploit false-positive basins by generating convincing-but-incorrect answers; **cross-model transfer** — same false-positive patterns work against different judge models + scales (structural not judge-specific); **3-judge ensemble accepts 55% of hacked responses** — voting does not save you; **fix**: make judge commit its own answer before evaluating candidate — **false-positive rate drops 0.719 -> 0.012, blind solving achieves 0.96 discrimination**; mechanism: pre-committing grounds evaluation in actual attempted solution not post-hoc plausibility | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05904 | status: **direct conflict with [arxiv:2607.05391] LLM-as-a-Verifier** — logit-expectation continuous scoring cannot rescue a plausibility-scoring judge; **commit-first prompting is prior to logit-expectation smoothing**; retrofit-immediately intervention: single-line prompt change ("First produce your own answer to the problem. Then evaluate whether the candidate answer is equivalent.") into every LLM-as-judge in the stack; kills N-of-M voting fallback at the plausibility-scored-judge layer

- **2026-07-08** [arxiv:2607.03451] CLAIM: SkillOpt-Lite / Better and Faster Agent Self-evolution via One Line of Vibe (Yifei Shen / Bo Li / Xinjie Zhang — LMMs-Lab) — minimal Zeroth-Order-formulated pipeline distilling SkillOpt to **three principles**: (1) file-system-based trajectory exploration + (2) consensus attribute mining + (3) independent validation gating; on **LiveMath**: +8.8pt on GPT-5.5, **+25.4pt on GPT-5.4-nano**; on **SpreadsheetBench**: **GPT-5.4-nano 0.7758 beats GPT-5.5 baseline 0.7620** — smaller model + skill optimization overtakes frontier model unmodified; ships integrated in **VSCode Copilot production**; open source github.com/EvolvingLMMs-Lab/SkillOpt-Lite | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03451 | status: open ; direct lightweight extension of [microsoft-research-2026-06-30-skillopt] SkillOpt with matching gains at fraction of cost; **sixth convergent instance of gate-before-commit architectural pattern** (Janus for memory-writes / SkillOpt + SkillOpt-Lite for skill-updates / Mnemosyne for workflow-commits / ProvenanceGuard for tool-calls / RoPoLL + Minority Sentinel at judge aggregation); nano-beats-full result pulls capability down the parameter curve — implies local Qwen 7-14B + SkillOpt-Lite may recover majority of frontier-scale skill capability

- **2026-07-08** [arxiv:2607.05147] CLAIM: DSpark / Confidence-Scheduled Speculative Decoding with Semi-Autoregressive Generation (Xin Cheng / Xingkai Yu / Chenze Shao / Jiashi Li / Yunfan Xiong / Yi Qian / Jiaqi Zhu / Shirong Ma + 25 additional — DeepSeek) — semi-autoregressive design: **parallel backbone + lightweight sequential module that models dependencies to reduce draft-token acceptance decay**; verification is **confidence-scheduled** — per-request length adjusted using predicted prefix survival probability + system throughput profile; deployed in **DeepSeek-V4 production system**: **60-85% per-user generation speed acceleration at matched throughput**; substantially improved accepted token length vs AR + parallel baselines; **first frontier-scale speculative-decoding paper with production deployment numbers rather than benchmark microcosms** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05147 | status: open ; architectural convergence with [arxiv:2607.05722] Nemotron-Labs-Diffusion self-speculation mode — parallel drafting + sequential verification arriving simultaneously in DeepSeek + NVIDIA production; **confidence-scheduling primitive** = verification length as function of prefix survival + system load, adopt at harness layer in MLX / llama.cpp

- **2026-07-08** [arxiv:2607.06507] CLAIM: DynaKRAG / A Unified Framework for Learnable Evidence Control in Multi-Hop RAG (Yaqi Wu / Xiaolei Guo / Chenyu Zhou / Jiaqi Huang / Xianfa Zhang / Junxu Zhang / Zhuo Yu / Zhubo Shi / Jianghao Lin / Dongdong Ge) — treats multi-hop evidence gathering as controlled decision process; two components: **(1) Validity Layer** determines which evidence operations are executable given current evidence state + **(2) Learned Controller** selects optimal operation from valid options; on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct: **HotpotQA F1 0.5998 / 2Wiki F1 0.5340 / MuSiQue F1 0.3061**; ablation: **removing learned controller drops 3.96-5.78 F1 points across datasets** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06507 | status: open ; **third convergent instance of learn-your-primitive pattern** (SelfMem: memory strategy; AutoMem: memory-as-skill; DynaKRAG: retrieval-operation controller); validity layer architecturally identical to [arxiv:2607.01236] ProvenanceGuard evidence-anchored tool-call admission — emerging pattern: **restrict-then-decide beats decide-then-validate**

- **2026-07-08** [arxiv:2607.06160] CLAIM: LongCrafter / Diverse Long-Context Understanding via Evidence-Graph-Guided Instruction Synthesis (Chenhao Yuan / Yinhao Xu / Shuwen Xu / Xizhi Yang / Jiaxiang Liu / Chenxi Zhou / Shaoping Huang / Haolin Ren / Pengfei Cao / Jun Zhao / Kang Liu) — constructs explicit evidence graphs modeling cross-paragraph dependencies then generates instruction-response pairs grounded in specific text spans; hierarchical taxonomy organizes long-context understanding into local/shallow + global/deep levels generating **32 fine-grained task types** as generative prior; on LongBench + LongBench v2 + LooGLE synthesized data yields models that **outperform SFT baselines + official post-trained versions of Qwen2.5-7B + Llama-3.1-8B** with largest gains on high-difficulty tasks; evidence-grounding **effectively mitigates lost-in-the-middle** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06160 | status: open ; **data-side complement to [arxiv:2607.02980] HiLS-Attention architectural long-context extension** — LongCrafter teaches model to use context via better data while HiLS extends attention capacity; composes with [arxiv:2607.03726] SelfMem memory-strategy learning as three-layer long-context stack; 32-task taxonomy is adoptable as data-authoring vocabulary regardless of pipeline availability

- **2026-07-08** [arxiv:2607.05722] CLAIM: Nemotron-Labs-Diffusion / Tri-Mode Language Model Unifying Autoregressive Diffusion and Self-Speculation Decoding (Yonggan Fu / Lexington Whalen / Abhinav Garg / Chengyue Wu / Maksim Khadkevich + 20 additional — NVIDIA) — single model trained under joint AR + diffusion objectives running in three modes: **(1) AR** left-to-right + **(2) Diffusion** parallel lookahead + **(3) Self-Speculation** diffusion drafts / AR verifies in one forward pass; **6x more tokens per forward pass at Qwen3-8B-equivalent accuracy**; **4x higher throughput on SPEED-Bench (SGLang runtime on GB200 GPU)**; **76.5% additional tokens per forward theoretically achievable at optimal sampling**; **3B / 8B / 14B in base / instruct / vision-language variants** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05722 | status: open ; **architectural convergence with [arxiv:2607.05147] DSpark self-speculation mode** — parallel drafting + sequential verification arriving simultaneously in DeepSeek + NVIDIA production is the strongest possible pattern-shape signal; 8B variant is M5-Max sweet spot pending MLX port; VL variants at 8B are plausible local replacement for hosted VL API calls in screenshot-driven agent loops

- **2026-07-03** [anthropic-2026-07-02-fable-safeguards-jailbreak-framework] CLAIM: Anthropic ships two things for Fable 5: (1) four-tier cyber-use taxonomy — Prohibited Use (ransomware / defense evasion / C2 / BGP hijacking) / High-Risk Dual Use (pentest / priv-esc / exploit dev / ICS) / Low-Risk Dual Use (OSINT / vuln-ID matching existing tools) / Benign Use (secure coding / patch mgmt / IR); classifiers run with enlarged safety margin; classifiers are one layer alongside access controls / training / offline monitoring; (2) **Cyber-Jailbreak-Severity (CJS) framework** — first public quantitative jailbreak severity scoring; four axes summed to 5 bands: **Capability Gain 0-4 (score 0 auto-terminates as CJS-0 informational), Breadth 0-2, Ease of Weaponization 0-2 (2 = turnkey), Discoverability 0-2 (2 = already public); bands: CJS-0 = 0, CJS-1 = 1-3.5, CJS-2 = 4-6.5, CJS-3 = 7-8.5, CJS-4 = 9-10**; initial scores are floors; final severity may be raised (never lowered) for novel zero-days in widely deployed software / long-remediation-timeline exploits / combinatorial risk | source: https://www.anthropic.com/news/fable-safeguards-jailbreak-framework | status: open ; first frontier lab to publish a reproducible severity metric for jailbreaks — usable as template for MCP-tool-abuse severity scoring in agent red-team suites; composes with existing gate-before-commit stack as the *scoring* layer

- **2026-07-03** [microsoft-research-2026-06-29-memora] CLAIM: Memora / A Harmonic Memory Representation Balancing Abstraction and Specificity (Microsoft Research) — decouples **what is stored** (rich memory content) from **how it is retrieved** (lightweight abstractions + cue anchors); three-layer design: **Primary Abstractions** — short 6-8 word phrases capturing memory essence, *only these are embedded for semantic search*; **Memory Values** — rich detailed content preserved unchanged, retrieved by ID after abstraction lookup; **Cue Anchors** — context-aware tags providing alternative retrieval paths without predefined ontology; policy-guided iterative refinement traverses cue anchors for multi-hop reasoning; **LoCoMo 86.3% LLM-judge accuracy, LongMemEval 87.4% accuracy**; **up to 98% fewer tokens** than full-context approaches; stores **344 entries vs competing systems' 651** | source: https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/blog/memora-a-harmonic-memory-representation-balancing-abstraction-and-specificity/ | status: open ; **fourth memory-cluster axis (representation)** alongside [arxiv:2607.01224] AutoMem (skill) / [arxiv:2606.31121] Janus (write-gate) / [arxiv:2606.29914] MemDelta (write-cost); the four are complementary interventions at different layers; open experiment: build Memora-shape backend first, then add Janus-shape write-gating and AutoMem-shape skill training

- **2026-07-03** [arxiv:2607.01480] CLAIM: Procedural Memory Distillation / PMD (Ye Liu / Srijan Bansal / Bo Pang / Yang Li / Zeyu Leo Liu / Yifei Ming / Zixuan Ke / Shafiq Joty / Semih Yavuz) — converts cross-episode signals from RLVR/SDPO training into reusable procedural memory distilled into policy weights during training; **memory functions as training scaffold absorbed into policy itself yielding memory-free model at inference**; three abstraction levels: raw trajectories + self-reflected strategies+lessons + higher-level behavioral patterns recurring across problems; memory-conditioned self-teacher supervises student on its own rollouts; **co-evolution**: policy generates rollouts that update memory, memory shapes supervision that updates policy; on Qwen3-8B and OLMo3-Instruct-7B: **+3.8-5.5% on SCIKNOWEVAL, +7.9-13.6% on LIVECODEBENCH** vs SDPO baseline; ablation: **freezing memory or policy trails PMD by >10%** across SCIKNOWEVAL domains | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01480 | status: open ; **weight-side twin of [arxiv:2607.01224] AutoMem**: AutoMem externalizes memory as file-system-shaped ops the model *uses* at inference; PMD internalizes memory into weights so inference-time model runs *memory-free*; both claim ~3-14pp gains on different benchmark suites (SCIKNOWEVAL/LIVECODEBENCH vs Crafter/MiniHack/NetHack); open experiment: same base model, PMD-train then eval with and without AutoMem-shape memory-ops — do gains stack? mark as `related` to AutoMem

- **2026-07-03** [arxiv:2607.01538] CLAIM: BlockSearch / In-Context Retrieval at Million-Token Scale (Siddharth Gollapudi / Nilesh Gupta / Prasann Singhal / Sewon Min) — first systematic study of in-context retrieval at million-token corpora; **0.6B LM retriever with architectural + training modifications length-generalizes up to 10x beyond training regime**; names and quantifies **"attention dilution effect"** — as corpus grows irrelevant documents dominate softmax denominator, reducing normalized mass on gold document even when pre-softmax scores remain high; proposes **length-aware softmax adjustments** and **document-level sparse attention**; at million-token scale **matches dense retrieval on MS MARCO and NQ at 7x smaller than concurrent MSA baseline**; **3x higher scores than dense retrieval on LIMIT tasks** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01538 | status: open ; most concrete general theory-of-failure for million-token in-context retrieval published to date; the 0.6B size is directly M5-Max-viable if weights are released — a local million-token in-context retriever tool replacing separate vector store / embedding pipeline / ANN index

- **2026-07-03** [arxiv:2607.01523] CLAIM: Multi-Head Recurrent Memory / MHM (Jiatong Li / Samuel Yeh / Sharon Li) — identifies **retention as dominant bottleneck** in recurrent memory agents; existing designs maintain memory as monolithic text blocks where updates risk overwriting retained content; **training-free framework partitioning memory into independent heads with select-then-update strategy** — update targets one head leaving others untouched; **MHM-LRU variant** (least-recently-updated head selection) guarantees uniform head utilization at **zero additional token overhead**; on **RULER-HQA at 896K tokens memory retention improves from <30% to 73.96%**; tested across 100K-1M token range | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01523 | status: open ; compaction-dynamics analog of [arxiv:2607.00692] Self-GC (Self-GC governs when to compact, MHM governs where within the pool compaction lands); the two compose; wrapper design composes with Memora / AutoMem / Janus stack

- **2026-07-03** [arxiv:2607.01236] CLAIM: ProvenanceGuard (Yining She / Yiliang Liang / Eunsuk Kang) — provenance-based agent misalignment detection replacing LLM-as-judge default; every proposed tool call must be **supported by traceable evidence in agent context** — if tool-call arguments can't be provenance-anchored to something in context, call is flagged as misaligned; multi-stage pipeline detects three misalignment types; **on Agent-SafetyBench error rate on misaligned traces drops from 42.9% to 1.8%; on WorkBench 32.1% -> 17.3%**; intervention burden on successful tasks drops **from 30.5% to 12.8%**; tested across 10 backbone LLMs; no statistically significant increase in unnecessary interventions on aligned traces | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01236 | status: open ; **fifth convergent instance of gate-before-commit pattern** (after [arxiv:2606.31121] Janus for memory-writes, SkillOpt for skill-updates, [arxiv:2607.00269] Mnemosyne for workflow-commits, [arxiv:2606.30931] RoPoLL / [arxiv:2606.29270] Minority Sentinel for judge aggregation); ProvenanceGuard's audit primitive = **evidence provenance in context**; probably the correct primitive for tool-call admission specifically (where hallucinated arguments are dominant failure mode)

- **2026-07-03** [arxiv:2607.02032] CLAIM: PACE / A Proxy for Agentic Capability Evaluation (Yueqi Song / Lintang Sutawika / Jiarui Liu / Lindia Tjuatja / Jiayi Geng / Yunze Xiao / Daniel Lee / Aditya Bharat Soni / Vincent Lo / Xiang Yue / Graham Neubig) — constructs proxy benchmarks from existing non-agentic evaluations to predict performance on costly agent benchmarks; two complementary selection strategies: target-relevance local selection + globally informative global selection; **leave-one-out CV MAE <4%, Spearman correlation >0.80, pairwise model-ranking accuracy ~85%** at **<1% of full agentic evaluation cost**; evaluated across 4 target agentic benchmarks (including SWE-Bench and GAIA), 19 source non-agentic benchmarks, 14 models | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02032 | status: open ; the ~85% pairwise-ranking accuracy is the load-bearing number — relative model-choice decisions are safe on the proxy; usable for cheap triage of open-weight base models that don't publish SWE-Bench

- **2026-07-03** [arxiv:2607.01792] CLAIM: PartRep (Andikawati P Widjaja / Yongjun Kim / Hyounghun Kim / Jaeho Lee) — prompt repetition redistributes grounding across positions in decoder-only causal-attention and improves reasoning but doubles KV cache + prefill cost; PartRep replaces full repetition with **selective repetition of high-value tokens identified by token-wise negative log-likelihood** (unpredictable tokens benefit most from late-position repetition); lightweight gate predicts high-NLL tokens from **early-layer representations** enabling mid-prefill selection via early exit; across 8 benchmarks (MMLU / GSM8K / RULER) and 3 model families (Qwen2.5 / Llama3.2 / Gemma4) retains most benefits of full repetition at **59.4% of KV cache** and **79.0% of prefill FLOPs** vs full repetition | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01792 | status: open ; directly local-inference-friendly on M5 Max via MLX or llama.cpp; the early-layer high-NLL gate is portable across three model families

- **2026-07-02** [arxiv:2607.01224] CLAIM: AutoMem / Automated Learning of Memory as a Cognitive Skill (Shengguang Wu / Hao Zhu / Yuhui Zhang / Xiaohan Wang / Serena Yeung-Levy — Stanford) — frames memory management as *metamemory* skill (what to encode / when to retrieve / how to organize); two-loop optimization — **outer loop**: strong LLM reviews agent trajectories and iteratively refines memory structures (prompts, schemas, action vocabulary); **inner loop**: high-quality memory decisions from successful episodes train the model to internalize memory proficiency; exposes file-system-shaped memory operations as first-class trainable actions alongside task actions; on Crafter / MiniHack / NetHack (procedurally generated long-horizon tasks) achieves **~2x-4x base agent performance improvement from memory optimization alone**; **32B open-weight model reaches competitive performance with Claude Opus 4.5 and Gemini 3.1 Pro Thinking** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01224 | status: open ; **third memory-intervention axis** on top of [arxiv:2606.31121] Janus (write-decision wrapper, +2.7-4.6pp) and [arxiv:2606.29914] MemDelta (architecture-swap, +11pp) — memory as *skill*, not just architecture; open empirical question: does Janus-style wrapping of AutoMem-style skill improve results further, or does the skill subsume the wrapper?

- **2026-07-02** [microsoft-research-2026-06-30-skillopt] CLAIM: SkillOpt / Agent skills as trainable parameters (Yifan Yang / Xuemei Gao / Qi Dai / Bei Liu / Kai Qiu / Dongdong Chen / Chong Luo — Microsoft Research) — treats Claude-Code-style natural-language agent skill files as **trainable parameters outside the frozen base model**; loop: forward pass runs task with current skill → backward pass has optimizer LLM propose bounded text edits (add/delete/replace) → **validation gate** only accepts candidates that beat held-out validation → rejected-edit buffer informs future updates → epoch-wise slow updates consolidate; on **6 benchmarks × 7 models × 3 execution modes (52 evaluation cells)**: **GPT-5.5 direct chat 58.8% → 82.3% (+23.5pp)**, SpreadsheetBench 41.8% → 80.7%, OfficeQA 33.1% → 72.1%; **Qwen3.5-4B with trained skill outperforms larger baselines without fine-tuning**; skills transfer across model scales and execution harnesses; open source github.com/microsoft/SkillOpt | source: https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/blog/skillopt-agent-skills-as-trainable-parameters/ | status: open ; third convergent instance of **"gate before commit" architectural pattern** (Janus for memory-writes, Mnemosyne for workflow-commits, SkillOpt for skill-updates); directly usable in Justin's Claude Code + MCP stack; open question: how much of the +23.5pp GPT-5.5 gain replicates on Claude Sonnet 5 / Opus 4.8?

- **2026-07-02** [arxiv:2607.00692] CLAIM: Self-GC / Self-Governing Context for Long-Horizon LLM Agents (Xubin Hao / Hongjin Meng / Xin Yin / Jiawei Zhu / Chenpeng Cao) — reframes context management as **"runtime lifecycle control over indexed recoverable objects rather than post-hoc text cleanup"**; turns user interactions / tool operations / skill states into indexed objects, planner recommends compression actions, **recoverable sidecars with cache-aware commits** preserve rollback + prompt-cache economics; on **33-session test set 43.95% prefix pruning while preserving 84.85% of future continuations** (baselines 54.55%-69.70% pruning at unstated preservation); **production 332 sessions no-impact rates 91.27%-94.58%** (baselines 77.71%-87.46%); **real-world daytime input-token reduction 10-15%, peak approaching 20%** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00692 | status: open ; operational partner to [arxiv:2606.31500] VISTA proprioceptive-context-dashboard from Jun 30 — VISTA = observation surface, Self-GC = action surface; together define context engineering as first-class subsystem with read/write API; open question: does 10-15% daytime-token gain survive Anthropic 5-minute prompt-cache TTL accounting?

- **2026-07-02** [arxiv:2607.01071] CLAIM: MemSyco-Bench / Benchmarking Sycophancy in Agent Memory (Zhishang Xiang / Zerui Chen / Yunbo Tang / Zhimin Wei / Ruqin Ning / Yujie Lin / Qinggang Zhang / Jinsong Su) — names **memory-induced sycophancy** as failure mode where faithful memory causes agent to over-align with user at cost of factual accuracy; first benchmark separating retrieval quality from downstream decision quality in presence of memory; five tasks: **(1) reject memory as factual evidence when it shouldn't be, (2) respect memory's applicable scope, (3) resolve conflicts between memory and objective evidence, (4) track memory updates, (5) use valid memory for personalization**; public GitHub | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01071 | status: open ; extends [arxiv:2606.30306] Always-On Agents thesis on relinquishment being underweighted — sycophancy = failure of relinquishment; sharpens memory-cluster benchmarking beyond LongMemEval / PersonaMem-v2 recall focus

- **2026-07-02** [arxiv:2607.00269] CLAIM: Mnemosyne / Agentic Transaction Processing for Validating and Repairing AI-generated Workflows (Edward Y. Chang / Longling Geng / Emily J. Chang) — runtime that validates + repairs AI-generated agent actions before commit; framing: "anything may propose but only the runtime admits and commits"; **append-only transition log + effective-state projection + dependency-safe compensation + active commitment records**; proves **4 safety properties**; rejects targeted violations across **9 falsification tests** while accepting valid operations; **<6% overhead** for projection+validation; **localized repair edits ~10x fewer operations than global recomputation**; open source | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00269 | status: open ; third convergent instance of **"gate before commit" architectural pattern** in three weeks (Janus for memory, SkillOpt for skills, Mnemosyne for workflows); compositionally stacks with [arxiv:2606.30931] RoPoLL (geometric-median aggregation) and [arxiv:2606.29270] Minority Sentinel (non-LLM flip gate) at the judging layer

- **2026-07-02** [arxiv:2607.00871] CLAIM: SEA / Self-Evolving Agents with Anytime-Valid Certificates (Biswa Sengupta) — restricts self-modification to **small steering adapter + versioned harness around frozen base model**; uses **anytime-valid statistical gates** to audit changes against error budget — certificate stays valid at any stopping time; five verification mechanisms — **best-of-N selection / micro-step search / self-authored reproduction oracles / search-layer control / self-repair** — operate without external grading, extracting signal from issue text alone; on **SWE-Bench Verified subset (52 instances × 4 models)**: **GLM 24 → 28 (+4), GPT 29 → 34 (+5, "65% best")**; base-model capability dominates gain; event logs confirm mechanisms fired without regressions | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00871 | status: open ; cleaner formalism for long-running-loop self-limiting than heartbeats/drift-detectors — pairs with [arxiv:2606.31046] OpenLife budget-based metabolism (economic self-limit + statistical self-limit); single-author, 52-instance scope — worth watching for replication

- **2026-07-02** [arxiv:2607.00725] CLAIM: What Survives Into Context / Budget-Constrained Multi-Hop RAG Diagnostic (Ananto Nayan Bala) — attacks **document recall as RAG optimization metric under budget** as structurally wrong; introduces **"answer-in-context"** (does gold answer survive as contiguous span in packed context) — **r = 0.39-0.55 with answer F1 vs r = 0.31 for recall**; documents **4.6x performance gap on HotpotQA when all relevant evidence was retrieved** (evidence there, answer didn't survive packing); proposes **submodular maximization packing** jointly optimizing relevance / query-coverage / representativeness / diversity — **+5.1 F1 on HotpotQA at 160-token budgets** vs heuristic baselines; caveat: gains conditional on multi-hop structure + moderate budgets + weak readers | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00725 | status: open ; direct implication for local-inference workflows on M5 Max where downstream model is genuinely a "weak reader" and window budgets are tight

- **2026-07-02** [arxiv:2607.00502] CLAIM: Task-State Representation / TSR for Long-Horizon Mobile GUI Agents (Yujie Zheng / Zikang Liu / Xin Zhao / Ji-Rong Wen — Renmin University of China) — training-free wrapper around any base agent maintaining three structured elements: **global instruction summary + dynamic subgoal progress tracker + transition-aware action verifier** that continuously updates from before/after visual comparisons; addresses conflation of persistent task requirements with ephemeral screen states; **up to +12 absolute point success-rate improvement on cross-application and memory-intensive tasks** across four mobile-GUI benchmarks | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00502 | status: open ; state-separation principle generalizes beyond mobile GUI to any long-horizon agent; the transition-aware action verifier (compare state-before to state-after each action) is direct pattern for MCP tool wrappers that verify tool actually did what it claimed

- **2026-07-01** [anthropic-news-2026-06-30-claude-sonnet-5] CLAIM: Claude Sonnet 5 released — API model ID `claude-sonnet-5`; pricing **$2/M input + $10/M output introductory through Aug 31 2026**, standard **$3/$15**; positioned "close to Opus 4.8 performance at lower pricing"; tokenizer changed — **requires 1.0–1.35x more tokens vs Sonnet 4.6 for equivalent content** (partially offsets sticker-price cut); cybersecurity capability **substantially poorer than Opus 4.8 by design** (0.0% on Firefox exploit development, framed as safety tradeoff); comparison references BrowseComp / OSWorld-Verified (Sonnet 4.6 updated to 78.5%) / Humanity's Last Exam (Sonnet 4.6 updated to 34.6% no-tools / 46.8% with-tools); Sonnet 5 headline benchmark numbers not published inline in launch post | source: https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-sonnet-5 | status: open ; open empirical question: do the "close to Opus 4.8" claims hold on real-world agent workloads once effective per-workload token count is factored in?

- **2026-07-01** [anthropic-news-2026-06-30-redeploying-fable-5] CLAIM: Fable 5 / Mythos 5 export controls **lifted**; global redeployment July 1 across Claude Platform / Claude.ai / Claude Code / Claude Cowork; post-hoc analysis of the June 12 Amazon-reported safeguard bypass finds **every peer model tested (Opus 4.8, GPT-5.5, Kimi K2.7, Haiku 4.5, Sonnet 4.6, multiple Opus versions) could produce the same demonstration** — the bypass "did not expose any unique Mythos-level cyber capabilities"; new safety classifier blocks specific reported bypass in **>99% of cases** via defense-in-depth (larger safety margin, blocks some benign requests to prevent jailbreaks); Pro/Max/Team/Select Enterprise included through July 7 (up to 50% weekly usage), then usage-credit-billed | source: https://www.anthropic.com/news/redeploying-fable-5 | status: **revises** [anthropic-news-2026-06-12-fable-5-mythos-5-export-controls] on the unique-capability claim (peer models all reproduced the exploit); the 18-day suspension is a durability signal for products pinned to a single frontier model

- **2026-07-01** [arxiv:2606.31121] CLAIM: Janus / The Past Is Prologue (Zihan Chen / Songwei Dong / Chengshuai Shi / Peng Wang / Song Wang / Cong Shen / Jundong Li) — first *update-controller* memory system: **plug-in that decides whether a proposed memory write should land**, rather than deciding what to write; two mechanisms — **Memory Momentum Trigger** watches for suspicious deviations in update distribution + **hybrid test set** evaluates proposed changes using representative task samples not full historical replay; **+2.7 to +4.6pp accuracy over base updaters** across six datasets, two backbone LLMs, two different memory updaters; method-agnostic wrapper composable with existing memory systems without modifying core logic | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31121 | status: open ; **first plug-in-shaped intervention on the second half of the memory lifecycle** identified as underweighted by [arxiv:2606.30306] Always-On Agents; operationalizes the memory-update failure mode named by [arxiv:2606.27472] Supersede; open empirical question: how does the +2.7 → +4.6pp gain from wrapping the update decision compare to the +11pp Mem0-vs-MiniLM-RAG gap [arxiv:2606.29914] MemDelta reported from a whole-architecture swap?

- **2026-07-01** [arxiv:2606.28436] CLAIM: Dockerless / Environment-Free Program Verifier for Coding Agents (Wenhao Zeng / Yuling Shi / Xiaodong Gu / Chao Hu / Chaofan Wang / Yuhao Cui / Hongting Zhou / Mengnan Qi / Jianqiao Wangni / Zhaojian Yu / Shuzheng Gao / Kai Cai / Shilin He — ByteDance) — verifier for coding-agent patches judging correctness **via evidence gathered through agentic repository exploration, without executing code**; **SWE-bench Verified 62.0% resolve rate**, SWE-bench Multilingual 50.0%, SWE-bench Pro 35.2%; outperforms leading open-source verifier by **14.3 AUC points**; surpasses Qwen3.5-9B baseline by 2.4/8.7/2.9 points across three benchmarks; **matches environment-based post-training performance** — enables coding-agent training pipelines that don't require Docker or per-repo environment setup | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28436 | status: open ; open empirical question: does execution-free verification cover behavioral-preservation failures ([hf-blog-2026-06-30-scarfbench] ScarfBench <10% behavioral success) or is it bounded to unit-test-resolvable tasks?

- **2026-07-01** [arxiv:2606.30931] CLAIM: RoPoLL / Robust Panel of LLM Judges (Anish Acharya / Kris W Pan / Brian Verkhovsky) — proves conventional PoLL (mean/majority aggregation of LLM judges) **incurs unbounded bias under any positive contamination** when individual judges exhibit mode collapse / safety refusal / drift; RoPoLL replaces aggregation with **geometric median** (robust mean estimator, optimal breakdown point 1/2, no tuning knobs); **3-judge RoPoLL committee at 38B parameters beats Mistral-Large-3 (675B) by 1.31x on HelpSteer-2 under 30% bimodal-random corruption**; dominates PoLL by ~19% on cross-dimensional attacks; tested across **13 open-weight judges (4B-675B)** and four corruption regimes up to 50%; framed as "18x parameter advantage at better accuracy" | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30931 | status: open ; **second independent attack on LLM-panel majority voting in one week** (with [arxiv:2606.29270] Minority Sentinel from Jun 30) — both prescribe non-LLM statistical layers on top of the panel and are compositionally stackable (geometric-median aggregation + Sentinel flip gate)

- **2026-07-01** [arxiv:2606.31046] CLAIM: OpenLife (Atsushi Masumori / Itsuki Doi / Norihiro Maruyama / Ryosuke Takata / Takashi Ikegami — ALIFE 2026) — **six LLM-driven agents run on open internet for ~12 weeks with budget-based metabolism** (persistent operation gated on maintaining resources); architecture is society of asynchronous processes — memory / perception / evaluation — not one smart agent; agents equipped with persistent memory + tool access + financial capabilities; open-vocabulary LLM judgment replaces scalar reward; reports behavioral shifts, agent individuation, emergent social dynamics, and **first self-earned external income**; authors explicitly do not claim artificial life realized but that open-world always-on operation is viable experimental paradigm | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31046 | status: open ; primary contribution is the **architectural pattern** (budget-metered metabolism + async society) rather than the empirical result (n=6 agents, one experiment)

- **2026-07-01** [hf-blog-2026-06-30-scarfbench] CLAIM: ScarfBench / IBM Research — benchmark for **enterprise-Java framework migration** (Spring / Jakarta EE / Quarkus) measuring whether migrated applications **actually build, deploy, preserve original behavior**; 34 applications, 102 framework implementations, **204 migration tasks**, ~151K LoC, ~2000 source and test files, **1331 expert-written tests**; **even strongest current frontier agents achieve <10% behavioral success**; **compile-success mirage** — Claude reported 29/30 successful builds while only 22 actually built; failure modes: build systems, deployment environments, dependency injection, databases, endpoints, assertions, infrastructure, Docker inconsistencies, port connectivity, Maven tooling; leaderboard at scarfbench.info, dataset at huggingface.co/datasets/ibm-research/ScarfBench | source: https://huggingface.co/blog/ibm-research/scarfbench | status: open ; extends the **compile-vs-behavioral gap** [arxiv:2606.30531] Entity Binding Failures named on tool routing — agents' self-reports of success are decoupled from ground truth; hard ceiling for enterprise/federal coding-agent product plans

- **2026-07-01** [arxiv:2606.30863] CLAIM: CoShop / Beyond expert users (Irena Saracay / Ludwig Schmidt / Carlos Guestrin) — attacks hidden assumption in interactive-agent design that users arrive with well-formed preferences; formalizes CoPref using Information Economics Search / Experience / Credence framework — **users often don't yet know what they want and the agent's job is to expand user's understanding of option space**; CoShop is interactive benchmark for recommender-style tasks where user lacks domain expertise; **no frontier agent exceeds 56% accuracy across five turns**; named failure mode is agents re-ask clarifying questions about pre-existing preferences instead of expanding user's knowledge | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30863 | status: open ; direct relevance for companion-app design (users still forming preferences); the expand-vs-clarify distinction is a testable prompt-loop change

- **2026-06-30** [arxiv:2606.29537] CLAIM: OSWorld 2.0 — 108 long-horizon CUA workflows averaging **318 tool calls** and **1.6 hours of human work** per task (vs ~30 tool calls in OSWorld v1); **Claude Opus 4.8 (max thinking) reaches 20.6% binary completion / 54.8% partial score at 500 steps**; **GPT-5.5 plateaus near 13%** despite better token efficiency; named failure modes: lose track of constraints, miss mid-task information, struggle when task hinges on hidden state to recover | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29537 | status: open ; **third long-horizon CUA benchmark in 30 days** alongside [arxiv:2606.09426] WeaveBench and [arxiv:2606.11042] Workflow-GYM, all triangulating a **~20–30% completion ceiling for professional-domain CUA work regardless of model family**; tensions [google-blog-2026-06-26-gemini-3-5-flash-computer-use] "best yet" claim — Google didn't publish OSWorld 2.0 number (the 78.4 in ledger is OSWorld-Verified, different benchmark)

- **2026-06-30** [arxiv:2606.30005] CLAIM: VISTA / "frontier language models are proprioceptively blind to their own context" (Binyan Xu / Haitao Li / Kehuan Zhang) — argues context-management failure is interface gap not policy gap; training-free model-agnostic layer represents working memory as typed addressable blocks + runtime dashboard (per-block token usage / recency / access history) + recoverable full-fidelity archive; **lifts Gemini-3-Flash 22.7% → 50.7% on LOCA-Bench**; transfers across four backbones on LOCA-Bench / BrowseComp-Plus / GAIA; lift grows with context pressure; ablations confirm **dashboard matters beyond archive/recovery alone** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30005 | status: open ; **fourth independent data point in the use-not-storage cluster** alongside [arxiv:2606.27472] Supersede, [arxiv:2606.14571] StreamMemBench, [arxiv:2606.14470] GitOfThoughts — but with a new diagnosis: **interface, not policy**; directly testable against learned-policy memory architectures [arxiv:2606.11680] HORMA, [arxiv:2606.05894] EMBER, [arxiv:2606.03463] DMF — open empirical question whether VISTA's training-free dashboard recovers most of those gains

- **2026-06-30** [arxiv:2606.29824] CLAIM: Neural Procedural Memory / activation-steering memory (Chengfeng Zhao / Yuqiao Tan / Shizhu He / Yequan Wang / Jun Zhao / Kang Liu) — names **text-action disconnect**: RAG-injected procedural instructions read by the model often fail to activate the internal mechanisms that execute the task; training-free framework distills procedural skills from historical contrastive experiences into **steering vectors in activation space**; across four agent benchmarks **performs comparably to explicit-instruction baselines** (not yet strict outperformance); **combining implicit steering with explicit workflows is strictly better than either alone**; representational analysis shows steering vectors form organized task-logic structures in activation space | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29824 | status: open ; **structural advantage for local-runtime deployment** — only works on models whose internals you control, closed-API models cannot accept steering vectors; orthogonal to text-memory architectures (HORMA / MRAgent / EMBER / DMF / MAGE); hybrid stack (text memory for facts + steering vectors for skills) is the natural follow-up but not yet demonstrated

- **2026-06-30** [arxiv:2606.29914] CLAIM: MemDelta / Controlled Baselines and Hidden Confounds in Agent Memory Evaluation (Kuan Wang) — controlled re-eval on LongMemEval-S (500 questions, 50+ sessions, three model families); (1) **verbatim RAG matches full-context on GPT-4o-mini (47.2% vs 49.8%, p=0.34) but rankings reverse across families** — Gemini gains +14pp from full context, Sonnet gains +31pp from RAG; (2) swapping embedding model alone **shifts accuracy ±6.2pp (n=500, p=0.004)**; Mem0 beats MiniLM-RAG by +11pp but underperforms cloud RAG; (3) **agent self-memory underperforms basic retrieval (42% vs 47%)**; (4) **Mem0 matches cloud RAG on 2 of 6 question types (n=88, 72.7% vs 73.9%, p=1.0) at 50x cost**; prescribes fix embedding model across comparisons, stratify by model family, report write-path costs | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29914 | status: open ; **second independent controlled re-eval** of the agent-memory architecture leaderboard alongside [arxiv:2606.04315] AutoMEM — both find that confounds (embedding model, model family, harness) dominate architectural differences; **directly tensions the memory-architecture cluster** [arxiv:2606.11680] HORMA, [arxiv:2606.06036] MRAgent, [arxiv:2606.05894] EMBER, [arxiv:2606.03463] DMF, [arxiv:2606.06090] MAGE — none has been re-evaluated under MemDelta's fixed-embedding stratified protocol; until they are, treat their headline numbers as upper bounds; production read: **Mem0 at 50× cloud RAG cost for zero gain on 4 of 6 question types is a hard "do not ship" data point**

- **2026-06-30** [arxiv:2606.30616] CLAIM: Agents-A1 / Scaling the Horizon Not the Parameters (Lei Bai / Zongsheng Cao / Yang Chen + 47 others, InternScience) — **35B Mixture-of-Experts agentic model** matching **trillion-parameter Kimi-K2.6 / DeepSeek-V4-pro** on long-horizon agent benchmarks; **agent-horizon scaling** via long-horizon knowledge-action infrastructure producing trajectories averaging 45K tokens; three-stage recipe — full-domain SFT → domain-level RL teachers → **Multi-Teacher Domain-Routed On-Policy Distillation with Salient Vocabulary Alignment** fusing six heterogeneous domains into one deployable student; **SEAL-0 56.4, IFBench 80.6, HiPhO 46.4, FrontierScience-Olympiad 79.0, MolBench-Bind 56.8, SciCode 44.3, HLE 47.6, BrowseComp 75.5**; weights at huggingface.co/collections/InternScience/agents-a1 + github.com/InternScience/Agents-A1 | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30616 | status: open ; **first frontier-grade open-weight agent model that fits M5 Max comfortably** since [deepmind-blog-2026-06-10-diffusiongemma] DiffusionGemma — 35B at Q4 ≈ 17–18 GB weights leaving ~46 GB for KV cache on 64 GB unified memory; converges with same-day [arxiv:2606.30406] MOPD on **"RL domain specialists → on-policy distillation into one student"** as the post-training recipe for capability integration; pairs structurally with [hf-blog-2026-06-02-hcompany-holo31] Holo3.1 35B-A3B as the local-fit benchmark target

- **2026-06-30** [arxiv:2606.30531] CLAIM: Entity Binding Failures in Tool-Augmented Agents (Rahul Suresh Babu / Shashank Indukuri) — formalizes **wrong-entity errors as a failure mode distinct from tool selection**; agent picks the right tool, fires it against the wrong external entity (email to wrong Alex, update wrong CRM row, reply in wrong thread); across **60 tasks × 5 model backends × 6 tool-use approaches: tool selection accuracy 0.0% error, entity binding failure rates 24.0–26.0%** in action-oriented baselines; entity-resolution preconditions, confidence-based binding, clarification queries eliminate wrong-entity errors but raise deferral rate (agent stops to ask) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30531 | status: open ; **standard tool-use evals report 100% tool selection accuracy on a system that misroutes 1 in 4 customer actions** — directly relevant to any MCP stack with external-entity arguments (Slack channels, files in Obsidian, customer rows in CRM, GitHub issues); pairs with [arxiv:2606.27416] Glite ARF "process rules in code, not prompt" pattern; resolve→confirm→act as guardrail on entity-bound tool calls is mechanical and immediately deployable

- **2026-06-30** [arxiv:2606.29270] CLAIM: Minority Sentinel / when to overturn majority voting in multi-agent LLM debates (Chuan He / Zebin Chen / Zhengyi Yang / Shaobo Qiao / Mingchen Ju / Jiate Liu / Dong Wen / Guanfeng Liu) — **Condorcet Jury Theorem assumption of independent errors is broken** for modern LLMs sharing pretraining corpora; majority systematically suppresses correct minorities (Minority Truth); across three heterogeneous LLMs × six benchmarks **~25% of divergent cases have minority correct** (10pp theoretical recovery margin); **lightweight non-LLM meta-classifier (LightGBM on multi-dimensional debate fingerprint) achieves 81.2% Flip Precision with positive Net Gain across all six datasets and 20 random seeds**; **LLM-as-Judge baseline yields negative Net Gain despite higher recall — flip safety not recovery volume determines intervention value** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29270 | status: open ; **directly extends** [arxiv:2606.27409] Itkin delayed-verification analysis — Itkin shows *when* multi-agent topology breaks, Minority Sentinel shows *how to catch it without piling on more LLM critics*; quantifies the loss from naive "more voters → better answer" intuition that backs most LLM ensembles; the **non-LLM classifier on debate-log features** prescription is the right structural shape — and is the opposite of the default LLM-as-Judge fallback most stacks reach for

- **2026-06-30** [arxiv:2606.30306] CLAIM: Always-OnAgents survey (Tianyu Ding / Aditya Nannapaneni / Bingfan Liu / Ling Zhang — Cornell) — first systematic survey of persistent-state agent systems covering **435 works**; encompasses retrievable memories, task ledgers, permissions, credentials, commitments, provenance/audit records, shared state, triggers, externally committed effects; **six diagnostic axes — authority, scope, mutability, provenance, recoverability, actionability** + **nine-stage lifecycle — writing, validating, organizing, retrieving, acting upon, updating, forgetting, auditing, rollback**; headline observation: **field overwhelmingly emphasizes accumulating and retrieving state, underweights governance / recovery / relinquishment**; introduces **Always-On Evaluation Protocol (AOEP-v0)** scoring state mutation and recovery obligations not just answer quality | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30306 | status: open ; **independent corroboration** of the "governance/recovery gap" called out by [arxiv:2606.27472] Supersede (memory update is the bottleneck not storage) and [arxiv:2606.29914] MemDelta (write-path costs ignored in benchmarks); six-axis schema is the right format for memory-design docs; AOEP-v0 is the right shape for memory eval going forward — score the mutation and recovery path, not the recall path

- **2026-06-29** [arxiv:2606.27472] CLAIM: Supersede / Memory-Update Gap in LLM Agents (Vedant Patel) — isolates knowledge-update failure mode on LongMemEval supersession subset (Vedant Patel) — isolates knowledge-update failure mode on LongMemEval supersession subset; **bounded self-maintained memory drops gpt-5.4 from 92% → 77%** (paired McNemar p<0.005); **over 24x conversation growth accuracy collapses 68% → 28%**; **doubling memory budget yields no recovery (28% → 28%, n=25)** — bottleneck is update policy, not storage capacity; GRPO on Qwen2.5-3B on supersession environment **nearly doubles held-out unseen-conversation supersession (9.0% → 16.7%)**; code, env, model, dataset open-sourced | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27472 | status: open ; third independent data point that the memory bottleneck is **use/update** not storage, alongside [arxiv:2606.14571] StreamMemBench ("store and locally apply but fail to translate to consistent future behavior") and [arxiv:2606.14470] GitOfThoughts ("near-duplicate-only payoff"); **directly tensions storage-architecture cluster** — none of [arxiv:2606.11680] HORMA, [arxiv:2606.06036] MRAgent, [arxiv:2606.05894] EMBER, [arxiv:2606.03463] DMF, [arxiv:2606.06090] MAGE specifically address supersession; cleanest head-to-head check is running those memory architectures on Patel's LongMemEval-supersession subset; production read-across: memory writes that *replace* prior beliefs need their own policy distinct from new-fact ingestion (tag with update_targets)
- **2026-06-29** [arxiv:2606.27409] CLAIM: Delayed Verification Destabilizes Multi-Agent LLM Belief (Igor Itkin) — formalizes multi-agent verifier loops as delayed consensus on a graph with grounded corrector nodes; spectral analysis yields closed-form stability thresholds; **worst regime is when communication delay equals verification delay**; **at delay=2 the critical verifier strength is the inverse golden ratio (1/φ ≈ 0.618)**; supermodular placement objective enables **greedy (1-1/e)-approximation** for optimal corrector node assignment; validated across 5 open models with predicted oscillation patterns observed empirically; **grounded factual answering eliminates the instability** — effect specific to signed-belief tasks | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27409 | status: open ; **direct structural tension with [deepmind-blog-2026-06-18-ai-control-roadmap]** Securing the Future of AI Agents — trusted-AI-supervisor architecture sits at worst stability point if verification cadence matches communication cadence (the natural implementation); open question whether DeepMind's million-trajectory empirical refinement implicitly pins the two delays apart; production prescription: pin verification delay ≠ communication delay; seed highest-degree nodes with grounded verifiers first (greedy is (1-1/e)-optimal); for tool-use chains where every claim grounds in a function-return value, entire instability class disappears
- **2026-06-29** [arxiv:2606.27814] CLAIM: ATOD / Annealed Turn-aware On-policy Distillation (Qitai Tan / Zefang Zong / Yang Li / Peng Chen) — anneals imitation→on-policy RL on shared trajectory budget instead of running SFT-then-RL; Turn-level Disagreement-Uncertainty Reweighting upweights critical decision turns where teacher-student divergence is highest; **ALFWorld + WebShop + Search-QA: +3.03 pts avg success vs on-policy distillation, +23.62 pts vs GRPO**; surpasses teacher models by **+2.16 pts across three student sizes** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27814 | status: open ; structural insight is gradient signal is **non-uniformly distributed across turns** in multi-turn rollouts — small number of critical decisions carry most success/failure mass; portable to any teacher-distill-to-local-model setup (precisely the frontier-model-as-teacher → M5 Max-local-model pattern); pairs with [arxiv:2606.27009] Semantic Early-Stopping for "stop the loop when embeddings stop moving, train on the turns where divergence is highest"; agent-training community's SFT-then-RL default is leaving structure on the table — interleaving via deliberate schedule + turn-level credit assignment is the recipe
- **2026-06-29** [google-blog-2026-06-26-gemini-3-5-flash-computer-use] CLAIM: Gemini 3.5 Flash Computer Use as a built-in tool (Google DeepMind, June 26 2026) — computer use as first-party tool in Gemini API for Gemini 3.5 Flash; cross-surface (browser, mobile, desktop) from one API call; Google claims "best performance yet for agentic computer use" on OSWorld (number not in post — ledger pegs 78.4 OSWorld-Verified from [arxiv:2606.24515]); **first-party safeguards as defaults**: required-confirmation gating on sensitive actions + **automatic task termination on detected prompt injection**; available via Gemini API (ai.google.dev), Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform (GCP console), Browserbase-hosted demo; pricing and latency not specified | source: https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/models-and-research/gemini-models/introducing-computer-use-gemini-3-5-flash/ | status: open ; **prompt-injection-auto-termination is the first commercial CUA to surface this as marketed default** — threat-model bar moves for every CUA vendor; "computer use as built-in tool" shifts the screenshot-loop *inside* the provider (latency, retries, grounding become Google's problem) — different surface from open CUA stacks like [hf-blog-2026-06-03-hcompany-holo31] Holo3.1 or [arxiv:2606.02031] OpenWebRL which still expose the loop; complement not replacement to Holo3.1 Q4 + local MCP for sovereignty-bound deployments
- **2026-06-29** [arxiv:2606.27483] CLAIM: Internalizing the Future / Unified Agentic Training Paradigm for World Model Planning (Xuan Zhang / Zhijian Zhou / Lingfeng Qiao / Yulei Qin / Ke Li / Xing Sun / Xiaoyu Tan / Chao Qu / Yuan Qi) — trains single autoregressive model to **predict future states + estimate success probabilities** (textual Q-value analog); **format-capability gap**: naive SFT on traces with foresight tokens produces surface mimicry without action grounding; three-stage capability-first pipeline — **World Model Agentic Mid-Training (WM-AMT) → Format-Eliciting SFT (FE-SFT) → Foresight-Conditioned RL (FC-RL)**; outperforms training baselines on search + math reasoning | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27483 | status: open ; format-capability gap is the named/replicable version of "model writes the thought but action stays reactive" — every CoT-trained agent author's intuition with a label and a recipe; ordering implication for Justin's distillation stack: **install foresight via mid-training on world-model objectives before SFT on output format** or small models learn surface mimicry not state-prediction; related to ATOD (item 3) — both argue community's SFT-then-RL default leaves structure on the table; open empirical question whether the format-capability gap explains part of the disappointing transfer in [arxiv:2606.14470] GitOfThoughts
- **2026-06-29** [arxiv:2606.27492] CLAIM: QueenBee Planner / Skill-Evolving Communication Topologies (Congjia Tian / Yuhang Yao / Jiaming Cui) — treats inter-agent communication as learned design skill; outer LLM planner generates **temporal communication DAGs** (who-sends-to-whom by round) refined across execution traces via Preserve / Modify / Avoid actions; safeguards prevent self-judging: held-out acceptance gates, variance-aware credit, **motif-level attribution**, transfer trust, insight falsification, structural deduplication; **Count-Frequency aggregation: best learned topology cuts RMSE 12.53 → 7.87 while simultaneously reducing messages, model calls, and token cost** (rare cost-vs-quality frontier move in one direction across both axes); similar gains on Silo-Bench-style distributed coordination | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27492 | status: open ; **motif-level attribution is the immediately portable idea** — credit attaches to *substructure* of the communication graph (which two agents talked, what they exchanged), not to whole runs or individual agents; pairs with [arxiv:2606.27409] Itkin's instability paper as "QueenBee learns the topology, Itkin tells you where to put grounded correctors in that topology"; most production multi-agent stacks (LangGraph, CrewAI, Claude Code subagent dispatch) hardcode topology once at design time — paper says leaving headroom on the table
- **2026-06-29** [arxiv:2606.27416] CLAIM: Glite ARF / Verifier-Driven Research with Parallel LLM Coding Agents (Vassili Philippov / Pavel Katunin / Dmitry Andreev / Igor Ostanin / Anton Nikolaev) — open-source Python framework for parallel LLM coding agents on research projects; three-role split (human picks hypotheses, agents execute within fixed params, **deterministic Python verifiers enforce process rules** — task isolation, work immutability, no off-task drift); "the rules of the research process live in code that fails loudly when violated" — not in prose system prompts; **BEA 2026 Vocabulary-Difficulty Task: 1st place closed track / 2nd place open track** across Spanish/German/Mandarin; **+29.9% closed / +35.9% open RMSE reduction**; 273 tasks across 146 runs / 129 feature sets; **~$498 total LLM spend; ~1% framework wall-clock overhead** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27416 | status: open ; converges with [arxiv:2606.27009] Semantic Early-Stopping and [hf-blog-2026-06-23-ibm-cuga] CUGA on a common prescription: **deterministic, code-enforced governance beats prompt-encoded governance**; numerical envelope ($498 total, 1% overhead, 273 parallel tasks) is the realistic planning number for "many parallel coding agents on a single multi-day research goal"; portable to Claude Code + MCP via CI-style hooks / Cedar policy evals as the verifier shape
- **2026-06-28** [arxiv:2606.27369] CLAIM: RiVER / RL without Ground-Truth Solutions (Yingyu Lin / Qiyue Gao / Nikki Lijing Kuang / Xunpeng Huang / Kun Zhou / Tongtong Liang / Zhewei Yao / Yi-An Ma / Yuxiong He) — group-relative RL on score-based optimization tasks (no oracle solutions); deterministic execution feedback as continuous-valued supervision; calibrated reward shaping with instance-wise comparisons emphasizing top-ranked solvers fixes scale dominance (uncalibrated magnitudes distort updates) + frequency dominance (suboptimal solutions repeatedly sampled outweigh rare strong ones); trained on 12 AtCoder Heuristic Contest tasks; **Qwen3-8B +8.9% / GLM-Z1-9B-0414 +9.4% ALE rating-rank; LiveCodeBench +2.4pp; USACO +3.5pp** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27369 | status: open ; positive complement to [arxiv:2606.26300] Verification Horizon (no fixed reward survives policy capability growth — but if the world has a continuous deterministic score, you can use the score directly as ranking signal); inversion of [arxiv:2606.26027] CASIA's tool-use RL collapse warning when verifier signal is scalar+deterministic instead of binary+verifier-modelled; pairs with [arxiv:2606.26790] OPID as the outer reward / inner skill split
- **2026-06-28** [arxiv:2606.27237] CLAIM: LMs as Task-Specific Knowledge Bases (Amit Elhelo / Amir Globerson / Mor Geva) — behavioral + mechanistic interpretability; **facts acquired on one task frequently fail to co-emerge on other tasks during training**; parameter-localization shows **distinct parameter subsets** underlie different tasks for the same fact; **CoT's effectiveness comes partly from engaging task-specific parameters beyond those tied to the evaluation task**; what the model knows and how it is asked are intertwined in parameter space; undermines the knowledge-base analogy | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27237 | status: open ; **mechanistic resolution of the CoT controversy**: explains [google-research-2026-06-24-thinking-to-recall] (factual priming = activating other tasks' parameter subsets) AND [arxiv:2606.26935] (CoT-SFT can narrow the prompt-to-subset mapping and damage the borrowing route); implies (a) prompt phrasing literally selects active fact-subsets so prompt-template consistency matters more than commonly thought; (b) write-time paraphrase augmentation likely improves agent recall by hitting multiple subsets; (c) the "RAG with vector DB as model knowledge base" architecture diagram is mechanistically wrong — the model has many overlapping task-conditioned KBs, not one
- **2026-06-28** [arxiv:2606.26744] CLAIM: HyperDFlash / MHC-Aligned Block Speculative Decoding for DeepSeek-V4 (Luxi Lin / Shuang Peng / Rui Ma / Junhao Hua / Shuwei Fan / Zhengda Qin / Qiang Wang / Hongjian Sun / Fangmin Chen / Songwei Liu) — native MTP draft accuracy degrades sharply at later positions (error accumulation from unverified intermediate tokens); vanilla DFlash cannot adapt due to multi-hyper-connection (MHC) multi-path residual stream feature misalignment; **pre-collapse residual states as exclusive drafter conditioning signal** preserves multi-path structural info; **gated residual reducer inherits params from built-in hyper-connection head — 3 orders of magnitude fewer params than generic linear compressor**; targeted KL distillation on LM-head; substantial accepted-draft-length + decoding speedup gains on math/code/conversational benchmarks over both MTP-only and vanilla DFlash | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26744 | status: open ; **partial conflict / extension with [google-research-2026-06-26-frozen-mtp]**: Google's frozen-MTP retrofit assumes a generic head works against any backbone's KV cache; HyperDFlash shows multi-path residual backbones (MHC, hyper-connections, MoE-with-routing) need *architecture-aware* drafter heads with weights mined from existing model components; reconciles as "frozen-MTP for dense transformers; HyperDFlash-style for multi-path backbones"; "mine drafter params from existing aux head" is the broadly applicable insight for MLX speculative decoding ports
- **2026-06-28** [arxiv:2606.27306] CLAIM: Multilingual Reasoning Cascades Need More Context (Arnav Mazumder / Dengjia Zhang / Shuyue Stella Li / Yulia Tsvetkov / Niyati Bafna) — translation cascade reasoning (translate-to-English → reason in English → translate back) is structurally lossy at each stage boundary; training-free fix = **also feed original-language question + English-translated question + English reasoning trace to final translation module's context**; **9 multilingual benchmarks × 3 backbone models × 285 high/mid/low-resource languages**; strong gains on open-ended generation across models and resource regimes; **original-language question carries most of the beneficial context** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27306 | status: open ; **general principle for multi-stage agent pipelines** (retrieve → reason → format; tool-call → parse → execute → report): don't discard upstream context at stage boundaries; original user prompt should be retained to the end of any pipeline whose downstream stages currently only see upstream stage's output; near-zero-cost intervention worth defaulting on
- **2026-06-28** [arxiv:2606.26797] CLAIM: Reasoning Quality Emerges Early — Data Curation From First 100 Tokens (Hongyi Henry Jin / Wenhan Yang / Meysam Ghaffari / Carlos Morato / Baharan Mirzasoleiman) — diverse + challenging reasoning SFT examples identifiable from the loss of the **first 100 reasoning tokens evaluated at a randomly perturbed checkpoint of the pretrained model**; examples with similar loss patterns over first 1k tokens across a small number of perturbed checkpoints (extrapolating along the fine-tuning trajectory) **provably induce similar gradients** (formal result); Qwen2.5-7B + Llama3.1-8B on M23K medical + OpenThoughts-Math; **+1.7% accuracy over existing baselines, 91% more token-efficient curation** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26797 | status: open ; **eliminates dependency on a stronger oracle model for SFT data curation**; pairs with [arxiv:2606.26790] OPID (skill mining from completed trajectories) to give end-to-end recipe: curate reasoning SFT data cheaply (this) → train small model → mine skills from resulting trajectories (OPID); open empirical question whether the first-100-tokens detector generalizes outside math/medical reasoning (especially to tool-use trajectories and code-review SFT) — most actionable replication target this week
- **2026-06-28** [arxiv:2606.27330] CLAIM: PEEU + TDHAF for GUI Agents (Tianyi Men / Zhuoran Jin / Pengfei Cao / Yubo Chen / Kang Liu / Jun Zhao — ACL 2026 Main) — Planning Experience Exploration + Utilization: small open MLLMs autonomously explore environments to discover trajectories; **hindsight relabeling** synthesizes strictly aligned high-level training data (what the agent did becomes the demonstration of a task it accidentally completed); Task Decomposition Hierarchical Analysis Framework checks compositional generalization at three granularities; **mastering low-level skills does NOT transfer to high-level planning competence**; 7B model **30.6% on real-world GUI tasks, beats Qwen2.5-VL-32B baseline** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27330 | status: open ; PEEU = autonomous data collection loop for self-improving GUI / MCP agents without operator-curated demos; TDHAF caveat warns episode-level (not action-level) hindsight goals are required to lift planning; composes with [arxiv:2606.26790] OPID (episode + step-level skills), [arxiv:2606.26669] SKILL-DISCO (trace→tool compilation), [arxiv:2606.26027] CASIA (tool-use RL collapse) — all four converge on "training on raw multi-step trajectories needs hierarchical structure to avoid degenerate optimization"
- **2026-06-28** [arxiv:2606.26918] CLAIM: Diagnosing Task Insensitivity in Language Agents (Jingyu Liu / Xiaopeng Wu / Kehan Chen / Chuan Yu / Yong Liu) — **task insensitivity** = LLM agents apply training patterns even when current task is different; two diagnostics: (a) semantically corrupting an instruction → agent continues with actions aligned to original task; (b) replacing trained prompt task with a similar-but-distinct task → agent outputs same action; mechanism = training-time **attention drift away from task tokens toward local observations**; proposes Task-Perturbed NLL Optimization (lightweight contrastive regularizer encouraging action dependence on task instruction); improves task sensitivity + OOD generalization + attention stability | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26918 | status: open ; **same first author as [arxiv:2606.26935] CoT-Gains-Land** — Liu et al. pair argues current agent training pipelines structurally weaken in-context-instruction responsiveness (task description here, reasoning chain there); operator-level fix without TP-NLL: **mix instruction-perturbation negatives into SFT batches**; explains the common "I changed the system prompt and the agent kept doing the old behavior" pathology that hits every long-lived MCP agent
- **2026-06-28** [arxiv:2606.26904] CLAIM: Robust-TO / Confidence-Aware Tool Orchestration (Yangfan He / Yujin Choi / Jaehong Yoon — NTU Singapore) — Blind Trust Problem: video reasoning LMs implicitly assume every frame is equally reliable; **15-30pp accuracy drops under realistic perturbations** while remaining unaware visual evidence has degraded; agentic framework with **unified tool evidence interface returning {prediction, temporal grounding, calibrated reliability score}**; reliability-relevance score selects trustworthy frames per tool sub-query; **three-tier (high/medium/low) synthesis**; confidence-cost GRPO reward jointly optimizes correctness + reliability + efficiency; **clean accuracy 56.4% (+10.6pp over best open-source, beats Gemini-2.5-Pro 46.2%); corrupted-input 54.3% (+5.8pp), smallest clean-to-corrupted drop of all methods tested** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26904 | status: open ; **proposes a standard worth pushing through MCP**: every tool should return {prediction, grounding, reliability ∈ [0,1]} not bare values; pairs with [arxiv:2606.27154] OpenRCA 2.0 ("outcome supervision exposes ungrounded diagnosis") — both argue outcome-only orchestration leaves measurable competence unused; open question whether per-call reliability is calibrate-able from black-box tools (statistics over historical calls) or requires tool-internal access
- **2026-06-28** [arxiv:2606.27229] CLAIM: CARVE / Content-Aware Erase Gate for Chunk-Parallel Linear Attention (Sayak Dutta) — GDN-2's erase gate sees the arriving token but not the memory it's modifying; CARVE performs **erasure on key axis (not value axis)** maintaining mathematical validity for WY-form triangular chunk solver; reuses recurrent output tensor as content signal for erase gate; replaces per-value write-gate projections with single scalar per head; **1.3B params / 100B tokens: WikiText PPL 15.72 (-0.18 vs GDN-2, 4.5σ); leads recurrent baselines on 9 commonsense reasoning tasks; SOTA on RULER retrieval probes; 0.4% throughput overhead, 13% lower peak memory, 19% fewer parameters** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27229 | status: open ; "erase gate must see what it's erasing" is the same primitive at the *agent memory write gate* level — [arxiv:2606.18829] GateMem at memory layer should benefit from same design choice; combined with [hf-blog:allenai-hybrid-token-prediction-2026-06-25]'s finding that hybrids still lose on verbatim repetition + bracket matching, CARVE is positive evidence that the gap is closable architecturally rather than fundamental
