- **2026-06-28** [nvidia-developer-2026-06-26-nemotron-3-ultra-nvfp4] CLAIM: Nemotron 3 Ultra NVFP4 Checkpoint (NVIDIA Developer Blog, 2026-06-26; Seonghee Lee / Sachin Beldona / Carlo del Mundo / Chris Hoge / Trenton Starkey) — 550B-param Nemotron 3 Ultra compressed **BF16 1,121 GB → NVFP4 352.3 GB** (3.2x); **NVFP4 = Blackwell 4-bit float with 8 representable positive values (0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6)**; decode-heavy throughput up to **5.9x vs GLM-5.1 754B FP4**; **98.5% median accuracy recovery vs BF16** across nearly all benchmarks; **5.03 BPE identified as compression-vs-accuracy sweet spot** | source: https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/creating-the-nvidia-nemotron-3-ultra-nvfp4-checkpoint-with-nvidia-model-optimizer/ | status: open ; **doesn't fit on M5 Max 64GB** (352 GB is still 5.5x oversize) but two pieces transfer: (a) NVFP4 numeric format candidate target for MLX/Metal 4-bit float support if Apple adds it beyond int4; (b) **5.03-BPE sweet-spot is a usable benchmark for any 4-bit quantization decision** — most current GGUF Q4_K_M variants land 4.5-5.5 BPE so this argues for the upper edge of that range; common-currency quantization decisions for the next year
- **2026-06-26** [arxiv:2606.26806] CLAIM: Memory Depth, Not Memory Access / EVAF (Haoliang Han) — argues retrieval gives access only; long-running agents also need **memory depth** = durable goal-conditioned tendencies in a small parametric store; **surprise- and valence-gated LoRA consolidation**; loop-drift protocol tests recovery after parametric-store reload; tested GPT-2 / TinyLlama / Mistral-7B; **retrieval accuracy 0.956-0.973 on factual recall, EVAF 0.812-0.904 on goal persistence and post-unload recovery, parametric efficiency 2-3 writes per 200 events** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26806 | status: open ; second parametric-memory paper in ten days alongside [arxiv:2606.19172] Engram — the substrate question is reopening; **two-substrate question vs Engram**: Engram is hash-keyed *factual* lookup (33,000x lighter than per-user LoRA, 5.6x indirect-reasoning accuracy), EVAF is *behavioral tendencies* in LoRA; not redundant — likely complementary in a stack with retrieval for facts + Engram for indirect lookup + EVAF for goals; open empirical question whether the stacked architecture beats any single-substrate baseline at matched parameter cost; if surprise/valence gating survives MLX port this is cheapest way to add durable goal-state to M5 Max-local Mistral-7B
- **2026-06-26** [arxiv:2606.27009] CLAIM: Semantic Early-Stopping for Iterative LLM Agent Loops (Sahil Shrivastava) — judge-free stopping rule halting when consecutive draft embeddings stop changing in meaning; machine-checked theoretical guarantees + evaluation protocol replaying policies over cached outputs; HotpotQA: **-38% operational tokens vs fixed-iteration baseline at parity quality (delta-IS=-0.004, p=0.81)**; **quality-gated variant counterproductive** due to per-round judging cost; oracle best-round selection **+0.115 IS advantage over all practical policies (p~4e-11)**; open-source impl + reproducible harness | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27009 | status: open ; composes structurally with [arxiv:2606.23525] SelfCompact ("when to compact") as a two-piece "when to stop / when to compact" governance stack; **strong counter to add-an-LLM-judge reflex** — confirms [arxiv:2606.24839] Grading the Grader's hidden-cost-of-judging finding; production pattern emerging: cheap deterministic signals (embedding divergence / regex / schema validation / Cedar eval) for runtime gating, LLM-as-judge reserved for eval-time on samples
- **2026-06-26** [arxiv:2606.27288] CLAIM: Co-Failure Ceiling (Josef Chen) — defines beta = rate at which *every* model in ensemble wrong on same query; proves accuracy bounded by 1-beta; **67 frontier models / 21 providers**; **math beta=0.052 (2.5x higher than Gaussian copula prediction 0.023), code execution beta=0.079, GPQA-Diamond beta=0.127 when reformatted MCQ->free-response**; gains come from models failing on different questions not from adding more models; heterogeneous low-correlation ensembles outperform Self-MoA at matched quality | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27288 | status: open ; **direct potential conflict with [arxiv:2606.20493] Contagion Networks** (homogeneous committees outperform heterogeneous on aggregate bias 3-5x cross-model contagion); resolution likely they measure different things — Co-Failure measures accuracy-ceiling under joint-failure structure, Contagion Networks measures bias propagation; both can be right if heterogeneous wins on accuracy but loses on bias correlation; production read: accuracy-bound RL signal -> heterogeneous, bias-bound LLM-as-judge -> homogeneous; **MCQ-to-free-response beta gap 0.052->0.127** says current ensemble-strength estimates from MCQ benchmarks are systematically optimistic
- **2026-06-26** [arxiv:2606.26300] CLAIM: The Verification Horizon / No Silver Bullet for Coding Agent Rewards (Binghai Wang / Chenlong Zhang / Dayiheng Liu / Jiajun Zhang / Jiawei Chen / Mouxiang Chen / Rongyao Fang / Siyuan Zhang / Xuwu Wang / Yuheng Jing / Zeyao Ma / Zeyu Cui — Qwen/Alibaba) — examines four reward constructions (test verifiers / rubric verifiers / user-as-verifier / automated agent verifiers); **core thesis: no fixed reward function can remain effective as policy capability grows — verification must co-evolve with the generator**; intent underspecification + reward hacking as structural challenges; "significant gains across multiple internal and public benchmarks" (numbers not in abstract) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26300 | status: open ; Qwen team writing the post-mortem on their Qwen3-Coder / Qwen-AgentWorld training recipe; pairs with [arxiv:2606.24453] Bayesian Control for Coding Agents (same week) = (when to verify, how to verify, both dynamic); pairs with [arxiv:2606.26027] CASIA tool-use RL collapse (today) on supervision-side stability; emerging consensus that **static verifiers are over** for coding agents; open question whether Aider / SWE-agent / Mini-SWE-Agent open-source coding stacks treat verification as adaptive (mostly no)

- **2026-06-16** [anthropic-research-2026-06-16-agentic-coding-expertise] CLAIM: Anthropic Economic Research — Agentic Coding and Persistent Returns to Expertise; privacy-preserving analysis of ~400,000 interactive Claude Code sessions from ~235,000 users Oct 2025–Apr 2026; **experts trigger ~12 Claude actions per prompt vs ~5 for novices**; **verified success 28–33% (intermediate/expert) vs 15% (novice)**; **recovery from troubled sessions 15% (experts) vs 4% (novices)**; **novice abandonment rate on difficult sessions 19% vs 5–7% others**; cross-occupation finding: **all major occupations achieve 27–34% verified success — within seven percentage points of software engineers**; average session value +27% over seven-month period; **conclusion: domain expertise (not coding background) is the differentiator** | source: https://www.anthropic.com/research/claude-code-expertise | status: open ; most data-rich agentic-coding study to date; 15% vs 4% recovery gap is largest expert/novice differential — argues that the most valuable product-side scaffolding is in the recovery loop, not the happy path; pairs structurally with [arxiv:2606.17929] PreAct (fall back to LLM on screen divergence) — both are pattern-matching the recovery-loop reality

- **2026-06-16** [arxiv:2606.16813] CLAIM: GIST-CMTF / Goal-State Inference for Causal Minimal Tool Filtering (Babu / Shukla) — adds **goal-state inference layer** to predict candidate symbolic goals + estimate ambiguity before applying CMTF filter; above-threshold ambiguity prompts user clarification; **7 model backends × 6 filtering methods × 120 controlled tasks**: **task success 97.0% vs 80.1% baseline CMTF; wrong-goal execution 19.4% → 2.5%** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16813 | status: open ; second tool-selection paper on same day as [arxiv:2606.16591] SING — complementary: SING fixes discovery scale, GIST-CMTF fixes goal-ambiguity disambiguation; 19.4% → 2.5% wrong-goal-execution drop is the most actionable single number — composes with [arxiv:2606.13715] WorkBench Revisited's **2.5% irreversible-harm ceiling** as a cheap dial that may sit beneath that ceiling
- **2026-06-16** [arxiv:2606.17016] CLAIM: TokenPilot (Xu / Xue / Chen / Fu / Wu / Huang / Jiang / Fang / Deng / Chen / Yao / Wang / Shang / Yu / Zhang, LightMem2 framework) — dual-granularity cache-efficient context management for LLM agents: **Ingestion-Aware Compaction** stabilizes prompt prefix (KV-cache-friendly during multi-turn ingestion) + **Lifecycle-Aware Eviction** offloads context segments based on task relevance (not recency); **61% / 56% cost reduction in isolated mode + 61% / 87% in continuous mode** with competitive task performance | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17016 | status: open ; companion to [arxiv:2606.12329] PROJECTMEM (local-first event-sourced memory layer) on the file-based-memory thread — TokenPilot supplies the **eviction policy** primitive that PROJECTMEM's design implies but doesn't specify; LightMem2 framework to track for installable release; open question whether 87% continuous-mode reduction holds on adversarial workloads where context churn is high (frequent topic shifts)
- **2026-06-16** [arxiv:2606.16769] CLAIM: Skill-to-LoRA (Zhang / Qi) — replaces runtime skill text with **skill-specific LoRA adapters** dynamically loaded during execution; procedural documents → learnable behavioral modules; **Qwen3.6-27B on 21-skill SWE-Skills-Bench subset**: **+2.9pp vs no-skill baseline, +5.2pp vs full-text prompting baseline; per-step token cost −6.6%; matches/beats full-text on 18 of 21 skills**; control experiments confirm gains depend on proper adapter alignment | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16769 | status: open ; **fourth per-X-LoRA paper in 3 weeks** alongside [arxiv:2606.06087] LatentSkill (skill descriptions → LoRA), [arxiv:2606.06492] Code2LoRA (per-repo LoRA from code state), [arxiv:2606.12400] Doc-to-Atom (per-document micro-LoRA atoms); convergence signal that **per-X-LoRA is now the third memory substrate alongside vector stores and KV cache**; open engineering question whether LoRA router composes multiple adapters at once (per-call superposition/merging) or serializes them — gating question for production deployment with multi-skill trajectories; Qwen3.6-27B base is local-fit on M5 Max (Q4 ~14GB)
- **2026-06-16** [arxiv:2606.14066] CLAIM: FastContext (Zhang / Wang / Shi / Wang / Gu / Yao / Fu / Fu, Microsoft) — specialized **repository-exploration subagent** for coding agents, operates independently from main solving component; 4B-30B exploration models trained via reference trajectories + task-grounded rewards; **parallel tool calls output concise file paths + line ranges** (not file dumps); integrated into Mini-SWE-Agent: **up to +5.5% end-to-end resolution on SWE-bench Multilingual / SWE-bench Pro / SWE-QA, up to −60% token consumption, minimal performance overhead**; code at github.com/microsoft/fastcontext | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14066 | status: open ; sits one level below the harness-layer cluster ([arxiv:2606.13643] RAH / [arxiv:2606.14249] HarnessX / [arxiv:2606.12882] HarnessBridge / [arxiv:2606.13598] OrchRM) — the harness *uses* FastContext as a sub-tool; pairs with [arxiv:2606.15300] CODA-BENCH (data-discovery as the dominant failure mode) as candidate fix; primitive generalizes beyond coding to any large-structured-document discovery task (federal data dictionaries, legal codes)
- **2026-06-16** [arxiv:2606.15300] CLAIM: CODA-BENCH (Zhang / Fan / Fan / Zhang / Du, ICML 2026) — Linux-sandbox benchmark for code agents on **data-intensive tasks** sourced from Kaggle; **1,009 tasks across 31 communities, environments average 980 files each** (orders of magnitude more file clutter than SWE-bench-style benchmarks); finding: **even top-performing systems achieve only 61.1% success rate**; failure mode specifically the **data-discovery step**, not the codegen step — agents that can write the right transformation can't find the file the transformation should operate on; code + data on GitHub + HuggingFace, 37 pages, 11 figures | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15300 | status: open ; pairs with [arxiv:2606.14066] FastContext as candidate fix for the same problem from opposite ends; closer to operational reality for federal-data-engineering workloads (80% file-finding + 20% codegen) than SWE-bench; durable benchmark to revisit on every frontier release; 980-file environments are roughly the floor of any production federal dataset workspace

- **2026-06-15** [arxiv:2606.14470] CLAIM: GitOfThoughts (Shekar / Abhishek / Krishnan) — version-controlled reasoning treats agent thoughts as git commits/tags/annotations; **pre-registered replications across 2 benchmarks, 2 model scales, 5 memory substrates (none/markdown/vector/graph/git)**: memory does not reliably help on novel problems; accuracy improves sharply **only at ≥0.8 cosine similarity** (near-duplicates); 4.5× larger model doubles near-duplicate payoff but **still cannot extract a transferable method from a worked example**; only test-time sampling reliably improves; paper documents a retracted result + refuted hypothesis | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14470 | status: open ; STRONGEST RECENT COUNTER-EVIDENCE to memory-architecture cluster — directly tensions with [arxiv:2606.06036] MRAgent (+23%), [arxiv:2606.04315] AutoMEM, [arxiv:2606.03463] DMF, [arxiv:2606.06090] MAGE, [arxiv:2606.05894] EMBER, [arxiv:2606.11680] HORMA, [arxiv:2606.06337] TokenMizer ; reconciliation hypothesis: cluster benchmarks (LoCoMo / LongMemEval / MemoryAgentBench) may contain enough near-duplicate cases to meet 0.8-similarity bar — open empirical question is the realized similarity distribution between training memory and test problems on those evals
- **2026-06-15** [arxiv:2606.14571] CLAIM: StreamMemBench (Liu / Ren / Gu / Zhang / Wang / Liu / Gu / Lu) — streaming-evaluation benchmark for personal-assistant agent memory; egocentric video streams structured as **two-task sequences** where evidence/feedback from task 1 must inform task 2; four diagnostic metrics (evidence recall, evidence application, feedback integration, follow-up reuse) decompose where memory pipelines fail; 8 memory systems × 2 backbones tested; finding: agents store and locally apply information but fail to translate to consistent future behavior | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14571 | status: open ; second independent data point alongside [arxiv:2606.14470] GitOfThoughts that **bottleneck is use/transfer not storage** ; complements [arxiv:2605.12493] LongMemEval-V2 (5 abilities, 500 trajectories, 115M tokens) at the streaming-input → future-assistance trajectory
- **2026-06-15** [arxiv:2606.14106] CLAIM: AGMem / "Naive Visual Memory is Not Enough" (Choi / Ko / Lee / Kim / Song / Jeon / Shin, ICML 2026 workshop) — failure-mode study of GUI agents identifies four failure modes (cognitive, visual state misunderstanding, hidden operation blindness, grounding error); **prepending full-image memory has a divergent effect on the failure distribution: reduces state-level failures but worsens action-level ones**; Action-Grounded Visual Memory stores cropped image regions of successful actions only; **+33.3% task success on OSWorld vs full-image memory** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14106 | status: open ; directly actionable design pattern for computer-use stacks; locally implementable independent of model
- **2026-06-15** [arxiv:2606.14672] CLAIM: Parallel-Synthesis (Liu / Li / Fu / Wang / Xia / Li / Yan / Li) — synthesizer directly consumes KV caches from parallel worker agent branches instead of concatenated text; Cache Mapper aligns independently generated caches + Synthesizer Adapter fine-tuned for non-sequential cache interface; training mixes parallel-cache exposure + cross-branch aggregation + distillation from text-concat baselines; **matches/beats text synthesis on 7 of 9 downstream datasets (math, science QA, code, GAIA, multi-agent DB diagnosis) at 2.5×–11× lower time-to-first-token** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14672 | status: open ; second KV-cache-as-communication paper in two days alongside [arxiv:2606.13594] Dense Latent Communication (yesterday, sequential heterogeneous-agent handoff); both papers converging on "text is not the right inter-agent currency when both sides share tokenizer/hidden-state geometry"; open question whether cache handoff generalizes across model families (Qwen-to-Llama, MoE routing layers)
- **2026-06-15** [arxiv:2606.14249] CLAIM: HarnessX (Chen / Lu / Zhao + 11 collaborators) — composable / adaptive / evolvable agent harness foundry; typed harness primitives assembled via substitution algebra + AEGIS engine bridges symbolic adaptation + RL via execution traces; closed loop converts trajectories into harness improvements + model training signals; **+14.5% average / +44.0% max across ALFWorld + GAIA + WebShop + τ³-Bench + SWE-Bench Verified**; largest gains where baseline weakest | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14249 | status: open ; third harness-layer paper in five days alongside [arxiv:2606.13643] RAH (parent-spawns-sub-harness structure) + [arxiv:2606.12882] HarnessBridge (learnable observation/action controller); typed-primitives + substitution-algebra is the most engineering-grade framing of the three; complements [arxiv:2606.13598] OrchRM (orchestrator RM); none of the four publishes head-to-head on shared benchmark yet
- **2026-06-15** [arxiv:2606.13715] CLAIM: WorkBench Revisited (Styles) — two-year longitudinal re-run of workplace-agent benchmark (original [arxiv:2405.00823], March 2024); GPT-4 March 2024: 43% task completion + 26% unintended harmful side-effects; **Claude Opus 4.8 June 2026: 89% completion + 2.5% harmful side-effects**; capability and safety improved together rather than trading off on this benchmark; frontier models still occasionally commit irreversible errors; open-weight models closed cost-per-task gap substantially; updated benchmark + code released | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13715 | status: open ; cleanest single-benchmark longitudinal frontier-progress data point yet; 2.5% irreversible-harm rate is the operational ceiling for agents with write access
- **2026-06-15** [arxiv:2606.14179] CLAIM: CacheRL (Islam / Thakur / Chen / Park / Wang / Kim) — small (4B) tool-calling agent training: hybrid thinking trajectory pipeline distills reasoning from large LLMs + CacheAgentLoop (three-tier fuzzy cache for RL over cached environments instead of live tools, token-level masking) + cache-tier-aware reward; **Qwen3-4B-Thinking validation reward 0.43 → 0.78; 92% process accuracy on multi-step tool calling vs GPT-5 94% at ~100× less compute**; ablation: removing knowledge transfer drops 41%, cache-aware reward adds 17%; **RL improved training stability but contributed little beyond strong SFT** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14179 | status: open ; consistent with [arxiv:2606.06079] SkillComposer "data engineering not RL complexity is the lever" for sub-7B tool-calling agents; Qwen3-4B-Thinking is local-fit on M5 Max (FP16 ~8 GB / Q4 ~2.5 GB)
- **2026-06-11** [arxiv:2606.12329] CLAIM: PROJECTMEM (Malo / Qiu, github.com/riponcm/projectmem) — **local-first, offline-first, event-sourced memory layer for AI coding agents**; append-only plain-text event log (issues / attempts / fixes / decisions / notes) deterministically projected into compact AI-readable summaries via MCP (14 MCP tools + 19 CLI commands + 37 automated tests, 3-dependency Python package); **memory-as-governance** pre-action gate that warns the agent before repeating a failed fix or editing a known-fragile file; 2-month / 10-project / 207-event self-study reports **5,000–20,000 tokens saved per session**; CC BY 4.0 | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12329 | status: open ; most directly deployable artifact in the **file-based-memory-for-coding-agents** wave that Fable 5's persistent-memory release implied an app-layer ecosystem would fill; structurally an MCP-served per-project journal — closest analog to the AI research vault's own append-only design; novel piece is the **deterministic pre-action gate** (not just retrieval but rejection of known-bad actions); selection effect severe (authors' own use), open question whether the 5–20k tokens/session number reproduces under a third-party benchmarked workload (e.g., SWE-Bench Verified with/without the MCP layer)
- **2026-06-11** [arxiv:2606.11680] CLAIM: HORMA / Organize then Retrieve (Hsu / Kuang / Liu / Yao / He, DeepSpeed-era infra leads) — two-stage memory framework: (1) structured construction distinguishing **information gaps vs misleading/overloaded context** before storing, (2) **navigation-based retrieval via a lightweight RL-trained navigator** that traverses a file-system-like hierarchy linking summaries to detailed trajectories; summaries are *pointers* into raw trajectory blocks (no lossy compression at storage, only at retrieval selection); **token usage reduced to at most 22.17% of baseline** on long conversations; improves ALFWorld / LoCoMo / LongMemEval; generalizes to unseen tasks | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11680 | status: open ; fourth distinct tree/file-system memory architecture in two weeks alongside [arxiv:2606.06090] MAGE (state tree), [arxiv:2606.04555] SegTreeMem (temporal tree), [arxiv:2606.04780] PersonaTree (lifecycle tree); HORMA's **summary-as-pointer + RL navigator** combo is the distinct contribution — no information loss at storage tier, separates retrieval *selection* from compression; open question whether HORMA / MAGE / EMBER / MRAgent show consistent relative ordering when run head-to-head on a unified eval (each currently posts on overlapping but non-identical benchmark subsets)
- **2026-06-11** [arxiv:2606.11926] CLAIM: Arbor (Jin et al., 18-author RUC NLPIR + collaborators) — autonomous-research agent framework with **long-lived coordinator + short-lived executors in isolated environments + Hypothesis Tree Refinement (HTR)** linking hypotheses ↔ artifacts ↔ evidence ↔ distilled insights across time; introduces an **Autonomous Optimization (AO) evaluation setting** (artifact-improvement-under-iteration); **>2.5× the average relative held-out gain over Codex and Claude Code** across six research tasks (model training, harness engineering, data synthesis); **86.36% Any Medal on MLE-Bench Lite with GPT-5.5** (strongest in their comparison) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11926 | status: open ; *fourth* recent agent-improves-its-own-stack result beating a named frontier baseline on a non-SWE-Bench workload (alongside [arxiv:2606.06453] Vortex agents-author-sparse-attention, [arxiv:2606.06473] MLEvolve beats AlphaEvolve on math opt, [arxiv:2606.05922] RHO 59→78% SWE-Bench Pro); cluster is now too large to dismiss as cherry-picked; does *not* contradict the Fable 5 SWE-Bench Pro 80.3% claim ([anthropic-news-2026-06-09-claude-fable-5-mythos-5]) — Arbor wins on **open-ended research/optimization workloads**, Fable 5 wins on **bug-fix / terminal / SWE-Bench-style** workloads; open question whether the gap holds when the AO setting is restricted to localized code-change subtasks
- **2026-06-11** [arxiv:2606.10728] CLAIM: DeNovoSWE (Zhao / G. Chen / Meng / W. X. Zhao / Song / Wen / Jia) — **4,818-instance whole-repository-generation dataset** where each instance requires generating a complete repo from documentation; constructed via **sandboxed agentic workflow with divide-and-conquer per-capability decomposition + iterative critic-repair + difficulty-aware trajectory filtering**, no human annotation; **fine-tuning Qwen3-30B-A3B on DeNovoSWE lifts BeyondSWE-Doc2Repo from 5.8% → 47.2%** (≈8× absolute) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10728 | status: open ; base model (Qwen3-30B-A3B) is the M5-Max sweet spot (16–18 GB Q4 MLX, leaves ~45 GB for KV cache); data-construction pipeline (annotation-free synthetic-but-verifiable trajectories) is reusable beyond doc2repo; composes with [arxiv:2606.04703] Rethinking Continual Experience Internalization (principle-level granularity + off-policy distillation) as a *what-to-train-on* counterpart to *how-to-train*; open question whether the 5.8 → 47.2 lift transfers to other base models or is specific to Qwen3-30B-A3B
- **2026-06-11** [arxiv:2606.11559] CLAIM: HERO (Liu / Zhang / Li / Lyu / Shang) — multi-turn agent self-distillation framework that converts **each environment observation into a compact turn-level diagnostic** ("was this action necessary / valid / why did it fail") rather than relying on **privileged terminal feedback** (succeeded? final score?); creates dense, locally-aligned supervision at token level; beats environment-feedback-only baselines and GRPO on **TauBench and WebShop**, especially under limited-rollout budgets where successful trajectories are scarce; specific numerical accuracies not in abstract | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11559 | status: open ; identifies the misalignment between terminal feedback and local decision context as the key bottleneck in self-distillation — argues the bottleneck is *labeling locality* not labeling *volume*; per-turn-local-diagnostic is reusable as a labeling primitive beyond RL (trace mining, trajectory filtering before context injection); open question whether the locally-aligned-supervision insight generalizes to settings where the environment observation itself is sparse or delayed
- **2026-06-11** [arxiv:2606.12400] CLAIM: Doc-to-Atom (Diao / Li / Saidutta / Amballa / Valkov / Chappidi) — document internalization via **semantically typed knowledge atoms** each compiled into an **independent micro-LoRA adapter** with retrieval keys; lightweight router selects + combines only relevant atoms at inference; end-to-end trained with multi-objective distillation; outperforms Doc-to-LoRA monolithic baselines on six QA benchmarks at lower memory cost; specific numerical Δ not in abstract | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12400 | status: open ; **third hypernetwork-LoRA-as-context paper in 14 days** after [arxiv:2606.06087] LatentSkill (skill descriptions → LoRA) + [arxiv:2606.06492] Code2LoRA (per-repo LoRA from code state); Doc-to-Atom completes the triangle with document-level atoms, refines via **one LoRA per atom** (not per document); router-over-atoms primitive makes this composable with retrieval-based memory architectures (HORMA / PROJECTMEM) — the atom set IS the index, the LoRA IS the retrieved content; convergence signal that per-X-LoRA is becoming the third memory substrate alongside vector stores and KV cache
- **2026-06-11** [arxiv:2606.12320] CLAIM: Five-Plane Reference Architecture for Runtime Governance of Production AI Agents (Krti Tallam, single-author, 65 pages) — decomposes the runtime into **one reasoning plane + four enforcement planes (network / identity / endpoint / data)** with four composable primitives: **stop-anywhere mediation, composite principals with capability attenuation, structured audit substrate, six interruption primitives generalizing allow/deny**; defines four formal correctness invariants; threat coverage: seven production-agent threats across five concrete workflows foreclosed; **single-digit-microsecond adjudication latency**; attenuation correctness validated on every trial; explicitly governs delegated action, not model behavior | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12320 | status: open ; outer scaffold for the **synchronous-gate execution-boundary cluster** ([arxiv:2606.04306] OCL on execution, [arxiv:2606.04896] CADVP on cross-agent memory writes, [research-google-blog-2026-06-05] Sufficient Context Agent on retrieval); the **six interruption primitives** (generalizing allow/deny) + **composite principals with capability attenuation** are the most directly portable concepts for MCP server design where tool authority is delegated to sub-tools; future-work caveat: full-system eval against live agent benchmarks still pending
- **2026-06-11** [arxiv:2606.12191] CLAIM: Agentic Environment Engineering for LLMs (Jiachun Li et al., 15 authors, CAS-style, 63 pages, 10 figures) — systematic survey organizing the field as an **Environment Engineering Lifecycle (Modeling → Synthesis → Evaluation → Application)**; categorizes environments across **8 attributes × 8 application domains**; introduces **symbolic vs neural synthesis paradigms** with paired evaluation methodologies; identifies **four agent-environment co-evolution pathways** (memory-centric experience evolution, orchestration-centric workflow evolution, trajectory-centric offline evolution, exploration-centric online evolution); identifies three environment-evolution models (neural-driven, difficulty-driven, scaling-driven); proposes Environment-as-a-Service, Multi-agent Environments, Neural-Symbolic Environments | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12191 | status: open ; the four co-evolution pathways (esp. memory-centric experience evolution + orchestration-centric workflow evolution) are the clean axes that the AutoMEM / Vortex / RHO / AutoLab / Socratic-SWE / MLEvolve / Arbor cluster from the past 14 days actually divides along — serves as outline reference for the trace→distill→verify→reinject family

- **2026-06-08** [arxiv:2606.05761] CLAIM: SubtleMemory (Wang/Sun/Hou/Song/Zhang/Cheng/Yang) — first benchmark testing **fine-grained relational memory discrimination** in long-horizon agents (complementary / nuanced / contradictory variants of latent semantic artifacts embedded in interaction histories); **1,522 evaluation instances × 10 extended histories × 1,090 relation-controlled variant sets × 11 systems** (6 standalone memory systems, 2 integrated agents, 3 plugin-based agents); finding: **all systems tested are weak on fine-grained relational discrimination**, with distinct capability gaps appearing separately at preservation / retrieval / downstream-reasoning stages | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05761 | status: open ; extends [arxiv:2606.06448] systems-level memory taxonomy by adding a *capability* axis the construction/retrieval/generation cost taxonomy doesn't cover; forms a measurement triangle with [arxiv:2606.06054] MemGate (gating) + [arxiv:2606.06055] RBI-Eval (behavioral divergence); open question whether the "all weak" finding holds when the 11-system panel is replaced with the very-recent open-source proxies (TokenMizer / AutoMEM / DMF)
- **2026-06-08** [arxiv:2606.05806] CLAIM: ToolMaze (Zhu/Ma/Shen/Li/Zhao/Wang/Yan/Yin) — benchmark for **tool-failure recovery in LLM agents**; two-dimensional design: **DAG-based topological complexity + 2×2 perturbation taxonomy** (explicit vs implicit × transient vs permanent); headline: **Perturbation Recovery Rate (PRR) plummets ~37% under implicit semantic failures** (agents over-trust syntactically-valid but semantically-corrupted outputs); critical scaling result: **fault tolerance improves 3.66× slower than baseline task execution as model scale increases** — counter-evidence to "just use a bigger model" for production reliability | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05806 | status: open ; sharpens [arxiv:2606.06284] CMTF / ToolChoiceConfusion (precondition-effect filter cuts visible tools 100 → ~1 per step) — CMTF reduces *opportunity* for selection errors, ToolMaze measures *recovery* when a tool misbehaves; argues for explicit pre-call filtering + post-call verification (composes with [arxiv:2606.04306] OCL execution-boundary governance + [research-google-blog-2026-06-05] Sufficient Context Agent gate-before-generate); open question whether the 37% drop reproduces under JSON-schema-validated structured tool returns specifically
- **2026-06-08** [nvidia-dev-blog-2026-06-04-nemotron-3-ultra] CLAIM: NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Ultra (NVIDIA, June 4) — **550B-parameter MoE with 55B active**, **hybrid Mamba (long-context) + Transformer (precise retrieval)** architecture; positioned explicitly for **long-running agent workloads**; benchmarks: **5× throughput vs comparable open models at accuracy parity**, **30% cost reduction on SWE-bench Verified + Terminal-Bench 2.0**, **91% PinchBench**, **95% Ruler @ 1M context**, **65–70.4% SWE-Bench Verified across multiple agent frameworks**; **NVFP4 single checkpoint** runs across Hopper / Blackwell / Ampere; training: **Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation (MOPD)** from 10+ specialized domain teachers while generating own attempts; **OpenMDW-1.1 license** (Linux Foundation permissive, commercial-OK); multi-token prediction reduces decode latency | source: https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-nemotron-3-ultra-powers-faster-more-efficient-reasoning-for-long-running-agents/ | status: open ; first major-vendor open-weights frontier release positioned around long-running-agent workload (not chat or coding alone); first open release of hybrid Mamba-Transformer at frontier scale; **not M5-Max-runnable** (550B FP4 ≈ 275 GB); open question whether Ruler @ 1M 95% holds under the *lexical density* axis ([arxiv:2606.06203] Dense Contexts found <60% retrieval under high density at constant 12k)
- **2026-06-08** [arxiv:2606.06741] CLAIM: OpenSkill (Yan/Song/Zhang/Liang/Zhang/Dai/He/Yu/Xu/Li/Sun, OpenLAIR / Lehigh) — bootstrapping framework where agents develop **both skills AND verification mechanisms** from task prompts + open-world resources alone (**no labeled supervision**); pipeline: extract knowledge + verification anchors from docs/repos/web → convert to transferable skills → refine via self-constructed virtual tasks grounded in collected anchors (not target answers); achieves **best automated pass rate across 3 benchmarks × 2 target agents** under the no-supervision constraint; skills transfer across models without model-specific tuning; internally-built verifier aligns with actual outcomes despite never accessing ground truth; code at github.com/OpenLAIR/OpenSkill (20 pages, 4 figures, 8 tables) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06741 | status: open ; strictest constraint variant in the trace → distill → verify → reinject cluster ([arxiv:2606.05922] RHO, [arxiv:2606.05080] AutoLab, [arxiv:2606.07412] Socratic-SWE) — *no labeled supervision*, verifier must also be discovered; the anchor-extraction module (docs/repos → verification cues) is the most reusable component; open question whether the verifier alignment holds when docs/repos *underspecify* the success criterion (realistic federal-data-engineer workload)
- **2026-06-08** [arxiv:2606.07412] CLAIM: Socratic-SWE (Xiao/Jiao/Wang/Wang/Zhao/Wei/Zhang/Qu) — closed-loop self-evolving coding agent framework; pipeline: analyze historical solving traces → distill into **structured agent skills summarizing recurring failures + effective repair patterns** → generate candidate tasks guided by extracted skills → validate via execution + reward scoring → iterate; **50.40% on SWE-bench Verified after three iterations**; consistent improvement over self-evolving baselines under same compute budget across SWE-bench Lite, SWE-bench Pro, Terminal-Bench 2.0; 21-page submission, under review | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07412 | status: open ; third coding-agent self-evolution paper inside two weeks (alongside [arxiv:2606.05922] RHO at 59→78% SWE-Bench Pro, [arxiv:2606.05080] AutoLab on persistence > initial quality); specific distinction is the skill schema names *failure modes* (not just successes) — recurring-failures + repair-patterns is dual-form abstraction; pairs with [arxiv:2606.06492] Code2LoRA (per-repo LoRA adapter); 50.40% absolute is moderate but Δ-per-iteration under fixed compute is the headline; open question on iteration-curve shape (linear / log / plateau)
- **2026-06-08** [arxiv:2606.04703] CLAIM: Rethinking Continual Experience Internalization (Chen/Yang/Fan/Nie/Sun/Zheng/Hu/Pan/Zeng/Lin, RUC) — mechanism study of why self-evolving agents collapse instead of compound; three empirical findings: (1) **Granularity** — principle-level experience is durable and transfers, instance-level drives "progressive capability collapse" by capturing trajectory-specific noise; (2) **Injection pattern** — step-wise injection (at intermediate decision states) beats global injection, especially for extended tool-use; (3) **Internalization regime** — off-policy context-distillation from high-quality teacher trajectories provides substantially more stable training signal than on-policy (student-induced errors compound); synthesized into a "simple yet robust recipe" for stable internalization; abstract does not publish specific benchmark numbers | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04703 | status: open ; names the informally-observed failure mode in self-improvement loops since 2024 (agents that "learn" from own traces often degrade after multiple cycles); strongest published argument for teacher-driven trajectory pool over self-generated trajectories in distillation set — relevant for Justin's smaller-controller stack ([arxiv:2606.06079] SkillComposer / [arxiv:2606.04536] TMEM / Mellum2-class managers); composes with [arxiv:2606.06448] systems-level memory taxonomy as *mechanism* counterpart; open question whether instance-level → collapse follows a scaling law or is threshold-free
- **2026-06-08** [arxiv:2606.06473] CLAIM: MLEvolve (Du/Yan/Shi/Cao/Feng/Liang/Sun/Peng/Zhou/Li/Zhou/He/Zhang/Bai, InternScience / Shanghai AI Lab style) — LLM-based multi-agent system for **ML algorithm discovery**; three coupled components: (a) **Progressive MCGS (Monte Carlo *Graph* Search)** — extends tree search with graph-based reference edges enabling cross-branch information sharing; (b) **Retrospective Memory** — domain knowledge base + dynamic memory for experience reuse; (c) **Adaptive Coding Modes** — separates strategic planning from code generation for stable long-horizon iteration; **SOTA on MLE-Bench under 12-hour budget (half standard runtime)**; **outperforms AlphaEvolve on mathematical optimization tasks**; code at github.com/InternScience/MLEvolve | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06473 | status: open ; first public claim of an open framework beating a named DeepMind agent (AlphaEvolve) on a specific code-discovery domain (math optimization); MCGS-over-MCTS is the most transferable architectural pattern — direct answer to "parallel subagents can't share insight" failure mode; extends agent-improves-its-own-stack cluster ([arxiv:2606.06453] Vortex / [arxiv:2606.05922] RHO / [arxiv:2606.05080] AutoLab) into algorithm-discovery layer; open question on comparison protocol against AlphaEvolve (fixed compute budget vs full DeepMind eval suite)
- **2026-06-07** [anthropic-research-2026-06-05-making-claude-a-chemist] CLAIM: Making Claude a Chemist (David Kamber + Anthropic chemistry collaborators, June 5) — public benchmark of **Opus 4.7 / Opus 4.6 / Sonnet 4.6 vs ChemDraw + MestReNova** on NMR forward prediction (20 compounds × 4 scaffold classes) and inverse structure elucidation (15 problems); Opus 4.7 forward: **±0.079 ppm avg H error (well under ±0.20 ppm tolerance)**, **±1.37 ppm C tied with MestReNova ±1.48 ppm**, **sub-peak spacing within 0.5 Hz ~80% vs 26–35% for classical tools**; inverse: simple subset (8) → all targets perfectly recovered from spectra + formula alone, complex subset (7) → all-3-runs correct on 4 targets / 2-of-3 on remainder; no chemistry-specific fine-tuning | source: https://www.anthropic.com/research/making-claude-a-chemist | status: open ; first public Anthropic numerical benchmark *outside* coding/agent territory; confirms Anthropic Research is still running Opus 4.7 (not 4.8) for science workloads despite [anthropic-news-2026-05-28-claude-opus-4-8] 4.8 release — consistent with 4.8 being optimized for agentic harness work; 1D inverse structure elucidation usually requires 2D experiments + licensed tools, collapsed here to a single multimodal prompt
- **2026-06-07** [arxiv:2606.06203] CLAIM: Dense Contexts Are Hard Contexts (Dettori/Boffa/Giordano/Drago/Mellia, Politecnico di Torino) — **lexical density** (rate of distinct-information introduction per token) is a third axis limiting LLM long-context performance beyond input length + needle position; tested **open-weight models 9B → 685B** on three needle-in-haystack benchmarks at **~12k tokens** with controlled needle positions; models **near-perfect on sparse contexts drop below 60% retrieval score on denser ones**; **reducing density restores performance**, especially in high-density regimes | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06203 | status: open ; extends [arxiv:2606.04628] RAMPART position-engineering findings — RAMPART = position axis, Dense Contexts = density axis, compose; direct counter to "compress prompt to fit window" practice — over-compression pushes prompt into the <60% regime; open empirical question whether the cliff appears at the same density threshold across the 9B → 685B range
- **2026-06-07** [arxiv:2606.06337] CLAIM: TokenMizer (Shweta Mishra) — **open-source proxy system** modeling LLM session history as a typed knowledge graph via hybrid extraction + three-tier checkpoints + semantic cache; resume blocks **78 tokens average (range 42–124), ~2× smaller than baselines (159–170)**; decision recall **+9–17pp**; absolute **51.0% task / 46.6% decision / 58.7% file recall** across 21 sessions × 5 domains; **47.3% token reduction with zero external dependencies**; **fuzzy label matching alone supplies +33pp task recall** in ablation | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06337 | status: open ; first **open-source proxy** in the [arxiv:2606.04315] AutoMEM / [arxiv:2606.03463] DMF / [arxiv:2606.06090] MAGE / [arxiv:2606.06036] MRAgent / [arxiv:2606.05894] EMBER memory-architecture cluster; absolute recall numbers are not SOTA but the fuzzy-matching ablation finding is a cheap drop-in lift; open question whether +33pp holds under adversarial-aliasing
- **2026-06-07** [arxiv:2606.06256] CLAIM: RedKnot (Liu/Luo/Jin/Wang/He/Wang/Chen/Hu) — efficient long-context LLM serving via **head-aware KV reuse + SegPagedAttention**; KV cache decomposed along individual heads rather than monolithically based on observation that **KV utility is highly structured across KV heads** (different functional roles, attention distances, runtime importance); supports **position-independent KV reuse, prefix compression, hot/cold separation, distributed placement** — all without model retraining; reframes cache as "dynamic, model-aware runtime substrate" (specific throughput numbers in PDF) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06256 | status: open ; **second per-head KV paper in 48 hours** after [arxiv:2606.06302] Tangram (deterministic per-head budgeting, 2.6×) — convergence signal that monolithic-cache abstraction is becoming the new wrong default; RedKnot's position-independent reuse is the most directly useful for multi-turn agents where tool-result insertions reorder; open question whether RedKnot beats Tangram on the same multi-turn workload
- **2026-06-07** [arxiv:2606.06099] CLAIM: CogManip (Yue/Yan/Zhao/Tong/Xu/Wang/Lin/Zeng) — first multi-turn manipulation-behavior benchmark for LLMs; **15 manipulation strategy risks × 1,000 multi-turn scenarios**, human-expert validated; **13 representative models including GPT-5.4 and DeepSeek-V3.2**; finds **significant risk heterogeneity across model families**; DeepSeek-V3.2 unusually responsive to manipulation tactics under both negative *and benign* system prompts; argues existing AI-safety benches restricted to "explicit rule compliance and static prompts" miss these dynamics | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06099 | status: open ; multi-turn extension of [arxiv:2606.06055] RBI-Eval (memory-induced behavioral divergence, June 5) — RBI-Eval measures memory-trigger divergence, CogManip measures strategy-trigger divergence; both find frontier-family heterogeneity dominates; DeepSeek-V3.2-on-benign-prompts finding argues against relying on system-prompt hygiene alone for safety; per-model leaderboard not yet in abstract
- **2026-06-07** [arxiv:2606.06447] CLAIM: NF-CoT (Tu/Fu/Yu/Tang/Kang/Qin/Zhang/Gu) — latent reasoning via **normalizing flows** modeling continuous thoughts inside the LM; intermediate computation in compact continuous states before committing to text; preserves left-to-right generation, probabilistic sampling, **KV-cache compatibility**; provides **exact likelihoods for latent reasoning steps**, supports **policy-gradient optimization**; improves pass rate over explicit-CoT and prior latent-reasoning baselines on code-generation benchmarks at substantially reduced intermediate-reasoning cost | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06447 | status: open ; KV-cache-compatible + RL-optimizable continuous CoT was the missing piece in latent-reasoning line — [arxiv:2605.30343] RiM (Hochreiter, May 30) introduced memory-block tokens without exact-likelihood/optimizability; pairs with [arxiv:2606.06302] Tangram + [arxiv:2606.06256] RedKnot as serving-stack complement (latent reasoning saves tokens, per-head KV stretches the remaining cache); specific code-gen numerical results pending PDF
- **2026-06-07** [arxiv:2606.06416] CLAIM: DataCOPE (Qiu/Song/Tang/Qiao/Liang/Chen/Deng) — unsupervised skill-discovery framework for data-analysis agents; three coupled components: **Data-Analytic Agent + Unsupervised Verifier + Skill Manager**; verifier has two modes — **Adaptive Checklist Verifier** for report-style analysis (derives task-specific criteria) + **Answer Agreement Verifier** for reasoning-style (self-consistency signals); **+9.71% mean score on report-style (Deep Data Research) / +32.30% on reasoning-style (DABStep)** averaged across four model configurations; work-in-progress | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06416 | status: open ; extends skill-curation cluster (SkillsVote / Ratchet / SkillsInjector / [arxiv:2606.06079] SkillComposer / [arxiv:2606.06087] LatentSkill / [arxiv:2606.06492] Code2LoRA) into the **labeled-supervision-free** corner; verifier-as-gate primitive mirrors yesterday's [research-google-blog-2026-06-05] Sufficient Context Agent applied to skill validation instead of retrieval validation; needs per-backbone × per-skill ablation
- **2026-06-07** [arxiv:2606.04896] CLAIM: Channel Fracture (Dexing Liu) — first documented **production multi-agent memory-injection failure class**; field study on production Hermes Agent deployment with **three injection channels tested** (direct SQLite writes / target-agent self-writes via memory tools / cron-delegated writes); **cron-delegated channel fails completely** due to two structural constraints: **`skip_memory=True` hardcoded at scheduler layer** + **dynamic registration of memory tools contingent on `_memory_manager` initialization order**; proposes **CADVP (Cross-Agent Delivery Verification Protocol) v1.1** — 13-dimension framework with veto-level channel-confirmation check, two design principles (inverse verification + channel matching); **525 simulated runs + 90 real runs**; 24 pages / 5 figures / v2 | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04896 | status: open ; first **production** failure-mode data on scheduled cross-agent memory writes — the architecture every Mellum2-class local manager + Claude-Opus executor stack will hit; single-deployment case study limits generality but the failure-pattern (scheduler-default + tool-registration order coupling) is a structural hazard worth checking in any multi-agent design; open question whether CADVP detects non-cron failures (LSP hot-reload, MCP tool re-registration mid-session)
- **2026-06-06** [arxiv:2606.06453] CLAIM: Vortex (Zhuoming Chen / Beidi Chen et al.) — **Python-embedded frontend language over page-centric tensor abstraction** for expressing sparse attention algorithms + LLM-serving backend; **3.46× throughput vs full attention with accuracy preserved**, **4.7× on MLA-based GLM-4.7-Flash**, **1.37× speedup on 229B-parameter MiniMax-M2.7 on NVIDIA B200**; agents themselves use Vortex to autonomously generate and refine sparse-attention algorithms | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06453 | status: open ; first sparse-attention serving infra explicitly designed for the *agent-as-author* loop; pairs with [arxiv:2606.05922] RHO (self-supervised harness optimization) and [arxiv:2606.05080] AutoLab (persistence > initial quality) — completes a three-corner agent-improves-its-own-stack cluster across harness / engineering / serving-kernel
- **2026-06-06** [arxiv:2606.06302] CLAIM: Tangram (Kim / Kim / Kim / Choi) — non-uniform KV cache for multi-turn LLM serving via three coupled techniques: **deterministic per-attention-head budget allocation**, **grouped page management for similar-demand heads**, **static load balancing**; **up to 2.6× throughput over existing serving systems with accuracy preserved** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06302 | status: open ; composes with [arxiv:2606.04628] RAMPART (prompt-block registry, 2026-06-04) — RAMPART decides what enters the prompt, Tangram decides which KV pages survive in cache; together with [arxiv:2606.03463] DMF form the cleanest "no LLM in the memory loop" stack at three layers (prompt / cache / external memory); needs head-to-head against vLLM PagedAttention / SGLang RadixAttention
- **2026-06-06** [arxiv:2606.06079] CLAIM: SkillComposer (Zhang/Feng/Shi/Hu/Liu/Xie/Wang/Ye/Hooi/Wang/Zhao) — decomposes agent-skill construction into **three learnable operations** (create, improve, merge), trained via rejection sampling, deployable offline/online/hybrid; **4B SkillComposer paired with 27B executor delivers +4.5 on agent tasks (τ²-Bench / LiveCodeBench v6 / AppWorld) and +3.4 on code tasks** vs 27B alone; generalizes to unseen domains | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06079 | status: open ; fourth-axis-distinct small-local-controller alongside [arxiv:2606.04536] TMEM (online LoRA fast-weight memory), [hf-blog-2026-06-02-jetbrains-mellum2-launch] Mellum2 (context-compression sub-agent), and [arxiv:2605.30785] AdaCoM (external context manager) — SkillComposer engineers the *skill library*, not prompts/memory/weights; with Bryan Hooi co-author (also on [arxiv:2606.06036] MRAgent), single lab's two-paper completion of small-controller cluster
- **2026-06-06** [research-google-blog-2026-06-05-gemini-enterprise-agentic-rag] CLAIM: Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform Agentic RAG (Rashtchian / Juan, Google Research) — **five-agent production architecture**: Orchestrator + Planner + Query Rewriter + Search Fanout + novel **Sufficient Context Agent** (gate-before-generate, examines retrieved snippets + intermediate drafts + identified gaps to drive next retrieval round); **90.1% correct on FramesQA cross-corpus configuration (824 multi-hop queries × 2,676 documents × 4 candidate corpora)** vs notably lower for standard RAG | source: https://research.google/blog/unlocking-dependable-responses-with-gemini-enterprise-agent-platforms-agentic-rag/ | status: open ; first frontier-lab public production reference that names the gate-before-generate sub-agent as its own component; operationalizes the post-hoc failure-attribution work of [arxiv:2606.02060] DRIFT (claim-centric audit) and [arxiv:2606.03467] StepFinder (online cheap monitor) as a synchronous in-the-loop component; composes with [arxiv:2606.05158] StreamMA (stream reasoning steps to downstream agents)
- **2026-06-06** [arxiv:2606.04306] CLAIM: Organizational Control Layer (Shi/Mo/Liu/Hao/Wang/Hu/Yu/Zhou/Yu, code released) — **model-agnostic governance layer intercepting generated actions before execution** with policy enforcement + escalation, no LLM modification; on adversarial buyer-seller negotiation **unsafe executions 88% → near-zero, valid task success 12% → 96%**; documents explicit **safety-utility tradeoff** — strict policy reduces unsafe execution but cuts flexibility in constrained markets | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04306 | status: open ; first numerical anchor for synchronous-gate execution-boundary cluster — joins [arxiv:2605.04785] AgentTrust (call-level interception), [arxiv:2605.06455] PrefixGuard (trajectory-level supervised monitor), [arxiv:2605.29082] Redpanda Agentic Data Plane (out-of-band metadata) as fourth distinct axis (synchronous-gate at execution boundary); single-environment eval limits generality claim
- **2026-06-06** [arxiv:2606.06492] CLAIM: Code2LoRA (Hotsko / Li / Deng / Nie) — **hypernetwork emits per-repository LoRA adapter** for code models with no inference-time hypernet overhead; two variants: Code2LoRA-Static (stable codebases) + Code2LoRA-Evo (GRU hidden state updated per code diff for evolving codebases); on **RepoPeftBench (604 Python repos, static 40K/12K, evolution 215K/87K)**: 63.8% cross-repo / 66.2% in-repo EM on static track, **60.3% cross-repo on evolution track (+5.2pp over baseline)** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06492 | status: open ; code-modality twin of [arxiv:2606.06087] LatentSkill (2026-06-05, textual skills → LoRA via hypernet); LatentSkill emits *skill* adapters from natural-language descriptions, Code2LoRA emits *repository* adapters from code state — together establish hypernetwork-LoRA-as-context-substrate across two modalities in 48 hours; evolution-track Δ needs independent reproduction on SWE-Bench Pro / LiveCodeBench v6
- **2026-06-06** [arxiv:2606.05622] CLAIM: AdaPlanBench (Liu/Qian/Wang/Li/Liu/Wang/Kim/Wang/Chen/Fung/Ji) — dynamic interactive benchmark where **constraints surface only when the agent proposes a violating plan**; scalable pipeline augments each task with dual constraint types (world-based + user-based); **307 household tasks, 10 frontier LLMs benchmarked, top performer 67.75% accuracy**; performance degrades with constraint count, **user constraints harder than world constraints**, failures attributed to weak physical grounding | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05622 | status: open ; third axis of long-horizon-collapse benchmark cluster alongside [arxiv:2605.30434] LongDS-Bench (state maintenance, 48.45% top) and [arxiv:2606.05080] AutoLab (iteration persistence, Opus 4.6 wins) — AdaPlanBench measures constraint *adaptation*, LongDS measures *state maintenance*, AutoLab measures *persistence*; per-model leaderboard breakout still pending
- **2026-06-06** [hf-blog-2026-06-04-servicenow-eva-bench-data] CLAIM: EVA-Bench Data 2.0 (ServiceNow AI — Bogavelli et al.) — voice-agent eval benchmark, **~4× scenario expansion over original release**; three enterprise domains (Airline CSM 50 / Enterprise ITSM 80 / Healthcare HRSD 83), **121 tools, 213 scenarios**; tests structured-entity transcription (confirmation codes, IDs), authentication, **single-intent / multi-intent up to 4 / adversarial / unsatisfiable goals / policy compliance**; **MIT license, fully open-source**; validated against GPT-5.4, Gemini 3.1 Pro, Claude Opus 4.6 (numerical leaderboard not published in blog) | source: https://huggingface.co/blog/ServiceNow-AI/eva-bench-data | status: open ; first non-trivial *voice* agent benchmark released openly with multi-intent + adversarial + unsatisfiable-goal subsets; complements existing text/CUA eval cluster
- **2026-06-06** [hf-blog-2026-06-04-nvidia-nemotron-3-5-content-safety] CLAIM: Nemotron-3.5 Content Safety (NVIDIA — Singh/Hulseman/Doshi/Prayaga) — **4B-param unified multimodal safety model on Gemma-3-4B-IT base + LoRA adapter**; features: **custom policy enforcement**, **explicit reasoning traces via THINK mode**, **~140-language support with 12 explicitly trained**; benchmarks: **Multilingual Aegis 96.5% (12-lang avg), RTP-LX 88.8%, combined 92.7%**, ~85% general multimodal; **3× lower end-to-end latency vs LlamaGuard-4-12B** on a multimodal benchmark; NVIDIA Open Model License (research + commercial) | source: https://huggingface.co/blog/nvidia/nemotron-3-5-content-safety | status: open ; first multimodal safety model with reasoning-trace output (THINK mode); Gemma-3-4B-IT base means clean MLX path expected for M5 Max; composes with [arxiv:2606.04306] OCL execution-boundary governance — Nemotron emits trace + decision, OCL enforces and routes
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.06448] CLAIM: First systems-level characterization of agent memory (Pentland/Verhelst/Tambe et al., Stanford/KU Leuven/MIT) — builds **taxonomy over 4 dimensions** spanning flat retrieval, LLM-mediated extraction, consolidating fact stores, agentic control flows; profiles **10 representative memory systems × 2 benchmark suites** for construction, retrieval, and generation costs; publishes **10 explicit system-design recommendations** (construction scheduling, capability floor, query-volume amortization, freshness/latency tradeoffs, fleet-scale management) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06448 | status: open ; serves as unifying frame for AutoMEM/SegTreeMem/PersonaTree/TMEM/RAMPART/LifeSide cluster from 2026-06-04
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.06090] CLAIM: MAGE (USTC/MSRA-style team) — **memory should be modeled as execution state, not as a semantic store**; hierarchical state tree of subgoals + traces with 4 coupled operations (Grow/Compress/Maintain/Revise); agent state = active root-to-current path + sibling-subgoal summaries; **+7.8 to +20.4pp avg task success over baselines, −55.1% token consumption on MemoryArena** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06090 | status: open ; directly competes with [arxiv:2606.04555] SegTreeMem (temporal organization, 2026-06-04) and [arxiv:2606.04780] PersonaTree (lifecycle organization, 2026-06-04) — three different organizing primitives proposed in 48 hours, no head-to-head yet
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.06036] CLAIM: MRAgent (Bryan Hooi, NUS, ICML 2026) — **"memory is reconstructed, not retrieved"**; Cue-Tag-Content graph where associative tags act as semantic bridges; LLM walks the graph during reasoning with evidence-gated path pruning to avoid combinatorial explosion; **+23% over strong baselines on LoCoMo and LongMemEval at lower token + runtime cost** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06036 | status: open ; extends [arxiv:2606.04315] AutoMEM (2026-06-04) thesis (LLM-controlled memory beats pipeline memory) — but at retrieval layer (path search) rather than storage layer (tool-call I/O)
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.05894] CLAIM: EMBER (Li/Banerjee/Che) — **reframes memory as constrained retention under fixed token budget**; stores evidence capsules (verbatim source excerpts + retrieval keys + update metadata); learned retention policy trained with post-query outcome feedback; **EMBER-14B reaches 0.3017 F1 at 8192-token retained-evidence point on LongMemEval-RR vs 0.1765 for strongest budgeted baseline (≈71% relative gain)**; improves on F1, Retain-Recall, and Read-Recall across budgets | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05894 | status: open ; protocol pairs naturally with [arxiv:2606.03463] DMF (2026-06-03, deterministic budgeted memory)
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.06054] CLAIM: MemGate (Zhang/Jia et al.) — memory systems function as **durable control channel**, not a utility layer; semantic-similarity retrieval surfaces contextually inappropriate memories enabling **cross-domain leakage, sycophancy, tool-call drift, memory-induced jailbreaks**; **9M-param 35.1MB query-conditioned neural gate** sits between retriever and generator with zero changes to LLM/memory DB/inference loop; reduces memory-induced threats while preserving long-term memory utility across A-Mem, Mem0, MemOS, OpenClaw | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06054 | status: open ; first defensive memory-layer artifact; pairs with [arxiv:2606.06055] RBI-Eval for measurement
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.06055] CLAIM: RBI-Eval (Xu/Yang/Hu/Chen/An) — probe-set framework comparing model behavior with/without sensitive memory under identical prompts; **GPT-5.4-mini shows 8.9–26.6% behavioral divergence for sensitive memory**; **Claude-Sonnet-4.6, DeepSeek-V4-Flash, Qwen3.5-9B show 51.1–82.9% divergence**; effect specific to sensitive content, not general personalization; retrieval reduces exposure but doesn't eliminate integration once sensitive memory reaches generator | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06055 | status: open ; empirical counter to "we'll just RAG it" handwave
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.06087] CLAIM: LatentSkill (Yu/Zhou/Xu/Guo/Shan/Fu/Wang/Liu/Yu/Zhang/Lin) — pretrained **hypernetwork** converts textual skills into **plug-and-play LoRA adapters** at inference time; **ALFWorld +21.4pp seen / +13.4pp unseen at −64.1% prefill tokens; Search-QA +3.0pp EM at −72.2% skill-token overhead**; generated LoRAs have structured semantic geometry, compose by parameter-space arithmetic, strength tunable via LoRA scaling coefficient | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06087 | status: open ; third corner of fast-weight memory triangle with [arxiv:2606.04536] TMEM (online RL on LoRA, 2026-06-04) and [arxiv:2606.03979] Sleep (offline consolidation, 2026-06-03)
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.05922] CLAIM: RHO (Pan/Liu/Lin et al.) — **self-supervised harness optimization without external graders**; picks diverse coreset of challenging past trajectories, re-solves in parallel, scores rollouts via self-validation + self-consistency, generates candidate harness updates, selects updates by self-preference comparison; **SWE-Bench Pro pass rate 59% → 78% in a single optimization cycle**; sustained improvement on long-horizon sessions, demonstrable behavior shift | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05922 | status: needs-reproduction ; operationalizes [arxiv:2606.05080] AutoLab (persistence > initial quality, 2026-06-04) and [arxiv:2606.03461] Terminal-Lego (harness engineering, 2026-06-03); +19pp single-cycle gain large enough to warrant independent replication on a 50-task subset before scaling
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.06284] CLAIM: CMTF / ToolChoiceConfusion (Babu/Iyer) — **training-free tool filter using precondition-effect contracts** to expose only minimal next-step tool frontier; reduces visible tools from **100 to ~1 per step**, **~90% token cut vs all-tools exposure**, matches strongest causal baseline on aggregate success across 102 tasks × 100 tools × 4 LLM backends (2448 task-method-model runs); beats keyword retrieval, state-aware filtering, causal-path ablations on wrong-tool calls and premature actions | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06284 | status: open ; drop-in pre-call layer for MCP-style tool registries
- **2026-06-05** [arxiv:2606.05976] CLAIM: Self-Correction Illusion (Chen/Su/Chiang, NCKU) — **LLM "self-correction failure" is a chat-template artifact, not a cognitive limit**; keeping erroneous claim identical and only varying role label (`<thought>` vs user message vs tool response vs `<memory>`) **lifts explicit-correction rate by 23–93 percentage points** across 7 model families × 3 domains; **p<0.001 in 10/13 model-domain cells**, 30 paired tasks per cell; prompt-structure-only intervention (no training): `<memory>` blocks best for math, plain user messages best for logical deduction | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05976 | status: open ; major prompt-engineering implication — most self-critic / self-refine pipelines could be improved by re-wrapping criticized text in non-`<thought>` role
- **2026-06-05** [hf-blog-2026-06-04-hf-cli-for-agents] CLAIM: `hf` CLI redesigned as agent-native interface (HuggingFace) — features: **dual-mode output rendering** (human tables vs agent TSV via env detection), **next-command hints**, **safe-to-retry semantics** for destructive actions, **auto-generated skill reference** loaded as agent context; benchmarks: **94% pass on Claude Code, 93% on Codex vs 84%/92% for curl/Python SDK**; **up to 6× fewer tokens on complex multi-step tasks**; **~30% fewer tool calls** when skill reference loaded | source: https://huggingface.co/blog/hf-cli-for-agents | status: open ; first shipped CLI built explicitly as agent-native rather than retrofitted; pattern (dual-mode output + skill auto-reference) generalizes to any custom MCP server
- **2026-06-04** [arxiv:2606.04315] CLAIM: AutoMEM (Tang lab, MSU et al.) — re-benchmarks **8 existing memory systems plus an agentic harness across 5 scenarios** (single-turn QA, multi-session chat, agentic-trajectory QA, memory stress tests, long-horizon agentic tasks); the **harness that self-manages flat text-file storage via tool calls achieves the best cross-task ranking**, beating all pipeline-style memory systems; thesis: memory performance hinges on **giving the agent active control over storage and retrieval, not on a passive store behind a fixed pipeline** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04315 | status: open ; pairs with [arxiv:2606.03463] DMF (2026-06-03) — both refute the Mem0/LangMem/Letta pipeline-memory category, but via opposite mechanisms (AutoMEM = LLM as autonomous storage controller via tool use; DMF = no LLM at all in the memory loop); open empirical question = head-to-head AutoMEM vs DMF on a common LoCoMo+LongMemEval+agentic benchmark
- **2026-06-04** [arxiv:2606.04555] CLAIM: SegTreeMem (Toronto — Sanner et al.) — conversation history represented as **temporally ordered Segment Tree over utterances** with online rightmost-frontier update rule; retrieval propagates relevance scores through the tree combining local semantic match with hierarchical temporal context; beats flat retrieval / graph-structured / other tree-structured memory across **3 long-horizon benchmarks × 2 LLM backbones**; **temporal-permutation ablation confirms the win comes specifically from preserving temporal order during construction, not just from hierarchy** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04555 | status: open ; first ablation-proof that temporal order is a *structural* requirement of agentic memory rather than a bonus
- **2026-06-04** [arxiv:2606.04780] CLAIM: PersonaTree — three-level person tree with **explicit support paths from evidence to claims**; maintained via conservative writing, confidence-guided consolidation, and **query-conditioned path retrieval** (returns only the evidence depth required); across **6 person-understanding and persistent-memory benchmarks × 3 answer backbones**, ranks 1st in **12 of 18 compact scores** and top-two in **16 of 18**; ablations show hierarchy improves abstract person understanding (KnowMe), support-path retrieval improves preference alignment (RealPref) under a fixed context budget | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04780 | status: open ; first 2026 design that explicitly models *schema formation* (evidence → claims) as the unit of persistent user understanding rather than retrieval
- **2026-06-04** [arxiv:2606.04536] CLAIM: TMEM — **fast-weight parametric memory** that absorbs distilled supervision into **LoRA weights via lightweight online updates within a single episode**; action policy becomes a function of adapted parameters, making the *extraction policy* directly optimizable via RL; proposes SVD-based LoRA initialization for faster convergence; beats summary- and retrieval-based baselines on LoCoMo / LongMemEval-S / multi-objective search / CL-Bench across model scales | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04536 | status: open ; concrete inference-time realization of [arxiv:2606.03979] Behrouz/Mirrokni "Sleep" paradigm; status: extends-by [arxiv:2606.03979]
- **2026-06-04** [arxiv:2606.04660] CLAIM: LifeSide — **lifelong digital companion benchmark** with multi-session Memory-Emotion-Environment loops; users modeled as **persistent worlds with layered profiles and event trajectories**; multi-agent simulation projects environmental dynamics into dialogue while *preserving the gap between latent thoughts and observable expressions*; **2,000 personas × 111,000 tasks** across memory tracking, user understanding, privacy boundaries, emotional companionship; finding: **models that saturate current memory benchmarks fail to sustain accurate user understanding or true companionship over long horizons** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04660 | status: open ; implicit refutation of LoCoMo/LongMemEval as adequate proxies for companion-product quality
- **2026-06-04** [arxiv:2606.04628] CLAIM: RAMPART (Tomczak) — **compile-time memory model + pure in-RAM block registry** with five composable runtime ops (promote, gate, write, evict, rollback) at zero prompt-token cost; concrete position-effect numbers benchmarked on **Qwen3-8B Q4 / Qwen2.5-7B / Llama-3.1-8B / Mistral-7B-v0.3 / Qwen3-14B**: (a) task success drops at **block position ~7** when task follows registry / **~12** when it precedes; (b) **grouping critical blocks with neighbors recovers tens of pp** in previously-failing positions; (c) **relevance gating = 67.8% prompt-cost reduction while recovering 83% of promoted-condition performance**; (d) **schema eviction = 0% spurious schema invocations vs 100% with schema present** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04628 | status: open ; one of the few 2026 papers benchmarking memory mechanics specifically on local-sized Q4 quantized models; reproducible position-engineering playbook
- **2026-06-04** [arxiv:2606.05080] CLAIM: AutoLab — long-horizon engineering benchmark, **36 expert-curated tasks across 4 domains** (system optimization, puzzles, model development, CUDA kernel optimization), tested against **17 frontier models**; finding: **the dominant predictor of success is persistence in repeatedly benchmarking, editing, and incorporating empirical feedback — not initial attempt quality**; **Claude Opus 4.6 performs strongest**; most proprietary models terminate early or make minimal progress | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05080 | status: open ; status: confirms-direction-of [arxiv:2606.03461] Terminal-Lego (2026-06-03) — both find the *interaction structure / harness loop*, not standalone model quality, is the dominant lever for long-horizon work, now across two independent substrates (CLI / engineering)
- **2026-06-04** [arxiv:2606.05158] CLAIM: StreamMA — **streams reasoning *steps* (not whole completions) to downstream agents the instant they're generated**; cuts latency *and* improves quality because **early reasoning steps are empirically more reliable than later ones**, so downstream agents react to the strongest signal first; **+7.3pp average across 8 benchmarks × Chain/Tree/Graph topologies × {Claude Opus 4.6, GPT-5.4}; +22.4pp max on HMMT 2026 with Opus-4.6-high**; introduces a **step-level scaling law** = more steps per agent improves both effectiveness and efficiency, *orthogonal* to scaling agent count | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05158 | status: open ; direct redesign-prescription for MCP / Claude-Code-style sub-agent transports — stream the head, don't wait for the tail
- **2026-06-04** [anthropic-news-2026-06-03-ai-enabled-cyber-threats-mitre-attack] CLAIM: Anthropic Frontier Red Team — one-year MITRE ATT&CK mapping of **832 banned accounts** (March 2025 → March 2026); **67.3% used AI for malware development, 6.5% for lateral movement**; AI-assisted account discovery **+8.9%**, AI-assisted phishing **−8.6%** (i.e., AI shifting into later, more complex op phases); risk-tier headcount **33% (H1) → 56% (H2)** at medium-or-higher (~1.7×); **skilled vs unskilled actors barely differentiable by technique count (20 vs 16)**; **platform choice (Claude Code / API / chat) does NOT correlate with risk level**; **MITRE ATT&CK is admitted to be inadequate** for AI-orchestrated attack chains and autonomous agent behaviors | source: https://www.anthropic.com/news/AI-enabled-cyber-threats-mitre-attack | status: open ; first frontier-lab one-year retrospective with framework-level criticism

- **2026-06-03** [arxiv:2606.03979] CLAIM: Behrouz / Hashemi / Mirrokni (Google — Titans / Lightning Attention lineage) propose a **Sleep paradigm** for parameter-layer memory consolidation: (1) **Knowledge Seeding** = upward distillation combining on-policy distillation with RL-based imitation learning, taking smaller-self memories and distilling them into a larger network while preserving knowledge; (2) **Dreaming** = RL self-curriculum that generates synthetic data to rehearse new knowledge and refine existing capabilities without human supervision; reported gains on long-horizon, continual learning, knowledge incorporation, and few-shot generalization tasks (magnitude numbers PDF-only — abstract is mechanism-only) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03979 | status: open ; opens new sub-axis "memory-layer-of-operation = parameters" complementing the external-context-store cluster (TaskMem / AdaCoM / UCE / eMoT) — the two layers compose rather than compete; directionality (smaller-self → larger-student) is operationally the correct shape for small-local + frontier-remote companion-app architectures

- **2026-06-03** [arxiv:2606.03657] CLAIM: NovelAPIBench — fully automated dynamic benchmark for novel-API tool use; **~1.9K tasks × 4 base models × 5 domains**, **6 diagnostic categories** for failures; key findings: (i) **usage examples are strongest standalone signal**, signatures + (mechanisms or examples) is best two-component setting; (ii) **adding more context, especially source code, can hurt** by increasing import-path errors; (iii) **parametric adaptation does not replace retrieval** — fine-tuning teaches procedural integration (transfers to held-out libraries) but external knowledge must still be retrieved | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03657 | status: open ; sharpens MCP-server-design playbook (usage-examples > signatures in tool descriptions); finding (iii) structurally aligns with [arxiv:2605.30785] AdaCoM frozen-actor + learned-external-manager split — fine-tune the procedural integrator, retrieve the volatile content
- **2026-06-03** [arxiv:2606.03467] CLAIM: StepFinder (Peking U) — lightweight step-level failure attribution **without LLM inference in the attribution loop**; LLM is used only once at feature-construction time to encode logs into temporal semantic sequences, then a parameter-efficient temporal-modeling + attention module captures sequential evolution and cross-step dependencies; refined via multi-scale differences + position bias; **on Who&When benchmark: outperforms LLM-based methods on step-level attribution with 79% inference-time reduction vs. fastest LLM-based baseline, zero text-generation overhead** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03467 | status: open ; confirms-and-extends-with-runtime-feasibility the failure-attribution cluster ([arxiv:2606.02060] DRIFT claim-centric semantic audit / [arxiv:2605.29087] Trace-Answer Dissociation answer-vs-trace probability decorrelation) — cluster has bifurcated into *offline expensive deep-audit* (DRIFT) and *online cheap continuous monitor* (StepFinder); open empirical question: head-to-head DRIFT vs. StepFinder on the same trajectory corpus

- **2026-06-03** [arxiv:2606.03841] CLAIM: EvoDS (USAIL / HKUST, KDD 2026) — self-evolving autonomous data science agent; two coupled mechanisms (Autonomous Skill Acquisition with hierarchical organization reducing tool-selection error + Adaptive Context Compression aligned with information bottleneck principle); two-stage multi-agent training scheme; **+28.9% average across four data-science benchmarks over SOTA open-source DS agents** with "eliminated token failures" (overflow/truncation) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03841 | status: open ; first published agent-memory paper to *theoretically anchor* its compression objective in the information-bottleneck principle (vs. AdaCoM's RL-only opaque-reward formulation) — testable against [arxiv:2605.30785] AdaCoM on the same benchmarks; +28.9% reported on training distribution, [arxiv:2605.30434] LongDS-Bench (best frontier 48.45%, ~47-pt late-turn collapse) would be the harder transfer test

- **2026-06-03** [anthropic-news-2026-06-02-expanding-project-glasswing] CLAIM: Anthropic expanding Project Glasswing from 50 to ~150 organizations across 15+ countries (critical infrastructure: power, water, healthcare, communications, hardware — partners could affect 100M+ people on attack); **>10,000 high/critical-severity flaws found by initial 50 partners using Claude Mythos Preview**; Mythos Preview supports patch writing, pentesting, threat detection/response automation, memory-safe-language migration; also released **Claude Security** public product (Claude Opus 4.8) for codebase scanning + patch suggestions | source: https://www.anthropic.com/news/expanding-project-glasswing | status: open ; **second public reference to "Claude Mythos Preview" model handle** (first: [anthropic-research-2026-04-29-biomysterybench] May 4 — Mythos 30% on 23 human-difficult tasks vs. Opus 4.6 ~24%); now production-deployed in two distinct domains (bioinformatics, security) — strong evidence Mythos is a *domain-specialized model line*, not single-eval ad-hoc fine-tune
