- **2026-05-23** [anthropic-research-2026-05-22-glasswing-initial-update] CLAIM: Project Glasswing month-one results — **10,000+ high/critical vulnerabilities** found by Claude-class models across ~50 partners; Cloudflare 2,000 bugs / 400 high-critical; Mozilla 271 in Firefox 150 (10× prior testing); open-source scanning **6,202 high-critical across 1,000+ projects at 90.6% true-positive rate**; **75 of 530 disclosed bugs patched (patch pipeline is the new bottleneck)**; **Mythos Preview "solved both UK AISI cyber ranges end to end"** + led ExploitBench / ExploitGym | source: https://www.anthropic.com/research/glasswing-initial-update | status: open ; **second public Mythos Preview reference** after [anthropic-research-2026-04-29-biomysterybench] (30% on 23 human-difficult tasks); patch-bottleneck framing aligns with [microsoft-research-2026-05-15-delegation-followup] "Python workflows degrade <1% on average" — production durability is increasingly about the artifact channel, not the model
- **2026-05-23** [arxiv:2605.21850] CLAIM: ACC (Agent Context Compilation, Feng Zhao cluster — likely USTC / Shanghai AI Lab) compiles agent trajectories from search / SWE / DB agents into long-context QA pairs (question + tool responses + observations across many turns) and trains the model to answer **without** invoking tools, supervising long-context reasoning over distant evidence segments; **Qwen3-30B-A3B with ACC reaches 68.3 MRCR (+18.1) and 77.5 GraphWalks (+7.6), comparable to Qwen3-235B-A22B**, preserving GPQA / MMLU-Pro / AIME / IFEval; mechanism analysis shows task-adaptive attention restructuring + expert specialization emerge | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21850 | status: open ; **largest publicly-reported open-30B long-context gain of May 2026 cluster (+18.1 MRCR)**; complements [arxiv:2605.04050] LCM (LCM compresses at inference, ACC distills cross-turn evidence into weights at training) — same axis, opposite ends; local-fit on M5 Max via existing Qwen3-30B-A3B baseline
- **2026-05-23** [arxiv:2605.22177] CLAIM: Maestro (Jianhua Tao cluster — Tsinghua / CAS) trains a **4B RL policy** to orchestrate ensembles of **frozen** expert models + two-tier skill library (when to invoke, which model-skill pair, when to terminate); **70.1% avg across 10 multimodal benchmarks vs. GPT-5 69.3% / Gemini-2.5-Pro 68.7%**; 59.5% with out-of-domain experts | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22177 | status: open ; **first open multimodal-benchmark result where a 4B orchestrator over frozen experts beats GPT-5**; adds frozen-expert-ensemble design point to orchestration cluster with [arxiv:2605.05007] Uno-Orchestra, [arxiv:2605.15040] Orchard; pair-compare with [arxiv:2605.22502] Subterranean Agents (opposite halves of "cheap orchestration": Maestro = small router atop frozen experts vs. Subterranean = distill workflow into worker weights)
- **2026-05-23** [arxiv:2605.22502] CLAIM: "Subterranean agents" — Simon Dennis et al. compile orchestration procedures into small fine-tuned model weights rather than external orchestrator chains; tested on **travel booking (14 nodes)**, **Zoom support (14 nodes, product-specific knowledge)**, **insurance claims (55 nodes / 6 decision hubs)**; **near-frontier quality at 2 orders of magnitude lower cost** vs. frontier-model orchestration baselines | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22502 | status: open ; product-side analogue is [microsoft-research-2026-05-21-magenticlite-fara15] MagenticBrain (Qwen3-14B fine-tune as planner/coder/delegator) — three independent landings in 48h converging on "distill agent logic into smaller weights"; opposite design choice from [arxiv:2605.22177] Maestro (route over frozen experts vs. compile workflow into worker)
- **2026-05-23** [arxiv:2605.22148] CLAIM: Ratchet — minimal hygiene recipe for self-evolving frozen-LLM agents around a natural-language skill library: **outcome-driven retirement + bounded active-cap + meta-skill authoring guidance + pattern canonicalisation**; **MBPP+ hard-100 Pass@1 0.258 ± 0.047 baseline → 0.584 rolling mean / 0.658 ± 0.042 peak (Claude Opus 4.7, 100 rounds × 3 seeds)**; rolling-mean lift **+0.328 ± 0.018 vs. +0.002 ± 0.005 no-skill control**; **+0.22 peak on SWE-bench Verified over 20 rounds**; ablations: retirement + meta-skill authoring load-bearing, explicit deduplication subsumed | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22148 | status: open ; sharpens [arxiv:2605.06614] SkillOS (curator-executor split) and [arxiv:2605.18401] SkillsVote (evidence-gating) along the *retirement* axis — **open empirical question whether outcome-driven retirement closes the [arxiv:2605.12978] consolidation-decay gap** (54% of LLM-solved ARC-AGI tasks re-fail after consolidation)
- **2026-05-23** [arxiv:2605.22154] CLAIM: IdleSpec (KAIST + Amazon AGI) speculatively plans (and pre-executes where safe) during the tool-wait window of agentic LLMs; rolls back if observations contradict speculation; scales across varying compute budgets per tool call; **+5.1 pp GAIA/FRAMES avg with Gemini-2.5-Flash (55.6%)**, **up to +9.1 pp MLE-Bench Any Medal** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22154 | status: open ; direct composition partner to [arxiv:2605.15077] AsyncFC (concurrency-via-symbolic-futures) — AsyncFC decouples decode from execution, IdleSpec uses the resulting idle window for useful inference-time-compute work; pairs with [arxiv:2605.20630] IBM MCP-pipeline caching as harness-level latency optimization for MCP workloads
- **2026-05-23** [arxiv:2605.22106] CLAIM: ArborKV — structure-aware KV cache for tree-of-thoughts reasoning: tree-aware allocation policy + lightweight value estimator drive token-extractive eviction with lazy rehydration for branch back-tracking; **~4× peak KV memory reduction with near-full-retention accuracy**, enabling larger search configurations at fixed hardware | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22106 | status: open ; KV codec specifically for tree-shaped inference-time-compute (OpenDeepThink Bradley-Terry / MAP / InsightReplay); composes orthogonally with [arxiv:2605.19660] OScaR (per-token codec) and [arxiv:2605.18643] ZEDA (active-expert reduction) on the M5 Max long-context-inference budget
- **2026-05-23** [arxiv:2605.22535] CLAIM: TerminalWorld (UCL/Meta/Tsinghua — Peter O'Hearn, Earl T. Barr, Mark Harman, Federica Sarro) — reverse-engineers benchmark from **80,870 real terminal recordings** → 1,530 validated tasks across 18 categories / 1,280 unique commands, 200-task Verified subset; **max pass rate 62.5%** across 8 frontier models × 6 agents; **Pearson r = 0.20 vs. Terminal-Bench** (the two benchmarks measure different things) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22535 | status: open ; second "real recordings, not synthetic sandboxes" benchmark in 2 weeks after [arxiv:2605.10912] WildClawBench (62.2% Claude Opus 4.7 ceiling); r=0.20 means existing Terminal-Bench-based model rankings need re-validation
- **2026-05-23** [hf-blog-2026-05-23-nvidia-nemotron-labs-diffusion] CLAIM: NVIDIA Nemotron-Labs Diffusion — open diffusion language models at 3B / 8B / 14B (text) + 8B VLM, base + instruction-tuned variants; generates 32-token blocks via parallel denoising; three modes in one model (AR / diffusion / self-speculation = diffusion drafts + AR verification); **8B +1.2% accuracy vs. Qwen3-8B; diffusion mode 2.6× faster than AR; self-speculation quadratic 6.4× faster (~865 tok/s on B200, ~4× AR baseline)**; fully open weights + Megatron Bridge training code + SGLang inference; 1.3T pretraining + 45B fine-tune tokens; commercially-friendly NVIDIA Nemotron Open Model License (text) | source: https://huggingface.co/blog/nvidia/nemotron-labs-diffusion | status: open ; first credible open diffusion LM at production scale; self-speculation pattern structurally aligned with [arxiv:2605.15077] AsyncFC and [arxiv:2605.22154] IdleSpec — three same-week orthogonal latency optimizations (one in decoder, two in harness)
- **2026-05-25** [blog-google-2026-05-19-gemini-3-5] CLAIM: Gemini 3.5 Flash — **Terminal-Bench 2.1 76.2%, MCP Atlas 83.6%, GDPval-AA 1656 Elo, CharXiv Reasoning 84.2%; "4× faster output tokens/s vs. other frontier models"**; claimed to outperform Gemini 3.1 Pro on coding/agentic benchmarks; bundled Antigravity harness with subagent deployment + Gemini Spark personal AI agent | source: https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/models-and-research/gemini-models/gemini-3-5/ | status: open ; vendor-self-reported, no side-by-side numbers vs. 3.1 Pro; pairs as `compare` against [microsoft-research-2026-05-21-magenticlite-fara15] Fara1.5-27B "more than 90%" Online-Mind2Web — both top-tier agentic-capability numbers on **different benchmarks**, no cross-evaluation published
- **2026-05-25** [arxiv:2605.23904] CLAIM: SkillOpt — treats skills as external state of frozen agent, applies text-space optimization with bounded add/delete/replace edits accepted only when held-out validation improves; stability primitives: textual learning-rate budget, rejected-edit buffer, epoch-wise slow/meta update; **52 configurations: 6 benchmarks × 7 target models × 3 execution harnesses**; gains on GPT-5.5: **+23.5 pts direct chat, +24.8 pts Codex agentic loop, +19.1 pts Claude Code**; outperforms human-authored skills, Trace2Skill, TextGrad, GEPA, EvoSkill across all 52 configs; **optimized skills transfer across models/harnesses without re-optimization** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23904 | status: open ; sharpens [arxiv:2605.22148] Ratchet (outcome-driven retirement → held-out-validation gating), [arxiv:2605.18401] SkillsVote (Post-Execution gating), [arxiv:2605.06614] SkillOS (curator-executor split) along the *gradient-style optimization* axis — open empirical question whether the four converge on a single canonical implementation
- **2026-05-25** [arxiv:2605.23899] CLAIM: From Raw Experience to Skill Consumption — model-generated skills are beneficial on average but exhibit **non-trivial negative transfer**; a model can be a strong extractor and weak consumer or vice versa (roles don't co-vary); skill utility is **independent of model scale or baseline task strength**; proposed meta-skill guides extraction toward utility-tied features, consistently improves skill quality and reduces negative transfer across 5 agentic task domains | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23899 | status: open ; companion paper to [arxiv:2605.23904] SkillOpt (same author cluster); converges with [arxiv:2605.22148] Ratchet ablation (meta-skill authoring guidance load-bearing) — **three independent recent works name "meta-skill that guides skill writing" as the load-bearing primitive**
- **2026-05-25** [arxiv:2605.23296] CLAIM: Parallel Context Compaction — sequential LLM-based summarization stalls agent inference tens-of-seconds with unpredictable output volume; parallel compaction processes summarization concurrently, gives operators fine-grained predictable control over summary volume; **evaluated across 8B–120B dense + MoE backbones on HotpotQA / LoCoMo; at matched decoding volume, reduces end-to-end wall time and improves compaction throughput vs. sequential baseline** (accuracy preserved, not increased — systems wins) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23296 | status: open ; serving-system axis on the harness/training/serving triangle alongside [arxiv:2605.04050] LCM (inference-time hierarchical compression) and [arxiv:2605.21850] ACC (training-time trajectory distillation)
- **2026-05-25** [arxiv:2605.23723] CLAIM: MemAudit — defense against MINJA query-only memory-injection attack via (a) counterfactual memory-influence score (causal contribution of each memory to harmful outputs via ablation) + (b) memory-consistency graph (structural anomaly detection on cross-memory relations); **QA-agent attack success 70% → 0%; reasoning-agent (RAP) attack success 83.3% → 0%** under post-hoc auditing | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23723 | status: open ; cleanest "near-perfect defense" numbers in the agent-memory ledger to date; counterfactual-influence-score mechanism family aligns with [anthropic-research-2026-05-07-natural-language-autoencoders] (different mechanism — NLAs decode activations, MemAudit ablates memory entries — same audit epistemics)
- **2026-05-25** [arxiv:2605.23311] CLAIM: DART — modular runtime for semantic recoverability in structured tool agents under partial failures; core observation "controller legality does not imply semantic validity"; localizes failed instance, certifies semantically-recoverable boundaries, aligns checkpoints, selects admissible restore points while preserving downstream work under dependency + effect constraints; **recovers all commitment-sensitive cases where baseline local recovery fails**; five-domain safety audit shows zero unsafe admitted rollbacks; LangGraph external validation (qualitative coverage result, no quantitative recovery-delta numbers reported) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23311 | status: open ; recovery-path counterpart to [arxiv:2605.15077] AsyncFC forward-path concurrency primitive
- **2026-05-25** [arxiv:2605.23590] CLAIM: Co-ReAct — injects rubric at each ReAct decision step to guide next Reason-or-Act choice; rubric generator trained via **GRPO with list-wise Spearman rank-correlation reward against multi-judge expert consensus** (not binary preference); consistent improvements on DeepResearchBench + SQA-CS-V2 across open-source 8B/14B and closed-source baselines; code released on GitHub | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23590 | status: open ; per-step decision-quality counterpart to [arxiv:2605.18401] SkillsVote Post-Execution gating (skill-outcome quality)
- **2026-05-25** [arxiv:2605.22905] CLAIM: EVE-Agent — proposer-solver framework extension where (a) proposer outputs question + answer + verbatim evidence span, (b) evidence verifier scores spans by marginal accuracy gain when evidence is provided, (c) verifier signal feeds training loop rewarding genuinely useful supporting text; zero oracle answers / human labels / external annotations; "substantially improves evidence-grounded correctness over prior self-evolving search agents"; auditable curriculum with inspectable source spans | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22905 | status: open ; light on quantitative benchmarks — track for whether evidence-marginal-gain replicates as portable training signal; complements [arxiv:2605.23723] MemAudit (audits existing memory post-hoc) by preventing new memory/skill entries without evidence backing

- **2026-05-26** [arxiv:2605.23986] CLAIM: MemForest — reformulates agent-memory write path as "write-efficient temporal data management"; introduces MemTree (hierarchical temporal index organising memory as time-ordered trees rather than flat global summaries) with parallel chunk extraction + targeted per-node updates replacing full-state rewrites; **79.8% pass@1 on LongMemEval-S (top among stateful baselines); ~6× higher memory construction throughput vs. EverMemOS**; evaluated on LongMemEval-S + LoCoMo | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23986 | status: open ; database-systems framing for agent-memory writes — first paper to treat write-amplification as the bottleneck; structurally same hierarchical-tree shape as [arxiv:2605.04050] LCM but applied to persistent-storage layout instead of inference-time summarisation
- **2026-05-26** [arxiv:2605.25430] CLAIM: CODESKILL — treats skill extraction + maintenance as RL-trained management policy distilling coding-agent trajectories into reusable procedural skills; trained with dense rubric reward + sparse execution outcome from **frozen** downstream agent; **+9.69% avg pass-rate vs. no-skill baselines; +4.01% vs. strongest prompt-/memory-based baseline; stable skill-bank size**; evaluated on EnvBench + SWE-Bench Verified + Terminal-Bench 2 | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25430 | status: open ; RL-policy counterpart to [arxiv:2605.23904] SkillOpt (text-space optimization) and [arxiv:2605.22148] Ratchet (outcome-driven retirement) — three independent ways to **learn** skill curation now all with code-agent benchmark numbers; open empirical question which dominates head-to-head
- **2026-05-26** [arxiv:2605.25971] CLAIM: ProAct — uses inter-interaction (user-wait) idle window to anticipate user needs by analysing evolving dialogue history + persistent memory and pre-acquiring information; **−14.8% interaction turns, −11.7% user effort, −28.1% hallucination rate, SOTA reflective accuracy on MemBench** across new ProActEval benchmark (200 scenarios × 40 domains); code on GitHub | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25971 | status: open ; user-wait counterpart to [arxiv:2605.22154] IdleSpec (tool-wait idle window) — two halves of "idle-time inference-time-compute" now both with shipped code; the −28.1% hallucination number is the operationally load-bearing result
- **2026-05-26** [arxiv:2605.24218] CLAIM: QUEST — open-source 2B–35B research-agent family trained on **8,000 fully synthetic tasks** via unified rubric-tree data-synthesis pipeline with verifiable rewards (no human annotation); evaluated across 8 deep-research benchmarks; **"approaches or surpasses frontier closed-source agents," best overall performance among recent open-weight agents"** (vendor-style framing without published side-by-side numbers in abstract) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24218 | status: open ; 8K-task data efficiency is the headline claim to verify; uses **rubric** primitive aligned with [arxiv:2605.23590] Co-ReAct and [arxiv:2605.25430] CODESKILL — three independent landings on rubric-grounded reward in two weeks

- **2026-05-26** [arxiv:2605.26047] CLAIM: Retrying vs Resampling in AI Control (Lucassen + Kaufman, Redwood-cluster) — retrying (blocking + retry) leaks monitor feedback to the model; resampling (drawing multiple samples) does not; **5 samples per step raises safety 61% → 71% at 0.3% audit budget at no usefulness cost** on BashArena (Claude Opus 4.6 + MiMo-V2-Flash); selective resampling recovers 6.2 pp using +10% samples; **maximum-suspicion auditing outperforms minimum-based — explicitly contradicting Ctrl-Z**; executing least-suspicious sample gives only +3.9 pp (overlapping CIs) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26047 | status: open ; **direct contradiction with Ctrl-Z prior work** on auditing-aggregation direction — needs third independent reproduction to break the tie
- **2026-05-26** [arxiv:2605.25624] CLAIM: CUA-Gym — Generator + Discriminator agents co-generate task instructions + environment states + deterministic reward functions; **32,112 verified training tuples across 110 environments**; LLM majority voting + agent rollouts for QA; **CUA-Gym-A3B 62.1% OSWorld-Verified; CUA-Gym-A17B 72.6%**; improvements on held-out WebArena; "smooth" scaling with data + diversity; pipeline + dataset + environments + models planned open-release | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25624 | status: open ; synthetic-verifiable-reward recipe same family as [arxiv:2605.18703] EnvFactory (tool use) and [arxiv:2605.24218] QUEST (deep research); A3B variant is M5 Max-local-fit; pairs as `compare` with [microsoft-research-2026-05-21-magenticlite-fara15] Fara1.5-27B >90% Online-Mind2Web (different benchmarks, no published cross-eval)
- **2026-05-26** [hf-blog-2026-05-25-agent-glossary] CLAIM: HF Agent Glossary (Paniego + Roy Gosthipaty) — defines `Agent = Model + Harness + Scaffolding`; **model** stateless LLM; **scaffolding** behaviour-defining (prompts, tool descriptions, context management, parsing); **harness** execution (calls model, handles tool calls, decides when to stop); skills (reusable knowledge packages) distinct from tools (actions) and sub-agents (independent reasoning); RL pipeline = environment → rollout → trainer → reward, rewards = **rubrics** (weighted dimensions, TRL GRPOTrainer / OpenEnv / Verifiers); claim: **same model + different harness = different product** (Claude Code vs. Cursor cited) | source: https://huggingface.co/blog/agent-glossary | status: open ; operational counterpart + same-day pair to [arxiv:2605.26112] Scaling the Harness — naming moment for harness-as-primitive; rubric-as-reward-unit aligns with [arxiv:2605.23590] Co-ReAct, [arxiv:2605.24218] QUEST, [arxiv:2605.25430] CODESKILL — four-paper convergence on rubric as the unit of training signal in 2026-era agent work
- **2026-05-27** [arxiv:2605.26494] CLAIM: MiniMax-M2 Series — 229.9B-total / 9.8B-active (4.3% activation) MoE trained via agent-driven verifiable-trajectory data pipeline + specialized RL infrastructure; **frontier-tier performance claimed on agentic coding, deep search, office tasks, reasoning**; M2.7 checkpoint reportedly self-debugs ("self-evolution"); MIT-licensed weights live on HuggingFace `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M2`/`M2.7`; tech report has 204+ contributors, 35 pages, 10 figures | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26494 | status: open ; no head-to-head numbers in abstract — verifiability of frontier-tier claim pending independent reproduction; model line itself has been public since 2025-10
- **2026-05-27** [arxiv:2605.27366] CLAIM: MUSE-Autoskill — unified skill lifecycle (creation / memory / management / evaluation) treating skills as **long-lived, experience-aware, testable assets** with skill-level memory accumulating across tasks; unit tests + runtime feedback drive selection and refinement; evaluated on SkillsBench; "improves task success, efficiency, reuse, and cross-agent transfer" vs static skill approaches (no quantitative deltas in abstract); 30 pages, working in progress | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27366 | status: open ; 7th distinct skill-lifecycle paper in 30 days alongside [arxiv:2605.06614] SkillOS, [arxiv:2605.25430] CODESKILL, [arxiv:2605.23904] SkillOpt, [arxiv:2605.18401] SkillsVote, [arxiv:2605.23899] model-generated skill systematic study, [arxiv:2605.05413] Constant-Context Skill Learning — field consensus emerging on `skill = Markdown + test harness + experience log`
- **2026-05-27** [arxiv:2605.26114] CLAIM: MobileGym — browser-based mobile-app simulator with **416 parameterized tasks (256 test / 160 train) across 28 apps**, structured-JSON state for deterministic judging, ~400 MB / instance, ~3 s cold start, hundreds of parallel instances; GRPO on Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct **+12.8 pp on test set**; **95.1% of simulation-side training gains transfer to real devices on a 59-task subset**; declarative task definition + structured AnswerSheet protocol | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26114 | status: open ; sim-to-real-transfer number is the key signal — makes simulator-based RL training defensible against real-device farms; sibling to [arxiv:2605.25624] CUA-Gym (synthetic-verifiable computer-use training) — both extend the [arxiv:2605.18703] EnvFactory synthetic-environment recipe
- **2026-05-27** [arxiv:2605.24219] CLAIM: Trajel (IBM Research) — multi-agent industrial-workflow hallucination taxonomy with 5 categories (factual / referential / logical / procedural / scope-based); **~50% of hallucinated trajectories involve multiple hallucination types simultaneously**; detectors with high binary accuracy still misclassify subtle types; **trajectory-aware detection substantially outperforms post-hoc final-answer verification** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24219 | status: open ; convergent with [arxiv:2605.06635] AST-citation factuality decay and [arxiv:2605.12925] AgentLens lucky-pass — three papers now argue final-answer eval is structurally insufficient for multi-step agents; 50%-overlap finding means single-classifier hallucination detectors will systematically under-report
- **2026-05-27** [arxiv:2605.27328] CLAIM: HarnessMutation (Garralda-Barrio, single-author) — formalizes agent-generated code artifacts as **persistent runtime capabilities** rather than disposable outputs; governance loop with explicit validation, traceability, evaluation, rollback constraints, so self-modification stays bounded and observable; reference impl at github.com/mgarralda/governed-runtime (CC BY-NC-SA) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27328 | status: open ; design-doc-class paper (no benchmarks); operationalizes the self-evolving-agent hygiene line ([arxiv:2605.22148] Ratchet, [arxiv:2605.18401] SkillsVote, [arxiv:2605.26112] Scaling-the-Harness) into a concrete validate→rollback contract
- **2026-05-27** [arxiv:2605.26178] CLAIM: ATOM — nucleus-electron hierarchy: stable offline-learned backbone (nucleus) + dynamically activated query-specific agents (electrons), trained via task-driven RL generating collaboration graphs from complexity-aware budgeting; **claimed SOTA across 6 benchmarks; up to 30% token-efficiency improvement vs strong baselines** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26178 | status: open ; sibling to [arxiv:2605.05007] Uno-Orchestra (learns full decomposition + worker + budget), [arxiv:2605.22177] Maestro (hierarchical model-skill ensembles), [arxiv:2605.06623] MASPO (multi-agent prompt optimization) — four-paper convergence on budget-/complexity-conditioned orchestration as the dominant pattern over flat agent graphs
- **2026-05-27** [arxiv:2605.26952] CLAIM: AKBE — during agentic RL, dual-path rollouts (with-tool / no-tool) on the same query generate targeted signal for when parametric knowledge suffices vs when an external tool is needed; **+1.85 avg accuracy across 7 QA benchmarks, 18% fewer tool calls, 25% higher tool productivity**; no accuracy-efficiency trade-off; plug-and-play across RL algorithms; code released | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26952 | status: open ; **first training-time positive recipe** for the knowing-doing gap surfaced by [arxiv:2605.14038] Model-Adaptive Tool Necessity, [arxiv:2605.00737] hidden-state tool-need estimators, [arxiv:2605.00136] tool-use tax — orthogonal to inference-time mitigations ([arxiv:2605.07112] Switchcraft router, [arxiv:2605.07990] tool-call steering vectors)
- **2026-05-27** [arxiv:2605.27081] CLAIM: ReMoE (ICML 2026) — router fine-tuning to bias toward **recently selected experts**, producing temporally stable routing that matches cache locality constraints; tested on DeepSeek + Qwen; **26% expert-reuse improvement; 8.4% throughput gain on vLLM GPU-CPU offload; 1.77–1.99× decode speedup on Jetson Orin NX with llama.cpp; 43.6–49.8% TPOT reduction**; downstream task quality preserved; code released | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27081 | status: open ; pattern portable to M5 Max (MLX/llama.cpp) for >64 GB MoE offload; relates-to [arxiv:2605.18643] ZEDA (skip half experts via self-distillation) — orthogonal axis (skip-experts at inference vs cache-aware routing for offload)
- **2026-05-28** [arxiv:2605.28732] CLAIM: MemTrace (Zhejiang Univ / zjunlp, Ningyu Zhang group) — transforms memory pipelines into **executable memory evolution graphs** for fine-grained tracing; MemTraceBench derived from Long-Context / RAG / Mem0 / EverMemOS; automated attribution traces operation subgraphs to root-cause failures; finds **memory failures are systematic and operation-level (not random)**; attribution-guided auto fault-correction yields **up to +7.62%** end-task; code at github.com/zjunlp/MemTrace | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28732 | status: open ; **first attribution/repair tool** over the 11+ memory-failure-mode catalogue (STALE / MEMOREPAIR / DeMem / MEME / LongMINT / consolidation-decay [arxiv:2605.12978]) — open question whether operation-level faults subsume the named taxonomy
- **2026-05-28** [arxiv:2605.28773] CLAIM: FluxMem (Zhejiang Univ, Ningyu Zhang / Huajun Chen — same group as MemTrace) — models memory as a **heterogeneous graph with continuously evolving connectivity**, refined in 3 stages (connection formation → feedback-driven refinement → consolidation); "repairs missing links, prunes interference, aligns abstraction granularity, distills recurrent successful trajectories"; claims **SOTA on LoCoMo, Mind2Web, GAIA** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28773 | status: open ; **tension-with [arxiv:2605.12978]** consolidation-decay (continuous LLM consolidation re-failed 54% of solved ARC-AGI tasks) — likely resolved like [arxiv:2605.13941] EvolveMem (evolve structure not content); neither paper runs the other's stress test

Abdi, Michel Galley, Zhikai Chen, Siheng Xiong, Xiaoqian Wang, Jing Gao — Microsoft Research + Purdue cluster) — replaces per-event LLM trigger from [arxiv:2605.25971] ProAct with a **220 MiB BF16 temporal-graph-learning (TGL) encoder** ingesting OS event-graph updates directly; one forward pass → per-event trigger probability + per-entity routing score; LLM call only at fluent-output stage when trigger fires; **+16.7 mean F1 (up to +46.0) across 14 backbones; 11.13 ms/event GPU, 13.99 ms/event consumer laptop; 4–7× GPU / 12–83× CPU speedup vs. LLM-as-trigger baselines** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30152 | status: open ; **direct compare with [arxiv:2605.25971] ProAct on idle-time triggering — dominates ProAct's LLM-as-trigger on both F1 and latency on the trigger sub-task, but does not test ProAct's downstream −28.1% hallucination claim**; pairs with [arxiv:2605.22154] IdleSpec as second instance of "LLM-on-every-idle-event is the wrong primitive" (IdleSpec: tool-wait window doesn't need full LLM call; this: user-wait window doesn't need full LLM call for trigger)

- **2026-05-30** [hf-blog-2026-05-27-delta-weight-sync] CLAIM: Delta Weight Sync in TRL (Dirhoussi, Gallouédec, Rasul, Tunstall, Beeching + 25 others, HF) — at typical RL learning rates (e.g., 3e-6) per-step weight updates are **smaller than bf16 precision threshold (|Δw| < |w|/256)**, so **~99% of bf16 weights are bit-identical between consecutive optimizer steps**; sparse safetensors deltas via Hub bucket (Xet content-defined chunking) replace NCCL broadcast; **per-step sparsity ~99% (min 98%) on Qwen2.5/Llama-3.2/Gemma-3; 1.2 GB → 20–35 MB on Qwen3-0.6B (~130× delta-size reduction); 1.1s inference pause on per-step sync**; **6 GB extrapolated delta for Llama-3.1-405B vs. 8s NCCL broadcast pause (100 GB/s cluster) → 2s pause + background upload** | source: https://huggingface.co/blog/delta-weight-sync | status: open ; first production-grade **disaggregated async-RL primitive across regions/clouds without NCCL**; released as `huggingface/trl#5417` with full Wordle proof-of-concept (trainer + vLLM Space + CPU environment Space, sync via Hub bucket); known limitations: two CPU bf16 snapshots, fixed anchor cadence, untested at multi-node FSDP2

- **2026-06-09** [anthropic-research-2026-06-08-agents-in-biology] CLAIM: Anthropic (with NCBI collaboration) — *Paving the Way for Agents in Biology*; introduces **VirBench** (120 realistic viral-sequence queries × 40 pathogens); raw frontier models without specialized tools span **16.9% → 91.3% mean accuracy** (Claude Sonnet 4, Claude Opus 4.7, Biomni OSS, Edison Analysis, GPT-5.2-pro, GPT-5.5); **same-query run-to-run variability is severe** (Claude Sonnet 4 returned 106 / 15 / 5 sequences on three identical runs vs. expected 266); **gget virus** deterministic NCBI retrieval layer lifts **every model to >90% accuracy, peak 99.7% (GPT-5.5)**, run-to-run variability largely eliminated; explicit conclusion: *adding the deterministic retrieval layer made model choice much less important* | source: https://www.anthropic.com/research/agents-in-biology | status: open ; pairs with [arxiv:2606.05806] ToolMaze ("fault tolerance scales 3.66× slower than baseline as model size grows") as two-angle convergence on *don't outscale, close at the tool boundary*; the same-query non-determinism is the failure mode that evades CI; open question whether the result generalises beyond NCBI/biology (SEC EDGAR / OpenAlex / agency-data endpoints)
- **2026-06-09** [arxiv:2606.09483] CLAIM: DCPM / *Memory Beyond Recall* (Fei / Song / Zheng / Yu) — dual-process cognitive memory for self-evolving LLM agents; hierarchy **raw inputs → atomic facts → diachronic belief trajectories → identity → domain schemas**; **System1** records belief updates inline (fast/sync), **System2** asynchronously identifies patterns and consolidates schemas (slow/off-path); headline numbers: **+5.20 improvement on PersonaMem-v2** where cross-session inference is rewarded; gains on LongMemEval implicit-reasoning tasks; strongest gains on tasks requiring understanding how users evolve over time; **minimal gains on span recall** (by design — architecture targets consolidation, not retrieval) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09483 | status: open ; first explicit System1/System2 split applied to agent memory; candidate architectural fix for the relational-discrimination gap documented by [arxiv:2606.05761] SubtleMemory; extends [arxiv:2606.06448] systems-level memory taxonomy and aligns with [arxiv:2606.04703] principle-vs-instance granularity finding (instance ≈ DCPM's belief-trajectory layer, principle ≈ DCPM's schema layer)
- **2026-06-09** [arxiv:2606.09498] CLAIM: Self-Harness (Zhang/Zhang/Li/Zhang/Chen/Zhang/Bai/Hu) — harness-level self-improvement framework; three-stage loop: **(1) identify model-specific failure patterns**, **(2) generate targeted harness modifications (not generic prompt edits)**, **(3) validate via regression testing before adoption**; **Terminal-Bench 2.0** results — **MiniMax M2.5 40.5 → 61.9%, Qwen3.5-35B-A3B 23.8 → 38.1%, GLM-5 42.9 → 57.1%**; key qualitative claim: does not just add generic instructions, turns model-specific weaknesses into concrete executable harness changes | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09498 | status: open ; operates at *harness* layer (distinct from [arxiv:2606.05922] RHO harness-optimisation, [arxiv:2606.06741] OpenSkill skill-bootstrapping, [arxiv:2606.07412] Socratic-SWE trace-distilled skills); first cross-model triangulation with double-digit gains across three base models on the same benchmark; pairs with [arxiv:2606.09748] DR-Eval (today) as positive-vs-negative comparison on *edit-surface design* — Self-Harness wins by constraining the edit surface, DR-Eval shows full-rewrite multi-turn regression

- **2026-06-09** [arxiv:2606.09748] CLAIM: *Multi-Turn Evaluation of Deep Research Agents Under Process-Level Feedback* (Sabharwal/Wang/Storkey/Pan, ICML 2026 SCALE workshop, Oral) — introduces **Research Gap Inference (RGI)** to identify strategic gaps in research processes; three findings: (1) **self-reflection produces negligible net improvement** — agents incorporate and regress on rubric criteria at nearly equal rates; (2) **single round of process-level feedback yields ~8–15 normalized points** improvement with **~35–40% incorporation rate**; (3) **multi-turn regression severe — agents regress on up to 24% of previously-satisfied rubric criteria when rewriting full reports**; explicit conclusion: "reliable multi-turn improvement remains out of reach" for evaluated architectures | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09748 | status: open ; sharpest published negative result against the implicit "iterative feedback monotonically improves" prior; the 24%-regression-on-prior-wins number is the decision-affecting datapoint for any rewrite-every-cycle loop; opposes [arxiv:2606.09498] Self-Harness (today) as edit-surface comparison — constrained-surface (Self-Harness) compounds gains, full-rewrite (this paper) decays them
- **2026-06-09** [hf-blog-2026-06-08-openenv-agentic-rl] CLAIM: OpenEnv (Hugging Face + Meta-PyTorch + ~20-org coordinating committee) — open interoperability layer for RL environments (terminals, browsers, code sandboxes); **client/server, Gymnasium-style API (`reset() / step() / state()`)**, transports over **HTTP, WebSocket, and MCP (Model Context Protocol)**, **Docker-packaged**; protocol layer only — does not define rewards or training loops; coordinating committee: Meta-PyTorch, Reflection, Unsloth, Modal, Prime Intellect, NVIDIA, Mercor, Fleet AI, Hugging Face; supporting orgs: PyTorch Foundation, vLLM, SkyRL (UC Berkeley), Lightning AI, Axolotl AI, Stanford Scaling Intelligence Lab, Scale AI, and others; repo at github.com/huggingface/OpenEnv | source: https://huggingface.co/blog/openenv-agentic-rl | status: open ; first time MCP is positioned as a first-class transport for RL environments (not just chat-time tool calls); broadest open-stack alignment in 2026 (Meta-PyTorch + NVIDIA + vLLM + Modal + Prime Intellect + Hugging Face on one protocol); open question on which trainers (TRL / Unsloth / vLLM-RL / SkyRL / Axolotl) ship a working OpenEnv driver in 90 days

- **2026-06-10** [arxiv:2606.10677] CLAIM: Infini Memory (Ji / Wu / Wang / Xia / Q. Li / Song-Wang / Ding / Z. Zhu / B. Li / Dai / Y. Wang) — long-term LLM agent memory as **maintainable topic-structured documents** (each topic = semantic unit collecting related evidence, preserving metadata, supporting fact revision), **staging buffer + periodic consolidation** for new observations, **iterative agentic retrieval** replacing single-step lookup; **64.7% on MemoryAgentBench**; ablations attribute gains to two complementary parts (topic-structured maintenance + iterative inspection) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10677 | status: open ; **second concrete write-side maintainable-memory design** after [arxiv:2605.23986] MemForest (database-systems write-amplification framing); composes with [arxiv:2605.28732] MemTrace (operation-level fault attribution) and [arxiv:2605.28773] FluxMem (heterogeneous-graph dynamics); structurally maps 1:1 onto Obsidian-vault / daily-inbox + nightly-consolidation pattern; representation-layer counterpart to today's [arxiv:2606.10532] ActiveMem (process-decoupling) and [arxiv:2606.10616] OSL-MR (retention-policy) — three orthogonal layers of the same memory-systems design space published the same day
- **2026-06-10** [arxiv:2606.10532] CLAIM: ActiveMem / *Distributed Active Memory for Long-Horizon LLM Reasoning* (Jiang / Duan / Guo / L. Pang / X. Sun / H. Shen) — **decouples memory from reasoning**: a high-level **Planner** reasons over distilled semantic gists while a **lightweight distributed memory system operates in parallel** to accumulate and consolidate gists; explicit cognitive analogy **prefrontal cortex (executive control) ↔ hippocampus (memory management)**; frames the "scaling reasoning trajectories ↔ context overload" trade-off as a *consequence of centralised memory organisation*, not inherent; reports **SoTA accuracy on BrowseComp-Plus and GAIA with significantly reduced overhead** (no headline percentages in abstract) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10532 | status: open ; process-decoupling answer to the same problem [arxiv:2606.10677] Infini Memory (rep layer) and [arxiv:2606.10616] OSL-MR (policy layer) attack from other angles; structurally implements the asynchronous-System2 path from yesterday's [arxiv:2606.09483] DCPM with explicit PFC/hippocampus framing; production-realisation = **two cooperating Claude Code instances + shared vault folder + heartbeat** (one for reasoning, one for memory consolidation)

Kang / Mingyang Liu / Shixiong Kai / Kaichao Liang / Tao Zhong / Mingxuan Yuan) — formalises memory retention as **constrained stochastic optimisation** with explicit budget feasibility, evidence utility, and **delayed costs (miss penalty, reacquisition delay, stale-information risk)**; enforces strict separation between **online-observable features** (deployment) and **offline-available supervision** (training); combines an evidence learner with a **Mixed-Score heuristic** that serves dually as deployable online-safe baseline + structured inductive prior; on **LOCOMO + LongMemEval consistently outperforms recency-based, Generative-Agents-style, and other heuristic baselines, particularly under tight memory budgets**; Mixed-Score prior improves precision while preserving recall; robust across cost configurations | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10616 | status: open ; **first formal observability-safe framing** of retention — most published "learned scoring" memory systems quietly condition on signals only available at training time and silently degrade in deployment; miss-penalty / reacquisition-delay framing is cleaner cost model than precision/recall for long-running companion-app memory; retention-policy layer of today's three-paper memory-architecture convergence (Infini Memory representation + ActiveMem process-decoupling + OSL-MR retention)
- **2026-06-10** [arxiv:2606.10933] CLAIM: *Frontier Coding Agents Use Metaprogramming to Adapt to Unfamiliar Programming Languages* (Sharma / Thorat / Chopra) — evaluates 6 contemporary coding agents on 4 esoteric languages (Brainfuck, Befunge-98, + 2 unnamed) with sequential file-edit / local-execute / hidden-test grading; finds **Claude Opus 4.6 and GPT-5.4 xhigh often avoid writing the target language directly** and instead **write Python programs that generate target-language code and debug those generators locally**; **forbidding metaprogramming causes large performance drops**; text guidance distilled from the strategy does not help weaker agents; **Opus-derived Python helper code (no solved benchmark programs, no hidden-test answers) sharply improves Sonnet 4.6 and GPT-5.4 mini** on the same problems; Haiku 4.5 remains low; more interpreter calls + output tokens amplify useful strategies in stronger agents but do not create them in weaker; **SWE-Bench Verified and Terminal-Bench 2.0 compress these capability differences into much narrower bands than esoteric-language evaluation reveals** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10933 | status: open ; **FLAGGED as benchmark-saturation counter-evidence** to the implicit framing of recent self-improvement papers ([arxiv:2606.09498] Self-Harness, [arxiv:2606.05922] RHO, [arxiv:2606.06741] OpenSkill) that use Terminal-Bench 2.0 / SWE-Bench Verified as discriminating signal — gains on those benchmarks *understate* underlying spread; "expensive-agent-derived Python helper lifts cheaper agent" is the *artifact-form* analog of skill distillation ([arxiv:2605.25430] CODESKILL, [arxiv:2605.23904] SkillOpt) but the artifact is plain code, not a curated skill bank

- **2026-06-10** [arxiv:2606.11052] CLAIM: Attention Amnesia (X. Zhou / B. Zhu / Y. Xu / Z. Li / Y. Chen / H. Wang / Z. Guo) — identifies failure mode in hybrid LLM architectures (**HypeNet, Jet-Nemotron** class): **standard CoT-SFT biases attention gradients toward short-range patterns, disrupting query-key projections responsible for long-range routing**; **HypeNet-9B on NIAH-S2@256K collapses 67.2% → 9.4%** post CoT-SFT (58-pt drop); proposed **QK-Restore** surgically recovers and even improves (**HypeNet-5B S3@256K: 65.4% → 76.4%**) while preserving CoT-SFT reasoning gains | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11052 | status: open ; **first published evidence** that the "fine-tune for reasoning, deploy at long context" recipe (default for sub-quadratic hybrid architectures) silently breaks long-range recall; defensive requirement: **benchmark NIAH-S2/S3 @ 256K before AND after any reasoning-distillation fine-tune on hybrid checkpoints**; **open empirical question** whether the bias-gradient-toward-short-range mechanism applies to attention-only Transformers under CoT-SFT — paper only tests hybrids

Li / Weizhi Fei / Xiangyuan Wang / Mengzhe Ruan / Hanxu Hou / Peisong Wang / Linqi Song / Shuang Qiu) — two-level KV cache budget allocator for long-CoT reasoning: **offline layer-wise allocation** captures "Reasoning Wave" architectural demand patterns + **online head-wise reallocation** dynamically shifts budget to information-rich attention heads during decoding; **training-free**, **plug-and-play with R-KV / SnapKV / Pyramid-RKV** eviction policies, minimal runtime overhead; evaluated on **MATH-500 + AIME 2024** with DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B / DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-14B / AceReason-14B; **greatest improvements at small budgets (128–512 tokens)** vs. uniform-budget baselines | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11164 | status: open ; **hierarchical-allocation axis** orthogonal to [arxiv:2605.19660] OScaR (per-token INT2 quantisation, 5.3× memory reduction) and [arxiv:2605.22106] ArborKV (tree-aware allocation, ~4× peak reduction) on the M5 Max 64 GB local-inference budget — three axes (token codec / structure-aware / layer×head importance) should stack on the same memory budget
- **2026-06-12** [deepmind-blog-2026-06-10-diffusiongemma] CLAIM: DiffusionGemma (Google DeepMind, June 10 2026, Apache 2.0) — open-weight **26B-total / 3.8B-active MoE text-diffusion LM** built on Gemma 4 backbone with novel diffusion head + **bi-directional attention**; generates **256 tokens in parallel per forward pass**; **1000+ tok/s on single H100, 700+ tok/s on RTX 5090**; fits in **~18 GB VRAM at NVFP4 4-bit**; **MLX/vLLM/HF Transformers supported at launch**, llama.cpp queued; Google explicitly acknowledges **lower overall quality than autoregressive Gemma 4** as the trade-off | source: https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/technology/developers-tools/diffusion-gemma-faster-text-generation/ | status: open ; **first frontier-lab text-diffusion LM shipped with first-party MLX support at launch** — direct M5 Max sweet-spot candidate at same active-param class as Qwen3-30B-A3B but with fundamentally different decoding loop; **open empirical question whether the 4× speedup transfers from H100/RTX-5090 to M5 Max Metal kernels** — published numbers are GPU-only, no M5 Max benchmark yet; right experiment is direct head-to-head vs Qwen3-30B-A3B on agent inner-loop workloads (JSON tool-call bursts, code infill, structured rewrites)
- **2026-06-12** [arxiv:2606.13681] CLAIM: EvoArena + EvoMem (Jundong Xu et al., 14 authors incl. Caiming Xiong, Bryan Hooi, Anh Tuan Luu — NTU + Salesforce AI Research + Singapore cluster) — benchmark models **environment change as sequences of progressive updates across terminal, software, social domains** + paired memory system tracking **memory evolution as structured update histories**; current agents avg **39.6% on EvoArena**; EvoMem yields **+1.5% EvoArena, +6.1% GAIA, +4.8% LoCoMo, +3.7% chain-level on consecutive evolutionary subtasks**; mechanistic analysis shows EvoMem improves "evidence capture, enabling better preservation of evolving environment states" | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13681 | status: open ; **first dedicated benchmark for "the world changed under your agent" axis** missing from HORMA/MemForest/MAGE/Infini Memory cluster (which all assume write-once-read-many); update-history primitive is **direct philosophical opposite of PROJECTMEM's append-only-immutable log** — both can be true depending on whether the supersedence event is preserved or destructive; open empirical question whether write-many-supersede-prior outperforms append-only when the underlying world is genuinely evolving
- **2026-06-12** [arxiv:2606.13663] CLAIM: HyperTool (Yaxin Du et al., SJTU + collaborators) — MCP-style **code-block tool-invocation primitive** that bundles existing MCP tool calls through their schemas + value manipulation + local intermediate-result passing into a single execution unit; names underlying problem **"execution-granularity mismatch"** where deterministic tool workflows consume excessive context exposing low-level dataflow to the reasoning trace; **Qwen3-32B 15.69 → 35.29% (+19.6pp) and Qwen3-8B 9.93 → 33.33% (+23.4pp) on MCP-Universe**; surpasses GPT-OSS and Kimi-k2.5 baselines on average accuracy; **schema-compatible with existing MCP servers** (no protocol break) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13663 | status: open ; **most directly portable MCP-stack artifact in recent months** — first named-and-reduced "MCP execution-granularity tax"; composes with [arxiv:2606.12320] Five-Plane governance (HyperTool reduces frequency of model-in-loop calls, Five-Plane controls which are allowed); +20pp deltas are unusually clean for a context-engineering primitive
- **2026-06-12** [arxiv:2606.13003] CLAIM: "The Illusion of Multi-Agent Advantage" (Prathyusha Jwalapuram et al., Salesforce AI Research + UBC + NTU) — systematic head-to-head shows **automatically-generated MAS consistently underperforms Chain-of-Thought + Self-Consistency despite up to 10× cost** across reasoning datasets + interactive multi-step workflows (BrowseComp-Plus); **expert-architected MAS still outperforms auto-MAS** on diagnostic synthetic dataset designed to isolate MAS advantage; frames as "architectural bloat prioritizing superficial complexity" + indicts existing eval frameworks for ignoring compute cost | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13003 | status: open ; **NOT a contradiction of expert-architected MAS positive results** (Arbor [arxiv:2606.11926] 2.5× Claude Code on research, MUSE-Autoskill [arxiv:2605.27366], MasReplay-T1) — the resolution is that **auto-MAS design pipelines** are the failed category, not multi-agent systems per se; actionable rule: any new agent in a system should beat CoT-SC at the same compute budget or it's decoration; **first sharp public counter to the auto-MAS-pipeline hype wave** of 2026
- **2026-06-12** [arxiv:2606.13662] CLAIM: EurekAgent ("Agent Environment Engineering is All You Need for Autonomous Scientific Discovery", Amy Xin et al., Tsinghua KEG) — position-plus-system arguing **bottleneck for autonomous scientific discovery shifts from prescribing workflows to designing environments** as agent capability rises; engineers environments along **4 dimensions — permissions (bounded execution), artifacts (filesystem/Git), budgets (exploration awareness), human oversight**; SOTA across math + kernel engineering + ML tasks; **new SOTA 26-circle packing result at ~$11 total API cost**; open-sourced | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13662 | status: open ; **executable counterpart to [arxiv:2606.12191] Agentic Environment Engineering survey from June 11** (survey identified 4 co-evolution pathways, EurekAgent demonstrates orchestration-centric one end-to-end on verifiable benchmark at hobbyist cost); $11 SOTA-on-verifiable-optimization is the calibration number — says well-designed environment + stock frontier model can match specialized methods on verifiable problems; structurally same long-lived-coordinator + scoped-executors + persistent-artifact pattern as Arbor [arxiv:2606.11926]
- **2026-06-12** [arxiv:2606.12908] CLAIM: SENTINEL (Ziyi Wang et al., NEU + collaborators) — RL training framework converting agent failures into targeted training tasks via **Controller–Proposer–Solver loop**: Controller mines failure patterns from unsuccessful trajectories, Proposer generates new executable tasks targeting those weaknesses, Solver trains on curated tasks; replaces fixed task distributions (which become uninformative as agent improves) with **failures-as-curriculum**; **Qwen3-4B-Thinking-2507 Pass@1 66.4 → 74.9% (+8.5pp) on Tau2-Bench Retail**; outperforms standard RL across Pass@k on general synthetic tasks | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12908 | status: open ; **failure-as-curriculum primitive generalizes beyond RL** to any agent self-improvement loop (failure log → propose-similar-task → run-and-grade → add to test suite); PROJECTMEM's append-only event log [arxiv:2606.12329] is natural input substrate; composes with HERO [arxiv:2606.11559] from June 11 (HERO converts each observation into turn-level diagnostic; SENTINEL converts trajectory-level failure into new task) — together they are the two halves of "learn from your own runs without privileged terminal rewards"
- **2026-06-12** [arxiv:2606.13115] CLAIM: G-Long (Minjun Choi et al., Sungkyunkwan University) — long-dialogue agent memory via **fine-tuned small LM doing structured triplet extraction + associative graph retrieval** + **attention-aware importance scoring** using T5-summarizer signals to identify salient info; **+9.8% response quality on MSC, 40.8% retrieval recall on LME**; substantially lower compute than baselines | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13115 | status: open ; the interesting thing is **small-fine-tuned-LM-as-memory-curator as separable tier** in the planner/executor/curator topology (distinct from both planner and executor); adds **associative-graph axis** to the multi-architecture memory thread (HORMA file-system tree, MemForest temporal tree, EvoMem update history, G-Long associative graph) — field is rapidly exploring orthogonal axes with **NO head-to-head head-to-head benchmark across the four**, becoming increasingly urgent gap
- **2026-06-12** [arxiv:2606.13177] CLAIM: MemRefine (Minjae Kim, Jinheon Baek, Soyeong Jeong, Sung Ju Hwang — KAIST) — memory-compaction framework that **uses similarity only to *propose* candidate entry pairs, then defers delete/merge/preserve decisions to LLM judge based on factual content**; iterates until target budget while preserving downstream performance; names underlying problem that **surface similarity correlates poorly with factual importance** so similarity-only memory pruning systematically deletes the wrong things; beats rule-based baselines under tight budgets across multiple memory frameworks | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13177 | status: open ; **counterpart to EvoMem at the deletion end** (EvoMem grows monotonically with supersedence events; MemRefine is the bounded-budget compactor that decides which historic entries survive); LLM-judge-over-similarity-proposals primitive is **reusable outside memory** for any "score and keep top-K" pruning where surface similarity is a poor proxy for content importance
- **2026-06-13** [arxiv:2606.13643] CLAIM: Recursive Agent Harnesses (RAH) (Elias Lumer, Sahil Sen, Kevin Paul, Vamse Kumar Subbiah) — **names and formalizes the pattern between Recursive Language Models (recursion over model calls only) and the production pattern visible in Anthropic's dynamic workflows** where a parent agent **writes and runs an executable script that spawns full sub-agent harnesses in parallel** (filesystem tools + code execution + planning per child) alongside structured function calls for small subtasks; on **Oolong-Synthetic (199 samples, context up to 4M tokens)**: **Codex baseline 71.75 → 81.36% with RAH on Codex (+9.6pp); Claude Sonnet 4.5 backbone 89.77%**; explicit quote from authors: "production coding agents have begun to write code that spawns subagents at scale, most recently in Anthropic's dynamic workflows" | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13643 | status: open ; first paper to give Anthropic's dynamic-workflow / Claude-Code-sub-agent pattern an academic name, an open benchmark, and a +10pp number; consistent with yesterday's "Illusion of Multi-Agent Advantage" [arxiv:2606.13003] caveat that **expert-architected MAS still beats auto-MAS** — RAH is exactly that (human-designed script template, model fills in dispatching); design lesson for Justin's MCP work: **expose primitives that subagents can call from generated scripts, not just from the orchestrator's tool list**; advantage is most visible at long-context scope (4M-token Oolong-Synthetic)

- **2026-06-13** [arxiv:2606.12882] CLAIM: HarnessBridge (Xiaoxuan Wang, Haixin Wang, Alexander Taylor, Jason Cong, Yizhou Sun, Wei Wang — UCLA inferred) — **learnable lightweight controller replacing hand-engineered agent harnesses via end-to-end instruction tuning**, two bidirectional projections: **observation projection** (raw trajectory → compact decision-relevant state) + **action projection** (proposed action → executable transition *or* trajectory-grounded rejection); claims **match-or-surpass specialized harnesses on Terminal-Bench 2.0 and SWE-Bench Verified with substantially reduced token usage and trajectory length**; generalizes from training on smaller generators to commercial-scale models; specific numerical results deferred to PDF | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12882 | status: open ; **compare** to Self-Harness [already in ledger, Terminal-Bench 2.0 40.5→61.9 with MiniMax-M2.5] — Self-Harness improves via agent edits the harness code, HarnessBridge improves via learn a controller end-to-end; both target the same bottleneck (harness layer between LLM and environment); open empirical question: which scales better as harness complexity grows? **bidirectional rejection mechanism** is a cleaner solution to "agent emits invalid action" than today's retry-on-parse-error loops if it reproduces under independent test; lesson for MCP-tool products — **observation-compaction layer is a separable artifact** you can train independently and ship with the tools
- **2026-06-13** [arxiv:2606.09426] CLAIM: WeaveBench (Wanli Li, Bowen Zhou, Yunyao Yu, Zhou Xu, Yifan Yang, Dongsheng Li, Caihua Shan) — long-horizon real-world benchmark for computer-use agents with **hybrid interfaces** (visual desktop control + CLI execution + code editing + browsers + external tools simultaneously), **114 tasks across 8 real-world work domains** grounded in real user requests with publicly verifiable artifacts; **trajectory-aware judge** over deliverables/files/screenshots/logs/action traces (not just final state); **best frontier model-runtime combination peaks at 41.2% PassRate** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09426 | status: open ; **41.2% is the realistic ceiling number for cross-runtime generalist agents today** — useful to revisit on every frontier release; computer-use eval has been balkanized (OSWorld desktop, WebArena browser, SWE-bench code, Terminal-Bench shell) with strong per-runtime scores climbing — WeaveBench rewards stitching them together; most real knowledge work is multi-interface, so this is the more honest benchmark for "agent that does my day job" framings

- **2026-06-14** [allenai-blog-2026-06-12-olmo-eval] CLAIM: Allen AI releases **olmo-eval**, OLMES successor — evaluation workbench for the model development loop with **modular task / suite / harness architecture** decoupling benchmark logic from runtime policy (same benchmark runs under lightweight direct eval *or* containerized sandbox for tool-using / code-execution / agentic); **pairwise checkpoint comparison view with standard error and minimum detectable effect (MDE)**; multi-turn tool-using evals integrated from day one; normalized experiment schema; sandbox + capability-routing layer with async planning | source: https://huggingface.co/blog/allenai/olmo-eval | status: open ; **task/suite/harness separation** is the right abstraction (cheap-indicative score under lightweight, expensive-definitive score under full agent harness, same benchmark); **pairwise + MDE** is the right report shape for "is checkpoint B actually better than checkpoint A" (separates real gain from noise); reusable workbench for any fine-tuning loop even without OLMo
- **2026-06-14** [arxiv:2606.13594] CLAIM: "See What I See, Know What I Think" (Siyi Chen et al., NVIDIA + academic) — **dense latent communication across heterogeneous agents via KV-cache exchange instead of text tokens**; lightweight cache transformation + two-phase training (reconstruction → generation) aligns hidden states across **Qwen3-4B / 8B / 14B**; demonstrated **in all six directional pairings**; reports **matching or surpassing text-based communication in context-aware scenarios at ~2–3× lower computational cost**; effective on in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks; beats prior heterogeneous-communication baselines especially in context-unaware transfer where text approaches fail | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13594 | status: open ; **structurally novel multi-agent primitive** — replaces tokenize/detokenize/context-rebuild on every cross-agent handoff; direct read-through to **local heterogeneous ensembles on M5 Max** (small fast router + large slower executor, both Qwen-family or both MLX-supported, sharing memory pool); MCP-style protocols may eventually grow a "cache-handoff" verb if cross-family generalization reproduces; open empirical question: does this work across model *families* (not just Qwen3 sizes)

- **2026-06-14** [arxiv:2606.13608] CLAIM: AgentBeats — **agentified agent assessment (AAA)**: judge agents evaluate subject agents over **standardized A2A (task delivery) + MCP (tool access) protocols** rather than fixed scaffolded benchmarks; validated by 5-month open competition with **298 judge agents and 467 subject agents across 12 categories** (includes coding-agent case study); framing problem — fixed benchmarks suffer "test-production mismatch" + prevent fair comparison across diverse agent designs | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13608 | status: open ; **MCP-as-evaluation-substrate** signal: if eval frameworks normalize on MCP for the tool surface, entrenches MCP as the agent interoperability layer (favorable for any MCP-tool product); **judge-agent design** is portable to internal QA — instead of hand-graded eval rubrics for an MCP tool, write a judge agent that interacts with the subject agent through the tool; competition scale (765 agents) is a real reproducibility claim
- **2026-06-14** [arxiv:2606.13106] CLAIM: SWITCH — **boundary-tokenized switchable latent reasoning compatible with on-policy RL**; explicit `<swi>` / `</swi>` boundary tokens mark entry/exit of latent-reasoning mode, making **policy ratio well-defined at decision points** (unlocks GRPO-style on-policy RL training for latent CoT, which prior hidden-state-recurrence approaches couldn't access); adds visible-to-latent curriculum + Switch-GRPO objective; mechanistic probing shows boundary tokens implement **learned switching (not stylistic markers)**, latent steps perform problem-specific computation, and computation concentrates at a **single hidden-state transition upon entry**; outperforms prior hidden-state-recurrence latent-reasoning at similar scale (numbers in PDF) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13106 | status: open ; cleanest published path to on-policy RL for latent reasoning; practical implication for agent products: **reasoning compute that doesn't blow up output token budgets** (model thinks in hidden state, only emits when ready) — directly cost-relevant for API-tier MCP-tool products if a frontier provider ships a SWITCH-style model

- **2026-06-17** [arxiv:2606.17929] CLAIM: PreAct (Bojie Li, single-author) — computer-using agents that **compile successful task runs into small state-machine programs** (states check screen conditions, transitions execute UI actions); **subsequent runs replay 8.5–13× faster without per-step LM calls**; safety primitives: per-action screen-state verification (return control to agent on divergence) + **only programs that pass independent clean re-run verification are stored** (prevents faulty-program accumulation); across mobile/desktop/web benchmarks the validation primitive added **+1.75–2.6 tasks per benchmark vs no-guardrail baseline**; **null finding** — prompt wording / runtime safeguards / retrieval method (LM vs embedding) had minimal effect, the compile-and-verify primitive is the leverage | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17929 | status: open ; **day-two of the Bojie-Li compile-to-executable-substrate thesis** — pairs with yesterday's [arxiv:2606.16707] User as Code (typed Python objects + functions as user-memory tier); UaC compiles memory tier, PreAct compiles action tier; combined claim is "right primitive for personal-agent products is *compile to deterministic executable substrate, fall back to LLM only on divergence*"; open empirical question whether UaC + PreAct compose cleanly (UaC preconditions trigger PreAct programs); the **independent-clean-re-run-verification gate is non-obvious and load-bearing** — programs admitted without it compound replay errors over time

- **2026-06-17** [arxiv:2606.18023] CLAIM: LoopCoder-v2 (Yang / Guo / W. Zhang / Zheng + 16 co-authors) — **Parallel Loop Transformers (PLT)** with **cross-loop position offsets (CLP)** + **shared-KV gated sliding-window attention**; turns sequential loop count (which scales latency + KV-cache linearly) into a practical inference-time-compute knob; **7B base trained on 18T tokens**; **SWE-Bench Verified 43.0 → 64.4 with PLT vs single-pass**; **Multi-SWE 14.0 → 31.0**; **methodological finding: two loops is optimal, more loops yield diminishing returns** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18023 | status: open ; **new local-fit ceiling for coding agents — 64.4% SWE-bench Verified at 7B** (territory previously requiring 70B+); two-loops-optimal is the clean budget rule for inference-time compute; pairs with today's [arxiv:2606.17930] *How Inference Compute Shapes Frontier Evaluation* (larger budgets substantially improve performance, benchmark scores are protocol-dependent) — PLT is concrete substrate for spending that budget without wall-clock blow-up; **shared KV across loops is the architectural reason PLT is M5-Max-friendly at 2 loops** (KV-cache does not scale linearly with loop count); custom decode required (PLT + CLP not in stock llama.cpp/MLX yet); 18T training tokens unreproducible by indie devs but PLT inference architecture is a fine-tune-on-top target
- **2026-06-17** [arxiv:2606.17591] CLAIM: Verbal RL Feedback Loop (Cui / X. Zhang / Y. Zhang / Shao / Shi / G. Wang / He) — **training-free verbal reinforcement learning** from objective world feedback (task outcomes, market returns, demand forecasts); names **"retention-forgetting dilemma in non-stationary environments"** (keeping outdated rules → negative transfer; discarding them → catastrophic forgetting when conditions return); three-layer architecture (**Rules** = distilled experience, **Evidence** = per-rule reliability logs across episodes, **Skills** = governance over which rules apply / conflict resolution / abstention) connected by **feedback-driven curation loop**; tested on financial forecasting (naturally non-stationary); **same accumulated experience either degrades performance below zero-shot baseline OR dramatically improves accuracy + risk-adjusted returns depending on whether curation loop is present** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17591 | status: open ; **strongest published reframe yet of GitOfThoughts ([arxiv:2606.14470]) and StreamMemBench ([arxiv:2606.14571]) null results** — argues the memory-cluster null is a **curation null, not a substrate null**; combined with yesterday's [arxiv:2606.16707] UaC (substrate matters: typed code 99% vs 6–43% on aggregates) the picture is now 2D: substrate matters AND curation matters; Rules/Evidence/Skills schema is implementable over any persistent memory layer; per-rule reliability log + governance step is **non-negotiable architectural primitive for any production memory layer** per this paper's bivalent finding; open empirical question whether Verbal RL's curation gains compose with UaC-style typed-code substrate

Zhang) — **multi-view evaluation framework for agent harness improvements**; measures updates across training / validation / held-out / replay diagnostics / operational costs; tested ACE / TF-GRPO / AHE on Terminal-Bench 2.0 + HLE; two surfaced findings the self-evolving cluster needs to confront: **(a) frequent updates may fail to improve held-out performance** (self-evolving curves partly overfit recent update batch); **(b) model backend can affect harness reliability** (harness tuned for one base may not transfer) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17546 | status: open ; **direct methodological counter to the self-evolving agent cluster** — [arxiv:2606.09498] Self-Harness, [arxiv:2606.05922] RHO, [arxiv:2606.07412] Socratic-SWE, today's [arxiv:2606.17628] OPD-Evolver, [arxiv:2605.18401] SkillsVote, [arxiv:2605.25430] CODESKILL all report striking gains without controlling for overfit-to-recent-batch or cross-backend transfer; open empirical question how many cluster improvement claims survive a SEAGym-style multi-view re-eval; cost-tracking view is the missing axis most self-improvement papers don't report — capability deltas without cost deltas is incomplete operational signal
- **2026-06-20** [arxiv:2606.20529] CLAIM: LedgerAgent (Md Nayem Uddin, Amir Saeidi, Eduardo Blanco, Chitta Baral) — **inference-time wrapper** for policy-adherent tool-calling agents: maintains observed task state in a **separate ledger rendered back into the prompt** and validates **state-dependent policy constraints before** environment-changing tool calls fire; tested on **four customer-service domains** with mixed open- and closed-weight models; improves **average pass^k** vs. standard prompt-based tool-calling baseline; **largest gains under stricter multi-trial consistency metrics** — i.e., stabilizes runs where stock agents intermittently use stale info or violate policy | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20529 | status: open ; composes with [arxiv:2605.10913] Shepherd typed-event execution traces and [arxiv:2605.12087] typed/versioned intermediate-artifacts position paper on "external agent state needs to be a typed, addressable, versioned data structure"; ledger-rendered-into-prompt is the cleanest published implementation of pre-execution constraint gating; no fine-tune required → works on stock vLLM / any tool-calling API

(Yuru Wang, Lejun Cheng, Yuxin Zuo, Sihang Zeng, Bingxiang He, Che Jiang, Junlin Yang, Yuchong Wang, Kaikai Zhao, Weifeng Huang, Kai Tian, Zhenzhao Yuan, Jincheng Zhong, Weizhi Wang, Ning Ding, Bowen Zhou, Kaiyan Zhang — Frontis AI) — **90 cross-discipline tasks** distilled from peer-reviewed Nature-family publications with auto-built per-task containerized environments via NatureGym pipeline (addresses env-fragmentation in prior research-agent benchmarks); strict web-search-disabled protocol; **10 frontier agent configurations evaluated; strongest model surpasses SOTA on only 17.8% of tasks (g>0.1 criterion)**; pathways analysis: agents succeed primarily through **methodological translation** (converting scientific tasks into familiar supervised-prediction problems) not genuine scientific invention; failures dominated by **wrong method choice + insufficient compute budget**, not task misunderstanding; benchmark + NatureGym pipeline + public leaderboard with maintainer-side reproduction released | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24530 | status: open ; **17.8% ceiling under web-search-off is sharp data point against "frontier coding agents are nearly here" narrative**; pair with [arxiv:2606.22883] CLI-Universe for terminal-vs-research-task coverage; failure-mode finding (wrong method, not wrong understanding) implies leverage point is method retrieval / pattern library, not better prompting
- **2026-06-24** [arxiv:2606.24033] CLAIM: Block-GTQ — RoPE-Aware Bit Allocation for KV-Cache Quantization (Fengfeng Liang, Yuechen Zhang, Jiaya Jia — JIA-Lab) — under RoPE, key's contribution to future attention logit decomposes into position-dependent sum over 2D frequency blocks → **key-cache quantization is a block-wise bit-allocation problem**; Block-GTQ computes label-free per-block energy score and greedily allocates integer bit-widths by marginal gain; built on TurboQuant-MSE (TQ-MSE); **per-layer MAE drops 32-80% at 2-3 b/dim K-only quantization, wins all 367/367 layer comparisons vs uniform TQ-MSE**; downstream gains — **Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct K2V2: NIAH avg 70.6 → 97.4, LongBench-EN avg 36.87 → 53.31; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B AIME 2024/2025 at K3V2 (no fp16 buffer): 51.7/37.5 vs fp16 54.2/37.9 while uniform TQ-MSE collapses to 0.0/0.0**; packed-cache serving on Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct + H800 — **K3V3 achieves 3.24x KV compression, 1.34x faster than fp16 FlashAttention2 at 128K, peak memory 56.31 GB → 19.85 GB, feasible at 256K and 512K where fp16 OOMs**; code at JIA-Lab-research/blockgtq | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24033 | status: open ; **most directly local-impactful paper this week** — KV cache headroom is binding constraint on M5 Max long-context agent loops; 3.24x compression at near-fp16 quality means real 128K-token MCP sessions fit in 64GB unified memory; needs port to llama.cpp Metal (upstream is CUDA H800)
- **2026-06-24** [arxiv:2606.24626] CLAIM: SAFARI — Scaling Long-Horizon Agentic Fault Attribution via Active Investigation (Chenyang Zhu, Jiayu Yao, Kushal Chawla, Youbing Yin, Nathan Wolfe, Pengshan Cai, Jingyu Wu, Spencer Hong, Sangwoo Cho, Shi-Xiong Zhang, Daben Liu, Sambit Sahu, Erin Babinsky) — replaces linear "load full trajectory into context" with **tool-augmented diagnostic loop** — specialized toolbox to read+search trajectory segments + persistent Short-Term Memory for cross-turn reasoning; **decouples diagnostic accuracy from architectural context limits**; **+20% over SOTA on Who&When at 1M token budget**, **+19% on TRAIL GAIA subset at 25K token budget**; **maintains 0.58 precision when target fault resides 5x beyond model's native context window** (scenario where traditional evaluators fail entirely) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24626 | status: open ; pattern is implementable today as custom subagent over trajectory file even before code release; pairs with [arxiv:2606.19409] OpenRath Sessions (artifact) + [arxiv:2606.20537] FlashRT Capsules (snapshot) — together sketch real durable-agents stack; 5x-beyond-context precision is the key data point for any system that has to debug its own past long-horizon runs
- **2026-06-24** [arxiv:2606.24855] CLAIM: OpenThoughts-Agent — Data Recipes for Agentic Models (OpenThoughts consortium, 50 authors led by Negin Raoof; Richard Zhuang, Marianna Nezhurina, Etash Guha et al.) — fully open data-curation pipeline for training generalist agent models (counter to single-benchmark-targeting open efforts like SWE-Smith, SERA, Nemotron-Terminal); **100+ controlled ablation experiments** systematically investigate each pipeline stage; final assembled set: **100K examples**; **Qwen3-32B fine-tune: 44.8% avg accuracy across seven agentic benchmarks, +3.9 pts over strongest prior open agentic data model (Nemotron-Terminal-32B 40.9%)**; outperforms alternative open datasets at every training set size in compute-controlled comparisons; training sets + pipeline + experimental data + models released at openthoughts.ai | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24855 | status: open ; **most credible generalist open agent model** at 32B class; pairs with [arxiv:2606.22883] CLI-Universe-Qwen3-32B (terminal specialist) — comparable size, complementary specialization; the 100-ablation pipeline-decomposition is reference architecture for any MCP-specific custom agent model from open weights
- **2026-06-24** [anthropic-2026-06-23-claude-tag] CLAIM: Anthropic — Introducing Claude Tag (2026-06-23 product launch, beta for Enterprise + Team plans) — Slack-native multi-user Claude agent on **Opus 4.8**; replaces existing Claude-in-Slack app over **30-day migration window**; **scoped identities with separate memories per use case** (admin-controlled tool + data access per channel + token spend limits + activity logs); **proactive "ambient" mode** optionally flags relevant information across connected channels; **multiplayer pickup** ("anyone can see what it's working on, and can pick up the conversation from where the last person left off"); **asynchronous autonomous work** over extended periods; Anthropic reports **internal version writes 65% of product team's code** | source: https://www.anthropic.com/news/introducing-claude-tag | status: open ; **productized version of [arxiv:2606.18829] GateMem + [arxiv:2606.24535] MemClaw governance thesis** — scoped identities with per-use-case memories is exactly the agent-memory governance abstraction those papers argue for; 65%-of-code internal metric is self-reported without methodology — treat as marketing until independently corroborated
- **2026-06-27** [arxiv:2606.26511] CLAIM: MemStrata / Temporal Validity in Retrieval Memory (Neeraj Yadav) — RAG fails on evolving facts because **cosine similarity 0.59 AUROC** distinguishing contradicted facts from duplicates (barely above chance); bi-temporal ledger: facts stored RAG-style for static recall, deterministic (subject, relation, object) supersession rule retires stale values on contradiction; **evolving knowledge 0.95-1.00 accuracy vs RAG 0.20-0.47; stale-fact errors 15-40% -> ~0%; retrieval latency ~2.1s vs 16-18s for LLM-rerank baselines**; code, evaluation datasets, marker-free protocol released | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26511 | status: open ; cleanest formulation of the "tool docs went stale and the agent kept calling the deprecated signature" problem; composes with [arxiv:2606.18829] GateMem (write gate) + [arxiv:2606.24535] MemClaw (propagation) as the temporal-retirement layer; 0.59-AUROC indictment of pure-vector RAG for evolving facts needs independent reproduction on long-running real-world workload before becoming load-bearing for production design
- **2026-06-27** [arxiv:2606.27136] CLAIM: JERP — Joint Learning of Experiential Rules and Policies (Shicheng Ye, Chao Yu) — couples NL rule pool with policy updates per episode using same trajectory; compares against successful reference trajectories for joint update direction; AlfWorld + WebShop consistent decision-performance gains over rules-only and policy-only baselines (concrete deltas not in abstract) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27136 | status: open ; first formalization of "rules drift from evolving policy" problem with measurable joint-update protocol; concept ports to weights-frozen API settings via rule-versioning + ablation-demotion; pairs with [arxiv:2606.26669] SKILL-DISCO (subgraph-to-tool distillation) and [arxiv:2606.24151] Metis (text + code memory) as the rule-side of a multi-substrate learning stack
- **2026-06-27** [arxiv:2606.26875] CLAIM: InfoKV — Information-Aware KV Cache Compression (Jushi Kai, Zhuiri Xiao, Alexandra Birch, Zhouhan Lin) — attention-only KV-compression selects tokens influential to **nearby** contexts only; Forward Influence metric reveals **high predictive-uncertainty tokens have substantially stronger distant-future influence**; combines entropy + attention scores; consistently beats attention-only baselines on Llama-3.1 / Llama-3.2 / DeepSeek-R1 long-context reasoning | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26875 | status: open ; orthogonal axis to [arxiv:2606.24033] Block-GTQ (Block-GTQ = how to compress kept tokens, InfoKV = which tokens to keep); stack composes for layered KV reduction on M5 Max long-context agentic loops; conventional "attention-eviction is SOTA" wisdom turns out incomplete — attention is near-future signal, entropy catches distant-future relevance
- **2026-06-27** [arxiv:2606.26666] CLAIM: PersistentKV — Page-Aware Decode Scheduling for Long-Context GQA Serving (Muhammad Ahmed) — native block-table decode engine for grouped-query attention with tile reuse across query heads + native page tables + compact workqueue executing only non-empty row-KV-head-sequence-split tasks; **RTX 3060 FP16 Hq=32/Hkv=8/d=128/page=16: 1.063-1.265x throughput on B8 bimodal/uniform/Zipf, 1.399x on B1 bucketed**; 7-page workshop paper | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26666 | status: open ; modest single-machine gains, but design pattern (page-aware GQA scheduling for heterogeneous sequence lengths) targets exactly the workload shape of agent loops (one big context + many short tool-response sequences); MLX unified-memory layout cheaper for page-tables than CUDA; worth tracking whether MLX picks up the primitive
