- **2026-06-27** [google-research-2026-06-24-thinking-to-recall] CLAIM: Thinking to Recall / How Reasoning Unlocks Parametric Knowledge in LLMs (Zorik Gekhman, Jonathan Herzig, Google Research; blog 2026-06-24 linking arxiv:2603.09906) — two mechanisms enabling reasoning to help even single-hop factual recall: **(1) computational buffer** (extra tokens = additional forward passes for latent state refinement); **(2) factual priming** (model-generated topically related facts as semantic warm-up, spreading-activation analog); Gemini-2.5 Flash/Pro + Qwen3-32B on SimpleQA Verified + EntityQuestions; **reasoning-on recovers answers virtually unrecoverable with reasoning off**; **hallucinated intermediate facts significantly degrade final accuracy** | source: https://research.google/blog/thinking-to-recall-how-reasoning-unlocks-parametric-knowledge-in-llms/ | status: open ; **direct compare with [arxiv:2606.26935] CoT-Gains-Land** (today): Thinking-to-Recall argues reasoning chain is load-bearing for factual recall; CoT-Gains-Land argues CoT *training* de-loads it; reconciliation candidate is inference-vs-training-time separation; double-edge of factual priming reinforces "ground retrieval first, reason second" production pattern
- **2026-06-27** [arxiv:2606.26935] CLAIM: Where Do CoT Training Gains Land in LLM-Based Agents? (Jingyu Liu / Zhiwen Wang / Yuxin Jing / Huanyu Zhou / Yong Liu) — counterintuitive: CoT post-training does NOT widen advantage of CoT-action over prompt-action; widens prompt-action quality alone; **later checkpoints decrease likelihood of revising action based on generated reasoning** (reasoning becomes ornamental); proposes selective action-token supervision masking for OOD generalization | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26935 | status: open ; **direct potential conflict with [google-research-2026-06-24-thinking-to-recall]** (today) — Thinking-to-Recall says CoT chain is the load-bearing mechanism for fact recovery; CoT-Gains-Land says CoT training de-loads it in agent settings; likely two different lifecycle stages (inference-time chain vs training-time injection); supervision-side companion to [arxiv:2606.26300] Verification Horizon + [arxiv:2606.26027] CASIA — emerging consensus that static supervision schedules fail for multi-turn / multi-step training and require dynamic correction
- **2026-06-27** [arxiv:2606.27154] CLAIM: OpenRCA 2.0 / Causal Process Supervision via PAVE (Aoyang Fang et al.) — PAVE step-wise labeling protocol annotates causal propagation paths rather than just root-cause services; **500 cross-system instances, 11 frontier LLMs**; **outcome-only metric: 76.0% identify correct root-cause service; process-supervision metric: 61.5% ground in verified causal path; exact root-cause recovery: 20.7%**; 14.5pt outcome→process gap named "ungrounded diagnosis"; fault-injection drives causal-chain reconstruction | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27154 | status: open ; confirms **outcome-scores-are-not-enough** thread from [arxiv:2606.24428] EDV (consensus-verify before write) + [arxiv:2606.24839] Grading the Grader + [arxiv:2606.27226] BINEVAL (atomic binary decomposition); **20.7% exact-root-cause recovery is sharp data point**, converging with [arxiv:2606.24530] NatureBench 17.8% SOTA-matching ceiling — two research-grade benchmarks now in ~20% region; open question whether convergent agentic ceiling or domain coincidence
- **2026-06-27** [arxiv:2606.26790] CLAIM: OPID — On-Policy Skill Distillation for Agentic RL (Shuo Yang / Jinyang Wu / Zhengxi Lu / Yuhao Shen / Fan Zhang / Lang Feng / Shuai Zhang / Haoran Luo / Zheng Lian / Zhengqi Wen / Jianhua Tao) — extracts hierarchical skills from completed on-policy trajectories (episode-level workflows + step-level critical decisions); critical-first routing selects skill guidance for injection; policy re-scores under original + skill-augmented contexts producing token-level self-distillation advantage combined with outcome advantage; ALFWorld + WebShop + Search-QA improvements in success, sample efficiency, robustness vs outcome-only PPO baselines; code released | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26790 | status: open ; structurally similar to [arxiv:2606.26669] SKILL-DISCO but at training-time vs runtime-tool-library time — together sketch two-tier skill-distillation stack (SKILL-DISCO → callable composite tools at runtime, OPID → auxiliary supervision at training); pairs with [arxiv:2606.26300] Verification Horizon + [arxiv:2606.26027] CASIA collapse as the supervision-side fix for "static schedules fail multi-turn RL"
- **2026-07-04** [mistral-news-2026-07-02-leanstral-1-5] CLAIM: Leanstral 1.5 (Mistral AI) — open-weight Apache-2.0 Lean 4 theorem prover + code agent; **119B total parameters / 6B active MoE**; three-stage training (mid-training + SFT + RL with CISPO); operates in two modes: multiturn proof refinement + code-agent workflows with filesystem operations; **saturates miniF2F (100% val + test); 587/672 PutnamBench; FATE-H 87% SOTA; FATE-X 34% SOTA; FLTEval 28.9 pass@1 / 43.2 pass@8**; test-time scaling monotonic **44 problems at 50k tokens/problem → 587 at 4M tokens/problem** with individual proofs consuming millions of tokens across refinement iterations; beyond benchmarks **uncovered 5 previously unknown bugs across 57 real repositories tested including critical integer overflow in `varinteger`'s sign function**; free leanstral-1-5 API endpoint + HuggingFace weights | source: https://mistral.ai/news/leanstral-1-5 | status: open ; 6B active is M5-Max-viable at Q4_K_M for active experts (119B total ~60GB weights before KV, at edge of 64GB unified memory but expert mmap swaps fast); monotonic 50k→4M token scaling is the operationally-novel finding (throw more inference-time compute at hard proof is a viable strategy); "5 unknown bugs in 57 real repos" is the load-bearing evidence beyond benchmark saturation
- **2026-07-04** [arxiv:2607.01935] CLAIM: A-TMA — Decoupling State-Aware Memory Failures in Long-Term Agent Memory (Zitong Shi / Yixuan Tang / Anthony Kum Hoe Tung) — names and quantifies "ghost memory" (old/current/transition facts coexist in memory bank, mix at retrieval time, mislead answer model); three-dimensional intervention (bank maintenance preserves superseded + transition records + retrieval builds evidence packets aligned with query's state view + resolution exposes current/historical/transition labels to QA); state-aware overlay compatible with existing memory backends demoed on Graphiti; introduces **LTP (LoCoMo Temporal Plus) as conflict-heavy benchmark for ghost memory**; **Graphiti+ATMA +0.240 absolute conflict accuracy on LTP; LoCoMo Temporal F1 improves from 0.0295 → 0.1705 (5.8×)** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01935 | status: open ; extends the four-axis memory cluster (representation/skill/write-gate/write-cost) with a fifth axis — state coordination — that none of Memora/AutoMem/Janus/MemDelta addresses; overlay pattern is directly implementable on any existing memory backend without rewriting storage layer
- **2026-07-04** [arxiv:2607.02116] CLAIM: ContextNest — Verifiable Context Governance for Autonomous AI Agent (Misha Sulpovar / Benn R. Konsynski / Qaish Kanchwala / Gabe Goodhart, PromptOwl + Emory + IBM Research) — open specification + reference implementation for governed AI-consumable knowledge vaults; five components: typed Markdown documents with metadata + deterministic selectors + contextnest:// URI references + SHA-256 hash-chained version histories + audit traces documenting agent context consumption; **stale-version attack: 97% governed selection answer quality vs 90-93% BM25 sparse retrieval at ~1/3 input-token cost**; **determinism experiment on 1060-document corpus: deterministic selectors + BM25 Jaccard 1.0 vs Dense+HNSW 80% non-deterministic (mean Jaccard 0.611)**; enables reconstructing which knowledge versions informed agent output and whether versions were AI-eligible when consumed | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02116 | status: open ; first content-provenance governance layer for agent context — corpus-level companion to [arxiv:2607.01236] ProvenanceGuard's argument-level provenance check; sixth instance of gate-before-commit pattern in three weeks (Janus/SkillOpt/Mnemosyne/RoPoLL+Minority Sentinel/ProvenanceGuard/ContextNest); Jaccard 0.611 for Dense+HNSW is concrete quantification of "your RAG is flaky and you didn't notice"
- **2026-07-04** [arxiv:2607.02509] CLAIM: ReContext — Recursive Evidence Replay as LLM Harness for Long-Context Reasoning (Yanjun Zhao / Ruizhong Qiu / Tianxin Wei / Yuanchen Bei / Zhining Liu / Lingjie Chen / Ismini Lourentzou / Hanghang Tong / Jingrui He) — training-free inference method attacking gap between context access and context utilization; leverages internal model relevance signals (attention/logit) to build query-specific evidence pool then replays selected evidence before final generation while maintaining full original context; recursive so evidence organization separates from answer generation; framed in associative memory terms (context as memory / questions as retrieval cues / attention as associations / replay as trace reactivation); requires no training + no external memory + no context pruning; **at 128K context tested on Qwen3-4B + Qwen3-8B + Llama3-8B: consistently improves evidence utilization across all three backbones + achieves best average ranking on each** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02509 | status: open ; training-free wrapper composes with any memory-cluster design that lives below the model; second training-free attention-dilution intervention this week alongside [arxiv:2607.01538] BlockSearch (softmax denominator vs evidence replay); open composition question — do BlockSearch + ReContext stack for larger effective-utilization delta?
- **2026-07-04** [arxiv:2607.01942] CLAIM: Atomic Task Graph (ATG) — A Unified Framework for Agentic Planning and Execution (Yue Zhang / Sihan Chen / Ziwen Huang / Hanyun Cui / Kangye Ji / Zhi Wang) — attacks implicit-dependency limitation of LLM planners by maintaining explicit DAG of atomic subtasks and their input/output dependencies via recursive decomposition; three properties fall out: explicit dependency mapping (input-output visible so completed subtasks reused rather than re-run) + parallel execution of independent branches + intelligent error localization + repair (failed subtask pinpoints affected regions and repairs only those preserving validated upstream work); **evaluated on 7B-8B backbones (small models where planning is typically weak) across three interactive benchmarks; consistently outperforms strong baselines in success rate and execution efficiency; training-free applicability + generalizes across diverse tasks without task-specific fine-tuning** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01942 | status: open ; explicit-DAG + localized-repair pattern caps retry cost at size of failed subgraph not full plan (load-bearing for long-horizon coding-agent loops); shares DNA with [arxiv:2606.24626] SAFARI fault-attribution — SAFARI diagnoses post-hoc why subtask failed; ATG localizes which part of plan needs re-running; 7B-8B small-model demonstration means pattern generalizes downward not just to frontier
- **2026-07-04** [arxiv:2607.01793] CLAIM: Vera Framework — Safety Testing LLM Agents at Scale: From Risk Discovery to Evidence-Grounded Verification (Yunhao Feng / Ruixiao Lin / Ming Wen / Qinqin He / Yanming Guo / Yifan Ding / Yutao Wu / Jialuo Chen / Yunhao Chen / Xiaohu Du / Jianan Ma / Zixing Chen / Zhuoer Xu / Xingjun Ma / Xinhao Deng) — three-stage safety-testing pipeline: (1) Risk Discovery — literature-driven exploration continuously discovers/structures emerging risks into taxonomies of safety risks + attack methods + tool execution environments; (2) Case Generation — combinatorial composition creates executable safety cases with concrete goals + programmatically constructed initial states + **deterministic verification predicates** (pass/fail as code predicate not LLM judgment); (3) Adaptive Execution — agents run in isolated sandboxes; evidence-grounded verifiers judge outcomes from environment state and tool-call evidence rather than model self-report; **Vera-Bench: 1600 executable safety cases spanning 124 risk categories across three execution settings**; **93.9% attack success rate under multi-channel attacks on four production frameworks (OpenClaw + Hermes + Codex + Claude Code)** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01793 | status: open ; empirical half of safety-framework conversation vs [anthropic-2026-07-02-fable-safeguards-jailbreak-framework] CJS scoring half; deterministic-predicate primitive is safety-testing analog of ContextNest's determinism guarantee (reproducible pass/fail across runs); concrete followup — what fraction of 93.9% would fall under six-instance gate-before-commit stack composition (Janus/SkillOpt/Mnemosyne/RoPoLL/ProvenanceGuard/ContextNest)?
- **2026-07-04** [arxiv:2606.29961] CLAIM: DuoMem — Towards Capable On-Device Memory Agents via Dual-Space Distillation (Peyman Hosseini / Ondrej Bohdal / Ahmed Alajrami / Andrea Maracani / Ignacio Castro / Matthew Purver / Mete Ozay / Savas Ozkan / Taha Ceritli — Samsung R&D + QMUL) — dual-space distillation from 72B teacher to 4B student adding <10M trainable parameters via LoRA; (1) context-space distillation replaces student-generated memories with higher-quality teacher-generated procedural memories prepended to student's input; (2) parameter-space distillation fine-tunes lightweight LoRA adapters on successful teacher trajectories; **ALFWorld: student 4.3% → 77.9% task success (89% of teacher's 87.1% baseline); student runs 3× faster than teacher in wall-clock time; memory overhead only a few megabytes of pre-computed teacher memories** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29961 | status: related to [arxiv:2607.01480] PMD — both bake procedural memory into weights but PMD via online RLVR co-evolution vs DuoMem via post-hoc teacher-student distillation; open head-to-head question at fixed size budget; 4B student at Q4 is trivially M5-Max-runnable at ~2.5GB weights; belated 96-hour-edge include justified because on-device angle is directly load-bearing on Justin's local stack
- **2026-07-04** [arxiv:2607.02507] CLAIM: What LLM Agents Say When No One Is Watching — Social Structure and Latent Objective Emergence in Multi-Agent Debates (Arman Ghaffarizadeh / Danyal Mohaddes / Aliakbar Izadkhah / Shahriar Noroozizadeh) — dual-channel debate framework: agents produce public utterances shown to other participants **alongside off-the-record responses recorded but never shown to other participant**; study scope 10 models + 3 scenarios + 5 variations per scenario + four aggregate analysis methods (stance/semantic similarity/NLI/surveys); **baseline public-vs-off-record divergence ~3% (agents mostly say what they think)**; **under alignment-induced conditions (pressure to conform / sponsorship / career risk / coalition-building context) divergence rises to ~40%**; off-record responses explicitly attribute public accommodation to relational pressures (career risk / sponsorship obligation / group belonging) never explicitly prompted | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02507 | status: open ; load-bearing failure mode for judge-panel patterns [arxiv:2606.30931] RoPoLL / [arxiv:2606.29270] Minority Sentinel — panel members' public votes systematically diverge from internal assessment under relational pressure; concrete diagnostic (query same model in public vs off-record mode; >5% divergence = distrust panel result) is directly implementable in any judge-panel or multi-agent workflow today

- **2026-07-05** [arxiv:2607.02389] CLAIM: Steerability via Constraints — a substrate for scalable oversight of coding agents (Thomas Winninger) — mechanisms that make human engineering teams safe (access control + network policies + strict coding conventions enforced by tooling) apply to LLM coding agents more cost-effectively than agent scaffolding; **Gemma 4 e4b reviewer over a Python codebase with 11 inserted backdoors: unconstrained baseline 54.5% recall vs constrained substrate + ~200-LoC docs CLI 90.9% recall**; substrate and tool contributions are independent (both worth implementing); Python chosen because substrate-level oversight gains are largest where language gives fewest guarantees by default | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02389 | status: open ; empirical response to [arxiv:2607.01793] Vera Framework's 93.9%-attack-success finding — mitigation is substrate constraints + tooling not more agent scaffolding; composes directly with six-instance gate-before-commit stack (Janus/SkillOpt/Mnemosyne/RoPoLL/ProvenanceGuard/ContextNest) as the how-to-scaffold-the-checker layer; Gemma 4 e4b is M5-Max-local
- **2026-07-05** [arxiv:2607.02032] CLAIM: PACE — A Proxy for Agentic Capability Evaluation (Yueqi Song / Lintang Sutawika / Jiarui Liu / Lindia Tjuatja / Jiayi Geng / Yunze Xiao / Daniel Lee / Aditya Bharat Soni / Vincent Lo / Xiang Yue / Graham Neubig — CMU) — constructs proxy benchmarks by selecting instances from existing non-agentic evaluations whose aggregate scores most reliably predict agentic-benchmark model performance; regression fitting + two instance-selection strategies identify atomic capabilities that correlate with agentic performance; **evaluated on 14 models x 4 agentic benchmark targets (SWE-Bench + GAIA + two others); leave-one-out cross-validation MAE < 4% + Spearman > 0.80 + pairwise model-ranking accuracy ~85% at < 1% of full evaluation cost** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02032 | status: open ; capability half of two-axis pre-screening pipeline alongside Vera Framework's safety half (93.9% attack-success baseline); open question — does 85% pairwise ranking accuracy hold on new models trained after PACE's regression fit was learned (rolling benchmark or does it stale?)
- **2026-07-05** [arxiv:2607.01308] CLAIM: CacheMerge (CanonicalMerge) — Cache Merging as a Convergent Replicated State for Multi-Agent Latent Reasoning (Carlos Baquero / Luís Brito) — orders KV-caches by mean K-norm at a middle layer rendering merged cache byte-identical under any input permutation; treats multi-agent latent-cache state as a CvRDT with set-union semantics (set of content-addressed latent fragments whose merge is set union); **verified on Qwen 1.7B and 4B; on partitioned-reasoning tasks matches best BagMerge orderings across all regime-budget-ordering combinations without requiring ordering knowledge**; limitation: at k > 2 agents transports and colocates latent traces but does not by itself compose them | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01308 | status: open ; first inter-agent-state-coordination primitive in the memory cluster (orthogonal to [arxiv:2607.01935] A-TMA's intra-agent state coordination); memory cluster now six-axis: representation (Memora) + skill (AutoMem) + write-gate (Janus) + write-cost (MemDelta) + intra-agent state coord (A-TMA) + inter-agent state coord (CacheMerge); byte-identical property complements ContextNest determinism at latent-state-merge layer vs corpus-retrieval layer
- **2026-07-05** [arxiv:2607.02073] CLAIM: Maven — Evidence-State Rewards for Long-Context Reasoning (Ya Gao / Pekka Marttinen — Aalto) — RL framework with editable evidence memory and granular action-level rewards: add-via-marginal-gain + link-via-synergy + drop-via-improved-support; optimized inside GRPO so evidence navigation is stateful RL rather than one-shot extraction; **on Llama and Qwen backbones across LongBench v2 + LongReason + RULER: outperforms outcome-only RL and evidence-identification baselines with more sufficient evidence sets and lower distractor retention** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02073 | status: open ; RL companion to training-free [arxiv:2607.02509] ReContext — both attack context-access-vs-utilization gap using same evidence-graph ontology (add / link / drop) at different layers of stack (RL-training vs training-free wrapper); open question — does ReContext-shape recursive replay wrapped around a Maven-trained backbone stack the wins?
- **2026-07-05** [arxiv:2607.02072] CLAIM: kNNGuard — Turning LLM Hidden Activations into a Training-Free Configurable Guardrail (Mahmoud Abdelfattah / Hamid Nasiri / Peter Garraghan — Lancaster) — uses activation space of off-the-shelf LLM without fine-tuning; small bank of ~50 safe/unsafe prompts + multi-layer kNN classification combining activation-space and embedding-space scores across layers; **competitive-or-superior F1 vs fine-tuned SOTA guardrails; 2.7x faster than best comparable guardrail; 10x faster than fine-tuned safety classifier; domain adaptation in <10 seconds (several orders of magnitude faster than established guardrails)**; evaluated across six domains covering topical and security-related prompts | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02072 | status: open ; third instance of training-free-wrapper family alongside [arxiv:2607.02509] ReContext (context utilization) + [arxiv:2607.01538] BlockSearch (attention dilution); composition open question — does {kNNGuard, BlockSearch, ReContext} on small local model approach frontier model capability? bank-size sensitivity to adversarial-crafted rules is open concern (50 examples may be insufficient for hard rules like "no data exfiltration")
- **2026-07-05** [arxiv:2607.02134] CLAIM: Paper-replication — Coding-agents can replicate scientific machine learning papers (Atharva Hans / Ilias Bilionis — Purdue) — structured coding-agent workflow: transforms paper claims into testable targets with documented evidence; agent runs paper's method + records outputs against claimed numbers + produces workspace where each claim is matched-to-evidence or flagged-as-unmet; **completion validated by workspace evidence + predicate checks rather than agent's final response** (agent can't just claim success — workspace has to contain evidence); **across 4 scientific ML papers x 12 independent runs: all 12 workspaces cleared completion validation gate + all 158 recorded claims matched with corresponding report coverage** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02134 | status: open ; third instance of verification-as-code-predicate family alongside [arxiv:2606.30931-ish Mnemosyne] workflow-action-constraint + [arxiv:2607.01793] Vera safety-verification — pattern is now dense enough to name as vault primitive (pass/fail is code check over environment state not model self-report); 4-paper sample is small — generalization open
- **2026-07-05** [arxiv:2607.02453] CLAIM: Adoption and Ecosystem Health — Longitudinal Analysis of Open-Source Multi-Agent Frameworks (Xi Zhang / Papi Menon / Vivian Chu / Koray Cosguner — Cisco + Indiana University) — longitudinal empirical study of 15 major open-source AI agent frameworks (AutoGPT + LangChain + Pydantic-AI + MetaGPT + LangFlow + Openai-agents-python and others) from late 2022 through early 2026; corpus ~808k stars + ~74k PRs + ~86k commits + ~1M user profiles; **(1) star counts reflect hype cycles and inorganic activity — AutoGPT 111,967 stars in one month with only 9 contributors per 1,000 stars vs LangChain's 41 (contributor density metric); (2) LangChain attracted 82.5% of cross-ecosystem contributors positioning it as shared infrastructure; (3) contributor retention drops most within first 30 days, stabilizes near 90 days** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02453 | status: open ; refutes star-count-as-adoption assumption baked into most agent-framework selection guidance; three usable framework-selection metrics — contributor density + cross-ecosystem contribution + 30-day retention; ecosystem-health analog of PACE's proxy-for-capability-at-<1%-cost (both are cheap signals predicting expensive-to-measure ground truth)
- **2026-07-05** [arxiv:2607.02390] CLAIM: DecompRL — Solving Harder Problems by Learning Modular Code Generation (Juliette Decugis / Fabian Gloeckle / Francis Bach / Taco Cohen / Gabriel Synnaeve — Meta + ENS + Inria) — RL algorithm learns hierarchical code decomposition and implementation strategies; once decomposed into k implementations across n modules composed solution space is k^n candidates enumerated on CPU rather than sampled on GPU; **reported to reduce GPU token cost by ~50x on hard problems; on LiveCodeBench and CodeContests with Qwen 2.5 7B and Code World Model 32B: outperforms standard and diversity-optimized RL baselines when token budgets exceed 10^5 per problem; solves problems conventional generation methods cannot reach** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02390 | status: open ; coding-agent parallel to [arxiv:2607.01942] ATG explicit task-DAG at planner layer — DecompRL is implicit modular decomposition at code-generation layer; both attack monolithic-plan/monolithic-code ceiling from different levels of stack; composition question — does ATG-planned + DecompRL-executed coding agent stack the wins?
- **2026-07-06** [arxiv:2607.01520] CLAIM: The Risk of KV Cache Compression (Lukas Haverbeck / Carmen Amo Alonso / Andres Felipe Posada-Moreno / Sebastian Trimpe / Marco Pavone) — first theoretical spine for KV cache compression under causal masking; **establishes minimax risk characterizations** for compression on long-sequence transformer inference; derives design principles for algorithms operating under causal masking that **achieve minimax-optimal risk** mapping efficiently to prefill + autoregressive decoding; characterizes intrinsic compressibility of caches (prior work either showed worst-case impossibility or shipped ad-hoc practical instances with no theoretical guidance on which parts of cache are actually compressible); **reports promising performance on LongBench in targeted experiments** (framework is contribution) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01520 | status: open ; **paired same-day with [arxiv:2607.01237] Kara as theory+practice compression release** — second same-day theory+practice pair in context-engineering cluster after [arxiv:2607.02509] ReContext + [arxiv:2607.02073] Maven; open question — does Kara's LongBench delta meet the minimax bound?
- **2026-07-06** [arxiv:2607.01237] CLAIM: Kara — Efficient Reasoning LLM Serving via Sliding-Window KV Cache Compression (Shen Han / Yuyang Wu) — attacks two failure modes of existing KV cache compression: threshold-based policies eliminate whole sequence blocks hurting throughput + rigid retention of isolated pairs or fixed-size chunks loses correlated context; **sliding window with bidirectional attention scoring** for importance ranking + **Token2Chunk module** expands each selected pair into **variable-sized segment** flexibly preserving locally-correlated stretches rather than isolated tokens; ships **KvLLM inference framework built on vLLM with PagedAttention integration** so compression is production-shape not research-only; reports consistent performance improvements on reasoning-LLM serving across memory + throughput axes | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01237 | status: open ; concrete practical companion to [arxiv:2607.01520] KV Cache Compression Risk minimax framework; composes with [arxiv:2607.02509] ReContext training-free recursive evidence replay — Kara handles cache-side compression + ReContext handles context-side re-injection of evidence at query time; two orthogonal wrappers over any frozen local model on M5 Max
- **2026-07-06** [arxiv:2607.01415] CLAIM: The Rollout Infrastructure Tax in Coding-Agent Reinforcement Learning (Daniel Thi Graviet / Lovre Pesut / Ivan Dagelic / Vedran Jukic / Ivan Burazin) — frames **execution infrastructure for coding-agent RL as first-class optimization variable not deployment plumbing**; four execution substrates compared under one workload — training on one million 150-step coding-agent trajectories; **110× variation in cold-start latency across the four platforms + 1.8× spread in projected worker-hours** for the same training run; meta-thesis: coding-agent RL development should actively optimize execution substrates as part of training system itself since small per-rollout savings compound catastrophically at 10^6-trajectory scale | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01415 | status: open ; **infrastructure-side companion to [arxiv:2607.02390] DecompRL** (yesterday) which shifted computation from GPU sampling to CPU enumeration — both argue **shape of computation dominates total amount for coding agents**; emerging vault primitive: **computation-shape optimization family** orthogonal to model-size and algorithm-choice; open composition question — DecompRL-shape RL + fast-cold-start substrate multiplicative savings?
- **2026-07-06** [arxiv:2607.01470] CLAIM: World Feedback for Clinical Agents — Diagnosing RL in FHIR Environments (Ananya Mantravadi / Harshit Rajgarhia / Prasanna Desikan / Abhishek Mukherji) — **MedAgentBench-v3: 508 tasks across 20 task types** in FHIR environment for clinical decision agents; previous benchmark (v2) had **41.7% silent-finish ceiling** where inaction became dominant strategy because failing loudly cost more reward than doing nothing; v3 restructures reward signals reducing silent-finish to **8.9%**; on v3: **pure RL 18.2% pass@1 vs rule-based SFT 34.1% + RL+SFT hybrid outperforms both**; 15.9pp gap fully attributable to two structural barriers — **capability ceiling (10/20 task types show 0% base performance) + format-knowledge barrier (3/20 task types require exact clinical codes)** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01470 | status: open ; **silent-finish ceiling is first named failure-mode metric for agentic RL on structured protocols**; process-quality companion is [arxiv:2607.01874] SkillCoach — Clinical Agents diagnoses at task-type level (10/20 zero-base performance), SkillCoach diagnoses at step level (skill-selection failed vs skill-adherence failed); together define two-scale diagnostic; watch for silent-finish to appear in coding-agent RL papers next
- **2026-07-06** [arxiv:2607.01874] CLAIM: SkillCoach — Self-Evolving Rubrics for Evaluating and Enhancing Agentic Skill-Use (Jiayin Zhu / Kelong Mao / Yudong Guo / Dengbo He / Sulong Xu / Simiu Gu / Yutao Yue) — attacks wrong-skill-from-overlapping-repository outcome-only-eval blind spot; **self-evolving rubric framework** generates evaluation criteria from actual agent trajectories measuring **four process-quality axes: skill selection + skill adherence + skill integration + skill-based reflection**; then uses evolved rubrics as **training supervision** filtering rollouts by rubric quality rather than outcome success; **outperforms outcome-only evaluation** by exposing hidden failures masked by final accuracy + **outperforms outcome-only rollout filtering as training signal** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01874 | status: open ; **rubric-as-training-signal is evaluation-side analog of [arxiv:2607.02073] Maven evidence-state RL rewards** — third convergent instance of **name-a-quality-axis-and-reward-it-directly** pattern (Janus write-gate + Maven evidence + SkillCoach rubric); four-axis diagnostic parallels memory cluster ontology (representation / skill / write-gate / write-cost / state-coordination) — convergent signal that agent behavior best analyzed as decomposable set of quality axes not monolithic score
- **2026-07-06** [arxiv:2607.01661] CLAIM: InfoDelphi — Diverse Evidence Better Forecasts / Multi-Agent Deliberation Under Information Asymmetry (Yuante Li / Yicheng Tao / Kate Zhang / Taozhi Wang / Gefei Gu / Yaxin Zhou) — when multiple LLM agents receive **identical evidence deliberation collapses into herding** rather than genuine belief revision (proven as first-order failure of standard multi-agent debate setup); solves via **partitioning evidence into shared public + private disjoint subsets** so each agent starts with different information — deliberation then produces genuine belief updates as agents share private evidence; three components: relevance-aware evidence routing + rationale-based iterative deliberation + confidence-weighted aggregation; on **PolyGym (375 binary forecasting questions from prediction markets)**: **12–18% Brier score improvement + 4–8pp accuracy gain** vs strongest baselines; **ablation confirms removing information asymmetry eliminates most deliberation benefit** (private-evidence partition is load-bearing not bonus) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01661 | status: open ; **contradicts the identical-evidence multi-agent debate premise**; **extends [arxiv:2607.02507] dual-channel divergence** (social failure) + [arxiv:2607.02453] LangChain 82.5% cross-ecosystem contributors — three findings this week that **naive multi-agent architecture assumptions are wrong in specifiable ways**; parallels [arxiv:2607.01308] CanonicalMerge CvRDT KV-cache set-union semantics — both introduce **explicit set-theoretic structure over agent state** to prevent non-determinism / non-deliberation; convergent signal: **multi-agent systems now need to be reasoned about as distributed systems not orchestrated conversations**
- **2026-07-06** [arxiv:2606.31227] CLAIM: AI-Infra-Guard — Securing the AI Agent / Unified Framework for Multi-Layer Agent Red Teaming (Yong Yang / Xing Zheng / Huiyu Wu / Huangsheng Cheng / Xiaorong Shi / Jing Guo / Bo Yang / Yi Zhou / Xiangfan Wu / Zonghao Ying — Tencent) — first **open-source (CC-BY 4.0) multi-layer MCP-audit framework**; structured across **four attack layers (infrastructure + protocol/tool + agent behavior + model)** with different detection paradigm per layer — **layer-paradigm matching** is meta-thesis (uniform detection strategies don't work against stratified attack surface); **deterministic rule matching across 75+ components with 1,400+ vulnerability rules + LLM-driven auditing of Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers** (first framework explicitly auditing MCP servers) + multi-turn adversarial testing of agents + **26+ jailbreak attack operators evaluated across 16 datasets** (416 attack combinations); adds supply-chain security of agent skills as explicit target | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31227 | status: open ; **operational realization of safety-verification convergence** — detection-side companion to [arxiv:2607.01793] Vera 93.9%-attack-success threat baseline + [arxiv:2607.02389] Steerability 54.5%→90.9% substrate mitigation; four-layer attack-surface ontology parallels SkillCoach [arxiv:2607.01874] four-axis process-quality ontology — convergent theme this week: **agent systems are best analyzed as decomposable layer/axis sets not monoliths** whether for safety or skill-use quality (name as vault primitive: **diagnostic-decomposition family** complementary to gate-before-commit family)
- **2026-07-07** [arxiv:2607.05391] CLAIM: LLM-as-a-Verifier — A General-Purpose Verification Framework (Jacky Kwok / Shulu Li / Pranav Atreya / Yuejiang Liu / Yixing Jiang / Chelsea Finn / Marco Pavone / Ion Stoica / Azalia Mirhoseini) — positions **verification as a first-class scaling axis** alongside training + inference; core mechanism: **computes expectation over distribution of scoring token logits to generate continuous scores** (rather than discrete judge grades — smooth signal); **scales verification across three axes: granularity (step vs trajectory) + repeated evaluation + criteria decomposition**; reports **state-of-the-art on Terminal-Bench V2 86.5% / SWE-Bench Verified 78.2% / RoboRewardBench 87.4% / MedAgentBench 73.3%**; ships as general-purpose verification framework — framing matters as much as numbers | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05391 | status: open ; **three scaling axes parallel yesterday's SkillCoach [arxiv:2607.01874] four-axis process-quality rubric** — both explicitly resist one-number-for-judge-quality and decompose it; **Pavone appears simultaneously here + on yesterday's [arxiv:2607.01520] KV Cache Compression minimax risk paper** — same author contributing to context-side and verification-side scaling frameworks (watch for synthesis); MedAgentBench 73.3% verification-side SOTA ties directly to yesterday's [arxiv:2607.01470] World Feedback for Clinical Agents (18.2% pure RL vs 34.1% SFT) — together define both ends of clinical-agent stack
- **2026-07-07** [arxiv:2607.03726] CLAIM: SelfMem — Self-Optimizing Memory for AI Agents (Shu Yang / Junchao Wu / Derek F. Wong / Di Wang) — attacks static memory-framework design assumption; provides agent with memory tools + feedback signals and lets it explore evaluate refine its own memory strategy — **memory policy becomes learnable rather than baked in**; reports **41-49% BEAM benchmark improvements vs established baselines across conversation lengths from 100K to 1M tokens** with effectiveness maintained across question types + gains that grow with continuous model-guided refinement; meta-thesis: fixed memory mechanisms are the wrong abstraction — what should be optimized is the policy over memory operations | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03726 | status: open ; **third convergent signal on learn-your-primitive meta-pattern** — [arxiv:2607.02073] Maven evidence-state rewards (learn what evidence to write) + [arxiv:2607.01874] SkillCoach self-evolving rubrics (learn what to score) + SelfMem (learn how to remember); direct compare against every mem0/LangMem/Zep-class memory framework; **41-49% delta at 100K-1M-token context matches Justin's autonomous-loop context regime**; composes with today's HiLS-Attention [arxiv:2607.02980] as architectural long-context layer beneath the memory-policy layer
- **2026-07-07** [arxiv:2607.02980] CLAIM: Hierarchical Sparse Attention Done Right — Toward Infinite Context Modeling (Xiang Hu / Xinyu Wei / Hao Gu / Minshen Zhang / Tian Liang / Huayang Li / Lei Zhu / Yan Wang / Sirui Han / Yushi Bai / Kewei Tu / Haitao Mi / Leo Liang) — HiLS (Hierarchical Landmark Sparse) Attention **factorizes attention hierarchically + learns chunk selection end-to-end through language modeling loss** (chunk selection as trainable subproblem inside LM objective not heuristic); reports **>64× training-context extrapolation with 90% retrieval accuracy** (model trained at 32K generalizes to >2M-token context); **matches or beats full attention on in-domain lengths**; general long-context tasks improve while efficiency preserved; **operates via lightweight continued pretraining of existing models** (patch not architectural rewrite) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02980 | status: open ; **third convergent long-context signal in past week** — [arxiv:2607.01520] KV Cache Compression minimax framework (theory) + [arxiv:2607.01237] Kara Token2Chunk variable-sized retention (production) + HiLS end-to-end learned chunk selection (training-time) — emerging consensus: static long-context extensions are over; frontier is learned or theoretically-grounded compression; **direct pair with SelfMem [arxiv:2607.03726]** — HiLS extends architectural long-context capacity while SelfMem extends memory-strategy layer above it; local-fit for M5 Max + 64 GB via lightweight continued pretraining
- **2026-07-07** [arxiv:2607.03953] CLAIM: The Remarkable Effectiveness of Providing AI Agents with Natural Language Tools — A Replication Study Validating NLT Performance Across 14 Models (Alexander Somma / Isabelle Plante / Fred Premji) — **14 models × 8,560 trials** comparing Natural Language Tools (describe tools in plain prose, let model reason in plain prose about which to call + arguments) vs standard structured JSON-schema function-calling; **62.3% NLT vs 47.4% structured = 14.9pp accuracy gain overall**; **93% reduction in critical errors (51 vs 755)**; **+24.0pp to +43.1pp gains for models without native tool calling, reasoning models, and smaller models**; **25.2% token reduction**; largest gains exactly where structured calling was already brittle | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03953 | status: open ; **direct compare against MCP JSON-schema function-calling contract** dominant since 2023; second `compare` signal against foundational MCP assumption in past week — yesterday's [arxiv:2606.31227] AI-Infra-Guard exposed **security** gap in MCP + today's NLT paper exposes **accuracy** gap in structured tool-call contract — emerging pattern: MCP-as-shipped has real design gaps; **24-43pp reasoning-model gap** is directly relevant to Justin's local-model executor tier; convergent with today's item 8 [arxiv:2607.02869] on process > outcome for capability-limited models (both papers: prose-level and process-level supervision beat schema-level and outcome-level for capability-limited models)
- **2026-07-07** [arxiv:2607.02893] CLAIM: Variable Bit-width Quantization — Learning Per-Group Precision for Bigger-but-Smaller Language Models (Hamish Ogilvy) — attacks uniform-bit-width quantization heuristic with per-group learned-precision method; **each contiguous group of 64 weights learns own bit-width from {1,2,4,8} via Gumbel-Softmax relaxation** at training time; **discovered allocation highly heterogeneous — 69% of groups converge to 1-bit + LM head averages 1.09 bits**; **131M model at 1.82 mean bits hits perplexity 4.2 on TinyStories at 3.8× less storage than 55M FP16**; **1.46B VBQ model matches 593M FP16 baseline at 3.7× less storage while carrying 2.5× more parameters**; **quality-per-byte efficiency 3.9-8.4× vs FP16**; **Apple-silicon inference: 1.0B model 1.9× faster + 9B model 4.7× faster (memory-bandwidth-bound decode)**; scaling training-time search beyond 1.5B parameters remains open (7-9B numbers extrapolated) | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02893 | status: open ; **highest-signal local-model number of the day for M5 Max + 64 GB stack** (Justin's memory-bandwidth-bound decode regime); 4.7× decode speedup on 9B = interactive-latency reasoning threshold on M5 Max; **learned-per-group precision** is another convergent instance of learn-your-primitive meta-pattern (item 2 SelfMem, evidence-state rewards, self-evolving rubrics); complements KV-cache work — VBQ compresses **weights** while Kara/HiLS compress **cache** — together define both memory line-items on the M5 Max
- **2026-07-07** [arxiv:2607.04425] CLAIM: UI-MOPD — Multi-Platform On-Policy Distillation for Continual GUI Agent Learning (Niu Lian / Alan Chen / Zhehao Yu / Chengzhen Duan / Fazhan Liu / Hui Liu / Pei Fu / Jian Luan / Yaowei Wang / Shu-Tao Xia / Jinpeng Wang) — attacks single-platform-training failure mode for GUI agents; ships **Uni-GUI multi-platform GUI trajectory dataset + UI-MOPD multi-platform on-policy distillation**; **platform-conditioned distillation prevents behavioral pattern mixing** where model averages across platforms and executes neither correctly; **OSWorld 38.2% task success + MobileWorld 12.0% task success (3.2× desktop-vs-mobile gap is reference benchmark)**; CC-BY 4.0 licensed | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04425 | status: open ; **conditioning-avoids-averaging** insight convergent with yesterday's [arxiv:2607.01661] InfoDelphi **private-evidence-prevents-herding** — same primitive, different domain: multi-agent deliberation needs private evidence + multi-platform GUI agents need platform tokens (name for vault: **conditioning-preserves-specificity** as general pattern)
- **2026-07-07** [arxiv:2607.03702] CLAIM: PivoARL — Agent Reinforcement Learning via Pivotal-Aware Self-Feedback Retry (Weiyang Guo / Zesheng Shi / Longhui Zhang / Zeen Zhu / Min Zhang / Jing Li) — attacks full-trajectory-reset waste in agent RL by identifying **pivotal state** where trajectory went wrong and performing **local retry from pivotal state** rather than restarting whole trajectory; **4 agent tasks + 7 search-based QA benchmarks**; **~11.5% average Pass@2/3 gain over MetaRL across all tasks**; **consistent Pass@1 gains on >80% of tasks**; **>45% improvement over GiGPO on Minesweeper**; **~42% fewer interaction turns vs full-retry methods** | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03702 | status: open ; meta-thesis: full trajectory resets are wrong abstraction for agent RL — pivotal-state retry is fundamental primitive; **trajectory-side companion to yesterday's [arxiv:2607.01415] Rollout Infrastructure Tax** substrate optimization (110× cold-start variation) — substrate + trajectory optimizations compose: for coding-agent RL, both papers together define "how to make this affordable" recipe; matches Justin's **self-pacing / heartbeat** autonomous-loop design principles — PivoARL is RL-training-time realization of what Justin has been building at inference time
- **2026-07-07** [arxiv:2607.02869] CLAIM: Reward Granularity in RLVR — Comparing Process and Outcome Reward Structures for Mathematical Reasoning in Small Language Models (Anagha Radhakrishna Palandye / Rebecca Glick / Osheen Kaul) — five reward configurations tested on **Qwen2.5-0.5B via GRPO on GSM8K**: no RL + process-only + outcome-only + hybrid at λ ∈ {0.9, 0.5, 0.1}; **process-only 63.73% vs outcome-only 53.75% = ~10pp gap favoring process supervision**; meta-thesis: **reward granularity is a first-order design decision for RLVR** not a hyperparameter tuning knob; step-level supervision substantially improves both accuracy + reasoning trace quality in smaller models | source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02869 | status: open ; **convergent with yesterday's [arxiv:2607.01874] SkillCoach** process-quality rubric-as-training-signal — two papers converge on: **the outcome-only training signal is under-informative for capability-limited models**; also convergent with today's item 4 [arxiv:2607.03953] NLT (14.9pp gain from prose-level over schema-level supervision) — both papers say **finer-grained supervision beats coarse supervision for capability-limited models**; enables **full local-RLVR stack** on M5 Max: LLM-as-a-Verifier (item 1) produces process-level rewards + PivoARL (item 7) reduces trajectory count + VBQ (item 5) quantizes model — 0.5B process-reward RLVR + VBQ is plausible local executor
